Deferral and Suspension correspondence

Siebel classifications for deferral and suspension correspondence.

Last published 29 April 2026

Favourite Notes Expand all 
Background

This page provides the procedure for classifying inbound deferral and suspension correspondence. 

Recent updates
Procedure Steps

Update the Siebel inbound activity according to your business area.

Business area
	Classifications

Frontline Compliance, excluding
high impact deferrals/suspensions
Debt advice and Legals activities
Frontline Services
	
Capability: Debt and Lodgment
Category: DEBT - GENERAL
Type: Deferral Suspension
Interaction: do not change
Finalisation Code: select the appropriate option
PORO Indicator: as appropriate
Description: where relevant, input a description based on the type of disaster and outcome of the enquiry. e.g. 'Flood - IT Lodgment Deferral Granted'
Case Id/Project Id:
if an active debt case exists in Siebel, copy and paste the Case Id into the Case Id/Project Id field of the Siebel activity
do not copy and paste the Project Id into the Case Id/Project Id field of the Siebel activity
Status: Done.



Frontline Compliance, 

high impact deferrals/suspensions
Debt Advice and Legals activities

	
Capability: do not update
Category: do not update
Type: do not update
Interaction: do not change
Finalisation Code: select the appropriate option
PORO Indicator: as appropriate
Case Id/Project Id:
if an active debt case exists in Siebel, copy and paste the Case Id into the Case Id/Project Id into the Case Id/Project Id field of the Siebel activity
do not copy and paste the Project Id into the Case Id/Project Id field of the Siebel activity
Status: Done.

Deferral and Suspension guide for Sophisticated Taxpayers
Deferral and Suspension guide for Sophisticated Taxpayers

Using data to understand our taxpayers as individuals and entities before we discuss their obligations with them.

Last published 20 November 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides information on using data to understand who our taxpayers are as, individuals and entities, before we discuss their obligations with them.

Recent updates

20/11/2025	Update to 'Check requests for other entities in the economic group' under 'Initial checks'.	SD-98668
08/10/2025	Updated Siebel classifications in the Initial Check section	SD-98617
Procedure Steps
Initial checks
Tax agent requests

If this is a tax agent request through the Lodgment program deferrals,
Opens in a new window
 check if this has been submitted on the correct excel form.

Open the xlsx attachment to establish the type of form. If:

the form title is Payment deferral application, continue to Check if the deferral has been actioned section within this procedure
any other form type, escalate to
Capability: Compliance
Category: Intermediaries
Type: Agent Deferral Complex
Status: UnAllocated
Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q.

Refer to Deferrals forms for registered agents
Opens in a new window
 for different deferral forms.

Information

RADR/VIPER back end processes will identify and allocate sophisticated taxpayers to be action by the relevant teams. Payment deferral processes differ, requests are not actioned via RADR.

Check if the deferral has been actioned

Review activity, lodgment status and decision notes. Go to:

Siebel, open Taxpayer Notes in ECP and check for deferral information, including prior decisions
ICP and review lodgment and/or payment status.

Check for previous deferrals:

go to ICP
Client calendar
Received Lodgments
use Form Type: Defer lodge/pay due date.
If the deferral has...	Then...


been actioned correctly

	

if the taxpayer:

does not require a new deferral, add a note
Opens in a new window
 and set to done
Opens in a new window
requires a new deferral or has provided additional information, continue with the procedure.



not been actioned correctly

	 continue with the procedure.
Public Groups (PG) Top 100 and Private Wealth (PW) Top 500

All requests for deferral of lodgment and/or payment from taxpayers in PG Top 100 or PW Top 500 must first have a Key Client Manager or Client Relationship Manager consulted.

To check if the taxpayer is in PG Top 100 or PW Top 500 and has a Key Client Manager assigned:

open ECP and do a TFN search
check the BMT Structures gadget (may need to be added in the gadget explorer)
note the TFN of the Ultimate Holding Coy
conduct a new ECP search on the ultimate holding company
view the BMT General gadget (may need to be added in the gadget explorer) and check the following fields
Population - PMB for Top 100
Population - POWG for Top 500
if either or both fields are populated, select the link to determine the Key Client Manager or Client Relationship Manager
contact the Key Contact Manager, to determine what actions to undertake.

If no Key Client Manager or Client Relationship Manager data is available, or contact is unsuccessful:

email
LargeServiceTeam@ato.gov.au (for Top 100)
Top500PrivateGroups@ato.gov.au (for Top 500 (excluding Top 100)
include the following
taxpayer's name
TFN
activity ID
information provided by the entity.
Large Service Team taxpayers

Deferral requests from taxpayers who meet all 6 of the below criteria must be escalated to LST.

Taxpayers who are Large Withholders and/or Significant Excise payers:

who are requesting payment deferral of PAYGW, GST and/or Excise and
can demonstrate that they
do not have access to alternate sources of funding
cannot make payment by the original due date without becoming insolvent, because of the impacts on the business due to COVID-19, and will be able to
make the payment by the proposed deferred due date, and
meet their ongoing tax related liabilities when due.
To identify these taxpayers
Entity type	Description


Large Withholders

	

As defined at section 16-95(1) of TAA, a Large Withholder can be either an entity that withholds more than $1m, or a member of a group where the group withheld more than $1m in the previous financial year.

To determine Large Withholder status, check roles in ICP for Large PAYGW cycle.




Large Withholders - Income Tax Consolidated Group

	

If the entity or group is Income Tax Consolidated, you need to determine if there is at least one entity that has a Large PAYGW cycle. Where there is, all requests for these ITC members would be required to be escalated to LST.

Confirm with LST if they will consider the consolidated group's request. Only refer to LST if they meet the eligibility of advised insolvency risk otherwise to discuss alternate administrative support as per EKM.




Significant excise payer

	

Any entity that would typically pay in excess of $1m of excise per week.

To determine Significant excise payer status, check ICP SOA for Excise value.

Consider the request

Consider if the request contains sufficient information and evidence, as required by the Large Service Team.

If there is...	Then...


sufficient information

	

email LargeServiceTeam@ato.gov. Include:

taxpayer's name
TFN
activity ID
information.

Leave your activity in System/Process Pending, update the review date for 2 business days.




insufficient information

	

contact the taxpayer advising to send their request in writing to LargeServiceTeam@ato.gov.au with the required information and evidence.

Add a note
Opens in a new window
 using the relevant Notes fly-out tab and close your activity.

Ineligible for deferrals

Deferral request cannot be offered for the following:

a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF) that has been contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination
awaiting outcome of class ruling, private ruling, guidance request. Taxpayers are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates. They can either lodge their returns
based on the current Australian Taxation Office (ATO) view of the law, or
how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined
in their class ruling or private ruling request)
excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Case Owners and infrequent escalations
Case type	Action


Employer obligation

	
go to Case Summary tab in Siebel Consolidated Taxpayer View (CCV)
locate the Project column
check the Project field for anything with 'EO' in the title.

If there is a case with a Status of 'Allocated' the request should only be considered by the case owner/auditor.

Contact the case owner, advise them of the request and assign to them.




Obligation enforcement

	
go to the Case Summary tab in Siebel Consolidated Taxpayer View (CCV)
locate the case type 'Lodgment case - enforcement' or 'Lodgment case - prosecution'
if there is a case owner, contact the case owner for further instructions.



Debt case owner

	
go to the Case Summary tab in Siebel Consolidated Taxpayer View (CCV)
check for an active case classified as
Type: Receivables, with
the values
Sub-Type: Debt Complex
Product:
Pre-Assessment
Post-Assessment
Case Management
Securities, or
the values
Sub-Type: Debt
Product: Debt Action
view the Primary Owner field to check for an active case owner
if active debt case owner exists, contact the case owner for approval before proceeding.



Litigation, Objections, or other active case owners

	
go to the Case Summary tab in Siebel Consolidated Taxpayer View (CCV)
check for an active case owner
if active case owner exists, contact the case owner to clear the request before proceeding.
Other checks
If you notice any of the below...	Then...

Annual ancillary fund returns
Australian Antarctic Division (AAD) employees
Taxpayer recipient of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy
Petroleum resource rent tax (PRRT)
Single touch payroll
	

go to Deferrals and suspension
Opens in a new window
 procedure.

Check requests for other entities in the economic group

Check which economic group this entity is, as well as the those of the economic groups they are linked to.

Open ECP
Opens in a new window
 and select the FC Advanced Level experience from the experience menu. From the requesting entity, select Group in the top right corner.

Select the relevant group, in the following order:

UHC (Ultimate Holding Company)
GW-T (Group Wealth - Total)
PG (Private Groups).

This is because the legal responsibility for the requesting entity is the UHC.

If the UHC is only the entity itself, consider if it is more appropriate to check the other groups, checking GW-T, then PG. If there are multiple GW-T groups, consider using additional information, such as the percentage ownership from an ISF10MOD group, to determine the appropriate GW-T group.

Step	Action


1

	

Locate linked deferral requests within the main group by selecting the Membership tab.




2

	

Open the Group Siebel WMA and Cases - Active gadget in ECP by:

accessing the Group Data Links - Internal gadget from the Group tab
selecting the link to the Profiling Dashboard
selecting Active Siebel Cases under Tags.

Once opened, if results are returned, complete the following:

select

Filters from the top left
Deferral Suspension in the Type field
Apply
assign the unallocated deferral activities to yourself in Siebel, or if they are assigned to another staff member, contact the officer to determine who will action the activities (all linked group deferral activities identified in ECP must be actioned by the same officer).



3

	

Go back to ECP and:

select

Filters again

Reset
High Impact Deferral in the Type field

Apply
assign these to yourself, otherwise, if they are assigned to another officer, contact the officer to determine who will action the activities (all linked group deferral activities identified in ECP must be actioned by the same officer).
Complete the relevant initial checks on the identified group

Some checks may need to be recompleted if a group other than the UHC is selected:

PG Top 100, and Large Service Team taxpayers
Private Wealth Top 500.
Profiling - Requesting entity

A brief profile of the individual entity through a whole of taxpayer approach should help you understand key details about this particular entity and what role they play in the broader economic group.

Consider the group resources (i.e. access to funds and professional resources to assist with lodgments) in assessing their request for a deferral. Check for other deferral requests and action all requests across the group. This may require you to call different authorised contacts and staff. You will need to be mindful of privacy considerations in actioning requests across the group.

Open ECP
Opens in a new window
 and select FC Advanced Level experience from the experience menu.

Taxpayer tab
BMT General gadget details:
Taxpayer Sub Type
Tax Agent
Broad Industry & ANZSIC
Segment
Primary Taxpayer Experience
Population - POWG
Population - PMB.

Taxable Payments Reporting gadget details

This shows the payments made (Payer) or received (Payee) by the entity and will help you understand the income type and taxpayer base of the entity. It may assist in determining how the taxpayer is impacted, for example the region or industries their taxpayers are based in may be impacted.

GST: Debit/Credit gadget details

Review and click on the current year data to view the monthly breakdowns. This information will give you a basic overview of the sales and expenditure.

PAYG Tax Withheld gadget details

Review and click on the current year data to view the monthly breakdowns. This information will give you a basic overview of the sales and expenditure.

Shareholder Summary gadget details

This will help you understand the ownership structure and to select the correct wealth profile/s from the Group view.

Property Summary gadget details

This gadget will help you understand the Property asset position of the entity and their capacity to seek finance.

STP PAYGW v CAC gadget details

You will be able to identify PAYGW/ITW trends, i.e. increase/decrease, to assist in understanding the cashflow position.

Wealth Summary gadget details

Specifically Return Total Net Assets and AIIR Total Interest Non Rltd Prty Cptl (estimated amount in bank accruing interest). This gadget will help you understand the Net Asset position of the entity and their capacity to seek finance.

Lodgment tab

Briefly review gadgets on this tab, to determine their lodgment compliance history.

Lodgment Overview Summary
BAS and ITR Summary
Failure to Lodge Penalty & Remission History Summary
BAS Lodgment.
Payment tab

Briefly review gadgets on this tab to determine their payment compliance history.

Account Balance
Debt Non-pursuit Amounts
Debt Snapshot.
Information

Completing a review of ECP data for the requesting entity will help you know your taxpayer and understand their compliance history. Review the linked economic group to understand the whole taxpayer.

Profiling - Economic group

Profiling the Economic group is required to gain an understanding of the relationship between the entity and the group, as well an overview of the group itself.

The ATOs definition of an Economic Group is a cluster of administratively and financially connected entities that share a common source of control, known as an ultimate holding entity. While your primary focus is the reporting entity and the request for deferral, the group structure and resources will indicate the breadth of the group, associated entities, and inter-relationships. When looking into the group, it is important to understand that each entity is legally separate.

Entities in an economic group

Entities that are part of an economic group with combined turnover greater than $250 million. This includes:

public, private, and foreign-owned companies
partnerships
trusts
super funds
individuals linked to the above.
Group tab

Open ECP
Opens in a new window
 and select the FC Advanced Level experience from the experience menu. From the requesting entity, select Group in the top right corner.

Select the relevant group, in the following order:

UHC (Ultimate Holding Company)
GW-T (Group Wealth - Total)
PG (Private Groups).

This is because the legal responsibility for the requesting entity is the UHC.

If the UHC is only the entity itself, consider if it is more appropriate to check the other groups, checking GW-T, then PG. If there are multiple GW-T groups, consider using additional information, such as the percentage ownership from an ISF10MOD group, to determine the appropriate GW-T group.

Group Client Details gadget details
The Ultimate Holding Company Name

The full name of the ultimate holding entity linked to the ultimate holding entity TFN. The ultimate holding entity has ultimate control over all other entities within an economic group.

Total Number Entities and Number of IT/GST reporters

These should be checked against each other, to see the number of entities in the group, against the number of unique IT and GST reporters.

This will guide your investigation into their lodgment compliance history. For example:

there are 5 entities in the group, only 1 IT lodger, and 3 GST lodgers

this means that this is an IT consolidated group, likely with UHC as the ITC Head

only the UHC will have IT returns, and their ability to lodge these could be affected by delays in the other entities.

Primary Industry Type

This is important to check, as certain industries are more likely to be involved in sophisticated approaches to managing their tax affairs.

Group Authorised Contacts gadget details
Key Client Manager

Under Key Client Manager, if any status other than No KCM details available select Key Client Manager.

If there is contact information, either in Manager or Team, contact the key contact manager, to determine what actions to undertake. If contact is unsuccessful, email the LargeServiceTeam@ato.gov.au with the:

taxpayer's name
TFN
Activity ID
information provided by the entity.
Group Data Links - Internal gadget details

Click the link for the Profiling Dashboard.

In the top right corner of the Profiling Dashboard navigator bar, select:

Experience
then for
PG, the PG Profiling Dashboard
PW, the PW Profiling Dashboard.

Breakdown of which PG or PW group the entity falls into.

PG
	PW

Top 100
Top 1000
Medium
Emerging
	

Each are also broken into:

Partnering
Encouraging
Influencing
	
Top 320/Top 500
Private Groups.

Check for:

SGE Data Available, check if this value is Y, as it means the group has an annual global income of greater than $1 billion. It would be expected these groups would have the capacity to pay therefore need to be questioned as to the reasons why they are unable to pay/lodge

International Dealings, check if this value is Y, as the group reports foreign income or has evidence of international dealings. This check would lead to reviewing AUSTRAC information
Prosecution, check if this value is Y, as this means that one or more group members has Prosecution linked Siebel Cases. This check would lead to reviewing the Group Siebel WMA and Case gadget for not just other deferral requests, but actively look for these Prosecution case/s, to determine if the deferral on the requesting entity would impact that case
Litigation, check if this value is Y, as this means that one or more group members has Litigation linked Siebel Cases. This check would lead to reviewing the Group Siebel WMA and Case gadget for not just other deferral requests, but actively look for these Prosecution case/s, to determine if the deferral on the requesting entity would impact that case.

Open the PG Assurance Profiling Dashboard.

Review the:

Overdue Lodgment section, as it provides a summary of the group's lodgment status

most recent Group Tax Summary as the current year may not be up to date. This shows the overall financial position of the group on a year-to-year view

net assets in the bottom left show the overall net position of the group.

Open the Private Wealth Assurance Profiling Dashboard.

Review the:

Overdue lodgment section, as it provides a summary of the group's lodgment status

Wealth Profile and Group Assets sections, to determine if there is a specific asset type forming most of the wealth.

Other gadgets in the Group Tab
Group Obligations gadget

Total Group Debt Overdue shows the overall debt position of the group, potentially indicating the other Stronger Action:

may be occurring
may need to occur
or the entity is in credit, and has funds available to pay their lodgment when it falls due.
Group Motor Vehicle Ownership gadget

A list of the reported Motor Vehicles owned by the group, presented as a table view from the Private Wealth Assurance Profiling Dashboard.

Group Financials gadget

Table view of the financial information available in the Private Wealth Assurance Profiling Dashboard.

Group Property gadget

A list of the reported property owned or previously owned, by the group presented as a table view from the Private Wealth Assurance Profiling Dashboard.

Group Austrac gadget

Sum of the group's total outgoing and incoming AUSTRAC information. Select the drill-down to access the specific information.

Information

This information is under the MOU with AUSTRAC, and you must be aware of this before disclosing any information to the taxpayer. This is only to be used as a guide to determine if the entity has significant global funds moving in or out. A red flag would be the irregular movement of funds offshore.

Membership tab
Group Member Obligations

This view shows the table view for each of the entities current overdue balance (including credits as a negative), as well as the number of outstanding lodgments for IT, BAS and FBT. This can be used to determine where any debt, or outstanding lodgments sit, and can assist with determining the position of the requesting entity, in relation to the Group Head and other members of the group.

Group Siebel WMA and Case - Active gadget

Table view of all the active cases and Siebel activities across the group. Review the information as a whole and take note of any activities or cases that may be of concern.

Use the filter to look for deferral activities, prosecution cases, litigation cases, as well as any other types of cases or activities that may be of concern.

Profiling - Internet Search

Complete a brief internet search of the entity and/or economic group to identify and understand additional information about:

The industry they operate in.
Their taxpayer base.
The region/s they operate in.
The service/products they provide.
The entities/groups they are associated with.

This information can generally be located on the business website under 'About Us'.

Consider Google searches for the following:

Search	Information


All

	

General information including links to company websites which may identify current and previous projects/contracts.




Maps

	

Location of the business, branches, etc.




Images

	

Pictures of current and previous projects/contracts.




News

	

Targeted articles in journals and publications which is useful for legal actions undertaken by third parties as the courts must advertise certain legal proceedings.

Having a brief understanding of this will assist you in 'knowing your taxpayer' and understanding what circumstances they may be impacted by. This information will consolidate the ATO's data and give you a better picture of:

the taxpayer's circumstances, and
what assistance would be best suited to their needs.
Considerations
Statutory due dates and timeframes

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date
concessional due date.
Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original or concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Decision

Determine if the deferral request meets the criteria. Consider the relevant PSLAs, your profiling of the taxpayer and their economic group and the reasons provided by the taxpayer in their submission.

Do you need to contact the taxpayer?

You may need to contact the taxpayer for additional information. Use the below table to assist with your contact attempts.

Attempt	Consider


First

	
calling on all numbers
leaving voicemail messages (where appropriate) for the requesting individual

sending an SMS to the requesting individual

via 1-to-1 tool

template 376
fill out the form with the relevant information
in Select the form type, select Other - free text
in Insert other form or request types type deferral request
allowing 5 business day turn around for messages and SMSs.



Second

	
calling on all relevant numbers

sending an email to the requesting individual

via 1-to-1 tool

template 310
fill out the form with the relevant information
in Contact us reason, select Other free text
in Free text contact us reason, type deferral request
allowing 2 business day turn around.



Third/Last

	
calling on all relevant numbers
issuing a contact us letter
Opens in a new window
 via post/portal.
Decision

Your decision should differentiate on what you are recommending and or approving; Lodgment, Payment, or Lodgment and Payment. Ensure the reasons for your decision are appropriately notated and need to include such points as:

lodgment and payment history of the entity and the economic group

specific impacts that have been provided

evidence.

Refer to:

Lodgment deferral PS
Opens in a new window
Payment deferral PS.
Opens in a new window
Information

Payment deferrals are generally a short-term measure to provide immediate assistance in extenuating circumstances. If payment is unlikely to be made in full by the deferred due date, a tailored payment arrangement may be more appropriate.

Taxpayer meets the criteria for a deferral

Seek authorisation (if necessary) and input the deferral in ICP in accordance with the Deferral and suspensions
Opens in a new window
 procedure. Contact the taxpayer as per Notify taxpayer of outcome.

If the deferral is being granted after the lodgment has been received.

Step	Action


1

	

Open ICP and enter the taxpayer TFN.




2

	

Select:

View all Roles in Client role summary
Periods for role type (if deferring a BAS, select Activity Statement Reporting as the role type)
Period start date for the lodgment period you are deferring.



3

	

Select Online forms.




4

	

In Form view select Other forms at Form Group.




5

	

Select Defer lodge/pay due dates at Form Type.




6

	

Select Submit.




7

	

Select:

Manual data entry at Input Reason
Due date to be deferred tab
appropriate options, and
enter details on Defer lodgment due date and Defer payment due date tabs as required.



8

	

Select Form Confirmation tab.




9

	

Select Submit.

Information

If a payment deferral has been granted you will need to adjust the effective dates in addition to the above. Refer to ICP-Adjust effective date.
Opens in a new window

Taxpayer does not meet deferral criteria

Consider if a suspension or payment arrangement is a better outcome.

Step	Action


1

	

Check if the taxpayer can pay in full. If:

yes, contact the taxpayer to discuss immediate payment or other payment arrangement options
no, go to step 2.



2

	

Check if the taxpayer can pay by entering into a payment arrange for up to 3 months. If:

yes, enter taxpayer into payment arrangement for the agreed period without requiring further financial documentation
no, go to step 3.



3

	

Check if the taxpayer needs a long-term payment arrangement. If:

yes, request any additional financials and consider the payment terms as proposed
no, consider other recovery options.
Notify taxpayer of outcome

Once you have made your decision determine how to notify the taxpayer, in addition to calling.

For	Granted	Refused


Lodgment

	

Any method (phone, portal message, in writing)

	

In writing.

Go to Deferral and suspension
Opens in a new window
 procedure to determine correct letter to issue.




Payment

	

In writing

If the taxpayer disagrees with the outcome

If the taxpayer does not agree with our decision, they can ask for an external review.

Time limits apply, taxpayers can refer to seek an external review of our decision
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website for more information.

ICP-Adjust effective date

How to adjust effective dates in ICP.

Last published 20 October 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

The purpose of this procedure is to show how to adjust effective dates in ICP.

Recent updates
20/10/2025	Minor update to 'Background'.	SD-98410
Procedure Steps
Adjusting the effective date
Step	Action
1	Navigate to ICP.
2	

Select Periods next to the relevant role in the Client role summary table.

Where the deferral is for a lodged BAS, navigate to the role which incurred the liability

GST

WET

LCT

FTC

PAYGW

PAYGI

FBTI

Each obligation due in the BAS will need to have the effective date adjusted individually.


3	

Select

Transactions next to the relevant period

Financials next to the relevant transaction

Adjust effective date from the page level navigation bar.


4	Enter the new effective date in the New effective date field.
5	Complete the Input reason.
6	Select Save.

D-1, B-1 - Guide to action a taxpayer's request for a deferral of lodgment and/or payment of their returns.

Last published 24 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1, B-1

This page guides you in actioning a taxpayer's request for a deferral of their obligations for:

lodgment-only
lodgment and payment, or
payment-only.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions of lodgment enforcement action, refer to the Deferral Suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

Exclusion checks

Deferral requests cannot be offered for the following:

where a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF) has been contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination
awaiting outcome of class ruling, private ruling, guidance request
taxpayers are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates
they can either lodge their returns
based on the current Australian Taxation Office (ATO) view of the law, or
how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)
Excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Vulnerability

Where you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. threats of self-harm, family and domestic violence or financial coercion) as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability,
Opens in a new window
 for:

telephony
warm transfer to Specialised Client Support via x92196
this is the end of the procedure
correspondence
escalate to Debt hardship as follows

Category: Debt - Specific
Type: Debt Hardship
Interaction: do not update
Status: UnAllocated
Description: Hardship
Notes: add a note

 including the closed inbound activity ID if appropriate
Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q
this is the end of the procedure.
Escalations

Complete the relevant escalation checks.
Opens in a new window

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment
Opens in a new window

registered agents
Opens in a new window

individuals
Opens in a new window

non-individuals.
Opens in a new window

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.
Opens in a new window

Deferral granted
Deferral type
	Action



Lodgment

	

advise the taxpayer:

failure to lodge penalties will not apply if they lodge by the new due date

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.




Payment

	

advise the taxpayer general interest charges
Opens in a new window
 will not apply if paid by the new due date.




Low-risk

	

add a note
Opens in a new window
 using the Low-risk criteria template in the Notes fly-out tab. 

Suspension granted

Advise the taxpayer:

the ATO agrees not to take further compliance action on the overdue lodgment obligation until the agreed future date 

failure to lodge penalties continue to apply from the original lodgment due date, and general interest charge
Opens in a new window
 will apply on any associated debt

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.

Background

General interest charge (GIC) rates

Find the current general interest charge (GIC) rates and when GIC applies to late payments.

Last updated 6 March 2026

Print or Download

When GIC applies
How GIC is calculated
Quarterly GIC rates
When GIC applies

We may apply general interest charge (GIC) if an amount of tax or some other liability remains unpaid after the date it should have been paid. This includes where:

there is a tax shortfall because of an amendment or correction
an instalment of tax is underestimated
a return is lodged late.

The GIC is updated quarterly with rates for the next quarter generally announced 2 weeks before the start of that quarter.

How GIC is calculated

Section 8AAD of the Taxation Administration Act 1953 specifies how the rate of the charge is calculated. To work out the daily rate, divide this rate by the number of days in a calendar year.

Good decision-making model
What you need to know

    The objective of all decisions about employees in the ATO is to achieve outcomes consistent with governing legislation, human resources. policies, ATO policies, APS values and Employment Principles and ATO cultural traits.
    All decisions should satisfy each of the principles of the good decision-making model.
    All provisions in the ATO Enterprise Agreement are underpinned by the principles set out in clause 7.
    Negative reactions to decisions can be avoided or minimised by putting some thought and care into the way a decision is communicated.
    There are consequences of bad decisions, for the decision-maker, the person who is affected by the decision, and the organisation.
    You are much less likely to have your decisions questioned or contested by ensuring that you include all the elements of a good decision.

Table of contents

    Decision-making in the ATO

    The good decision-making model

    The ReFLECT model

    The consequences of bad decisions

    Elements of a good decision

    Validity of decisions

    Communicating the decision

Decision-making in the ATO

The objective of all decisions about employees in the ATO is to achieve outcomes consistent with governing legislation, human resources policies, ATO policies, APS values and Employment Principles and ATO cultural traits.

The ATO approach to decision-making is to empower decision-makers who have responsibilities for managing people.

When making a decision, decision-makers use their discretionary powers to make a choice among multiple possible courses of action. In the exercise of choice, judgement is required as to which course of action is the most appropriate.

As well as the principles outlined in the rest of this model, there are some specific principles that underpin enterprise agreement provisions. They are:

    providing a safe, secure and fair environment
    assisting employees to balance their work and personal commitments
    the ATO being as flexible as it can, taking into account the employee's preferences and personal circumstances
    fostering strong cooperative relationships between the ATO and its employees
    safeguarding the health and wellbeing of employees
    respecting and valuing diversity
    preventing discrimination and harassment
    treating employees fairly and impartially
    making the most efficient use of resources
    supporting sustainable environmental management.

 
The good decision-making model

ATO principles assist you to use your judgement to reach sound decisions. All decisions should satisfy each of the principles of the good decision-making model:

    Legal

    Ethical

    Equitable

    Overt

    Sensible

    Timely

    Natural justice

    Environmentally sustainable.

These elements may not guarantee that you will make technically correct decisions, but they can help significantly. Where there is no 'right' answer, others are more likely to accept your decision if they can see that it is well reasoned.

Ethical dilemmas are not always easily resolved. They may need to be dissected to sort out the different options. Both this model and the APSC ReFLECT model for decision-making are valuable tools to use when faced with an ethical dilemma - a situation in which there is no clear right or wrong answer.

 
The ReFLECT model

The APSC’s ReFLECT model is the acronym of 6 steps in the decision-making process:

Re= Recognise a potential issue or problem

F= Find relevant information

L= Linger at the ‘Fork in the Road’

E= Evaluate the options

C= Come to a decision

T= Take time to reflect

A conversation with an objective, independent person, such as a trusted colleague, can also be helpful.

The Ethics Centre has also released a video on Ethical decision making - YouTube (3.5 minutes). Video transcript: Ethical decision making.

 
The consequences of bad decisions

Many of us will have personal experience of a decision that we believe has been badly made. Some examples include a job application that was knocked back, or poor grade on a school assignment. The feeling of being unfairly treated is a bad feeling made worse if we are helpless to change the decision.

As well as the personal consequences for those on the receiving end of a bad decision, there are also negative consequences for the organisation.

Some of the possible consequences of a bad decision are:

    feelings of resentment and unfair treatment among those affected, leading to a non-cooperative work environment

    time wasted in dealing with complaints, grievances and appeals

    decisions may not stand up against grievance or appeal processes, forcing decision processes to be done again, or requiring the payment of compensation

    damage to the reputation of the decision maker and the organisation

    loss of faith in the process used leading to the need to set up supplementary processes

    disengagement and efficiency problems in the workplace.

As you can see there are some compelling reasons to ensure that the decisions you make are well founded and can stand up to the scrutiny of others.

 
Elements of a good decision​​​​​​​
Legal

For a decision to be legal, you must have considered and complied with:

    legislation

    regulations

    determinations

    awards

    registered agreements, and

    contractual obligations.

Unfortunately, the legal aspect is often the beginning and end of decision-making. This is possibly because much of the legislation we deal with is complex. It is easy to feel that once we have found the legally correct answer the job is finished.

However, people experienced in the law know that there is no decision so obviously correct that the contrary view cannot be well supported with strong arguments. Many well-established principles of taxation and administrative law were once hotly disputed before the highest tribunals as parties appealed unfavourable decisions.

You must answer three questions to determine if your decision is legal.
1. Does the law authorise the making of this decision?

This means making enquiries to ensure you are fully informed about what the law requires of you in making the decision. This may be a simple exercise or require exhaustive research and you may want to refer the matter to other more expert staff for advice. You will have to judge what research is necessary consistent with the circumstances surrounding the decision. There is usually a point at which you can see that little more is likely to be achieved by further research.
2. Am I the right person to make this decision?

In the ATO environment, power to make decisions flows from legislation that gives these powers to the Commissioner of Taxation. Powers to do with staffing, finances, employment and the ATO's internal processes are usually delegated to the permanent head of a department. These powers can be delegated to you, in which case you are responsible for the decisions you make using the delegated power.

If you are a delegate, you cannot be directed to reach a particular decision, even by your superiors. However, your superiors may reverse your decisions provided they hold an identical delegation. They will then assume all responsibility for the decision.

As an alternative to delegating powers, staff may be formally authorised to decide certain matters. Authorisations substitute you for the person holding the power. They remain responsible for your decision (a further incentive to make good decisions). Because they are responsible for your decisions, they are allowed to direct you to come to a specific decision.

See Delegations to find out if you are the correct person for decisions about employment matters.
3. Have all procedures required by the relevant law been observed?

If a tribunal or review process finds that procedures have not been properly observed they can order the decision making process to be carried out again using the correct procedures.

The consequences of the poor application of procedures may be much worse than simply having to do the work again. The ATO's rights or options may be severely compromised. Further action may be effectively prevented by your original failure to observe correct procedure.

Example

In debt collection work, incorrect service of process when we had the chance may prevent us obtaining judgement. By the time the defect is brought to our attention, we cannot readily obtain good service on the defendant.
Ethical

Ethics are the standards or principles by which each of us personally decides what is right or wrong. Words such as integrity, standards, values, honesty, fairness, courage and so on, also help to describe ethics.

Ethical beliefs will differ from individual to individual, and occasionally personal ethics and behaviour outside the workplace may not align with organisational ethics and behaviour expected of employees. The APS Values, Employment Principles and Code of Conduct, and the ATO cultural traits describe the ethical standards that apply to yourself and those for whom you are responsible.

A 'one law for them and another for us' situation should never occur. The specific details of a particular code of ethics are usually of little consequence as long as they are consistent and predictable in their application. That is, we should practise what we preach.

If you are struggling with a decision or ethical dilemma or want advice about our internal integrity policies, you can seek advice from the ATO Integrity Unit via ATOIntegrityUnit@ato.gov.au.

If you want to seek independent advice outside of the ATO on ethical or integrity matters, you can contact the APSC Ethics Advisory Service on (02) 6202 3737 (Monday-Friday from 9:00 am to 5.00 pm AEST) or ethics@apsc.gov.au.

 
Attention
	These channels are not for lodging complaints.
Equitable

Equity within the ATO means you apply the rules that govern your decisions equally to all people. You must ensure you do not unjustly discriminate against or add bias in favour of any person on grounds of race, colour, sex, religion, political opinion, age, or any other characteristic defined under anti-discrimination legislation.

See What is workplace discrimination? for more information.
Overt

Do not pretend to make a decision for one reason if you are really making them for another reason.

If you believe your real reason justifies your decision state it openly. if not, do not make the decision. It is often tempting to use convenient rationalisations to avoid controversy. However, people have a nose for such situations and will detect or, worse, assume your ulterior motives, which can harm your name or reputation; apart from any formal sanctions that may apply.
Sensible

Common sense is an important aspect we overlook in making a decision. Examples where decisions seem not to be sensible are the different dates for starting and finishing daylight savings in some states, or the different rail gauges used in each state.

It will always be hard to justify a decision if it does not make sense to ordinary people.
Timely

People affected by a decision have a right to know the outcome of a decision as soon as possible, and a right to expect that decisions within an appropriate timeframe.
Natural justice

Natural justice exists when a sound decision has been made and the decision-maker has acted fairly, in good faith, and without bias.
1. The hearing rule

The hearing rule requires that people whose interests may be affected by a decision are given the opportunity to be heard. A reasonable opportunity to present a case must include a reasonable opportunity to prepare a case.
2. The 'no bias' rule

This requires the decision-maker not to have a personal interest in the outcome. The underlying rationale of this rule is that justice must not only be done, but must be seen to be done.
3. The 'no evidence' rule

The 'no evidence' rule requires a decision to be based upon reasonable evidence that is relevant to the matter being determined.
Environmentally sustainable

For a decision to be environmentally sustainable, it needs to integrate both short and long-term considerations to ensure the outcome does not negatively impact the environment.

Some projects, activities and/or products that are part of our core ATO business may impact the environment, for example:

    outbound correspondence

    internal administration processes

    IT equipment

    procurement.

When making a change or improvement to a business process or upon commencement of an ATO project, the decision-maker must:

    assess the environmental impact of the activity/product (if any), for example, paper usage, packaging, energy consumption

    avoid unnecessary additional environmental impacts, and

    consider if the solution is sustainable for the organisation and the environment.

 
Validity of decisions

A number of circumstances can cause a decision to be invalid. A decision may be invalid where the decision-maker:

    is not empowered through an Act or delegation

    fails to conform to a procedure in the relevant legislation

    acts for a wrong purpose

    takes into account irrelevant considerations

    fails to take into account relevant considerations

    acts unreasonably

    acts on the basis of insufficient evidence, or

    acts at the dictation of another person.

Decisions can have a direct and often powerful effect on people. It is important that decision-makers are aware that often people's circumstances and the consequences of decision may differ widely. Decision-makers must ensure those affected by their decisions understand the basis of those decisions.

Equally, people affected by a decision should ensure that they understand the basis of the decision and where decision-making processes were sound, accept that decision.

People who are dissatisfied with decisions have the right to review in accordance with community standards as reflected in ATO review processes.​​​​​​​

 
Communicating the decision

Take the time to communicate your decision appropriately. Negative reactions to decisions can be avoided or minimised by putting some thought and care into the way a decision is communicated.
Mode of delivery

Whenever the decision you make is likely to have a major impact on someone else's life, it is always best to try to talk to them face to face or over the phone before they receive the news in writing.

It is also important that you put your reasons in writing so that each party has a precise record of the decision.
Depth of explanation

Decisions which are likely to have a minimal impact on people will need little explanation to be accepted by others. However, decisions which have a major impact on others may need to have the reasoning clearly explained in some depth before people will accept them.

Where a lot of detail is required choose several forms of communication in order to ensure your reasoning is well understood.

    Initially inform a person of your decision and briefly discuss your reasons over the phone

    Follow up with the full detail of your decision set out in writing

    Lastly, you can arrange a face-to-face meeting with the people affected to clarify any points they do not understand.

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Vulnerability

Where you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. threats of self-harm, family and domestic violence or financial coercion) as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability,

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment

registered agents

individuals

non-individuals.

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.
Opens in a new window

Process
Information

Where there is an active payment arrangement, a lodgment and payment deferral may be appropriate to prevent a possible default occurring. This should only be considered if the taxpayer meets the payment deferral eligibility criteria.

Information

For requests received from a registered agent:

do not process any deferral requests. For appropriate action, refer to Registered agent requests
for suspension requests, continue with this procedure.

Complete required actions below to enter a deferral or suspension, then go to Finalise below.

Select the relevant lodgment type:

	Action



Lodgment

	

advise the taxpayer:

failure to lodge penalties will not apply if they lodge by the new due date

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.




Payment

	

advise the taxpayer general interest charges
Opens in a new window
 will not apply if paid by the new due date.




Low-risk

	

add a note
Opens in a new window
 using the Low-risk criteria template in the Notes fly-out tab. 

Suspension granted

Advise the taxpayer:

the ATO agrees not to take further compliance action on the overdue lodgment obligation until the agreed future date 

failure to lodge penalties continue to apply from the original lodgment due date, and general interest charge
Opens in a new window
 will apply on any associated debt

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.

D-1, B-1 - Guide to action a taxpayer's request for a deferral of lodgment and/or payment of their returns.

Last published 24 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1, B-1

This page guides you in actioning a taxpayer's request for a deferral of their obligations for:

lodgment-only
lodgment and payment, or
payment-only.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions of lodgment enforcement action, refer to the Deferral Suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

low-risk criteria is to be applied to only one request within the last 12 months
Frontline staff can process these requests
go to Exclusion checks below.

refer to Principles for managing requests from Sophisticated Taxpayers
Opens in a new window

go to Exclusion checks below.

Exclusion checks

Deferral requests cannot be offered for the following:

where a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF) has been contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination
awaiting outcome of class ruling, private ruling, guidance request
taxpayers are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates
they can either lodge their returns
based on the current Australian Taxation Office (ATO) view of the law, or
how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)
Excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Vulnerability

Where you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. threats of self-harm, family and domestic violence or financial coercion) as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability,
Opens in a new window
 for:

telephony
warm transfer to Specialised Client Support via x92196
this is the end of the procedure
correspondence
escalate to Debt hardship as follows

Type: Debt Hardship

Status: UnAllocated

 including the closed inbound activity ID if appropriate
Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q
this is the end of the procedure.
Escalations

Complete the relevant escalation checks.
Opens in a new window

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment
Opens in a new window

registered agents
Opens in a new window

individuals
Opens in a new window

non-individuals.
Opens in a new window

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.
Opens in a new window

Start activity - view activity details prior to actioning
Start activity - view activity details prior to actioning

Guideline describing the steps that must be followed prior to commencing action on an activity.

Last published 8 April 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides the steps to review an activity before starting the activity.

Recent updates

29/09/2022

	

Minor update to Task 1, step 8.

	

SD-55535

Procedure Steps

There are three ways you may receive a new activity:

using the pick next function (self allocation)

direct allocation by another tax officer

direct system allocation.

Task 1 - Review activity details
Step	Action
1

not open an ICP or MCID window, go to task 6.

to take ownership

click the MVG button in the Assigned To field

locate yourself using the Find dropdown menu

highlight your record and click the Add button

select the Primary field checkbox against your record

highlight the previous owner and select Remove

select OK, and go to step 9

not to take ownership, go to task 4.

not open an ICP or MCID window, go to task 6.

Section 13 AD (JR) Act requests for Debt related matters

Procedure for when a taxpayer is requesting a statement of reasons for a debt related decision (section 13 ADJR request).

Last published 26 August 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page contains procedure to refer or escalate requests where the client is requesting a statement of reasons for a debt related decision (Section 13 ADJR request).

Section 13 ADJR request

Section 13
Opens in a new window
 entitles a client to apply for and receive a statement of reasons for the decision from the decision maker, when the decision is a reviewable decision under the Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977 (AD(JR) Act) (section 13 statement).

There are statutory timeframes associated with section 13 ADJR requests:

Within 28 days of receiving the request, a statement must be furnished to the person who made the request, if a statement is required.
 If the request was not valid as the person was not entitled to make the request, notice in writing of this view must be provided within 28 days of receiving the request.
If the Commissioner refuses to furnish a statement as the request was not made within 28 days of receipt for a written decision or within a reasonable time for any other decision), notice in writing and reasons for the refusal must be given within 14 days after receiving the request as to why the statement will not be provided.
Within 10 business days of an ADJR application being filed the Commissioner must file the statement of reasons if one has not yet been given to the applicant.

All section 13 statements must be reviewed by Litigation & Legal Services (LLS) prior to forwarding to the client, agent, or a representative.

Recent updates
11/08/2025	Update to task 5.
	SD-96368
Procedure Steps
Before you begin
Warning Alert
Warning
It is important that any request received from a taxpayer or registered agent is actioned promptly to ensure statutory timeframes are met.
Task 1 - Identify case owner
Step	Action
1	

Check for an active case in Siebel.

On the Case summary screen identify an active debt case with below values:

Type: Receivables
Sub-type: Debt
Product: Debt Action
Status:
On-hold
New
Reopened
UnAllocated
Reallocated, or
In Progress

OR

Type: Receivables
Sub-type: Debt Complex
Product:
Pre-Assessment
Post-Assessment
Case Management, or
Securities
Status:
On-hold
New
Reopened
UnAllocated
Reallocated, or
In Progress.

2	

View the Primary owner field for the relevant debt case.

Warning: HRPN and HRPNQ are not case owners.


3	

If there is an active case owner, refer the request to the case owner for action by phone. If contact is unsuccessful:

escalate as follows
Capability: Debt case
Category: Collection negotiation
Type: Refer Case
Description: Section 13 AD(JR) request
Notes: add a note
Opens in a new window
 that includes the caller's
name
phone number
preferred contact time and
the reason for their call
Assigned To: case owner's user ID
Status: In Progress
email the case owner (CC the case owner's manager) advising the Siebel activity ID.
Task 2 - Identify authorised decision maker
Step	Action
1	

Run a null query
Opens in a new window
 in Siebel to locate all activities.


2	

Locate the original request in Siebel and review Notes to identify the authorised decision maker.

This will be the person who had the authorised power to make the decision and may not be the operative who actioned the activity and issued the decision letter/enforcement notice.

Generally, refusal decisions are advised in writing. Search in Siebel for activities with an Interaction of Outbound correspondence and use the Service request link to locate the original request. You may need to review Notes in all activities attached to the service request.

For decisions to issue enforcement notices (for example, garnishee, 353-10), search in Siebel for activities with an Interaction of Outbound correspondence and use the Service request link to locate the activity or decision note.

Seek support if you require assistance identifying the authorised decision maker.

Note: Where the authorised decision maker is no longer in the ATO or Frontline Operations, go to task 5.


3	

If you have received a request for a section 13 statement of reasons, and:

you were not the decision maker, go to task 3
you were the decision maker (the decision was not authorised by anyone else), go to task 4
you are a case owner and the decision was approved by another officer, go to task 5.
Task 3 - Refer request to decision maker
Warning Alert
Warning
These steps must be taken on the day the request is identified as a section 13 ADJR request.

Task 4 - Review decision
Step	Action
1

yes,

assign the activity to your user ID

go to step 2

no,

Opens in a new window
 client’s request

go to step 2.


2	

Review your original decision and ensure all material facts have been considered.

Where the client, agent, or representative has provided additional facts or information with the statement of reasons request, check if this information requires a reconsideration of the original decision.

Refer to the relevant EKM procedure when reviewing your original decision.

Note: Contact the client/agent/representative if clarification of circumstances is required.

Check if you are satisfied with your original decision. If:

yes, go to task 5

no, go to step 3.


3

granted in full or withdraw the original notice, go to step 5

granted in part, refused in part, go to step 6.

Ensure the response is included in your Siebel note.
Opens in a new window

This is the end of the procedure.


6	

Ask the client, agent, or representative to withdraw their section 13 ADJR request they made based on the original decision.

agrees, add a note
Opens in a new window
 to your Siebel activity

does not agree, go to task 5.

Hardship - Disaster
Hardship - Disaster

Information page to assist taxpayers who report they have been impacted by a disaster.

Last published 10 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides information to assist taxpayers who report they have been impacted by a disaster.

We take an empathetic approach when working with these taxpayers. To support them to get them back on track, we offer a range of options including:

more time to pay
remitting penalties or interest charged at a time they were affected
payment plans with in some cases, interest free periods
release from payment in certain circumstances.
Recent updates
10/04/2026	Update to include 2026 WA Tropical Cyclone Narelle.	SD-103889
26/03/2026	Update to include QLD Flood March 2026    	SD-103109
18/03/2026	Update to 'Floods' under 'In Detail'.	SD-103109

the taxpayer using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.
