Natural disaster events

	

Natural disaster events
Opens in a new window
 (Access via Firefox)

In Detail

Natural disasters can include:

bushfires
severe storms
flooding
tsunami
cyclones
drought
earthquake, and
industry-wide disasters such as the equine influenza outbreak.
Taxpayers

Where taxpayers advise their inability to meet a tax obligation is attributed to a natural disaster, the ATO will view their circumstances empathetically and assist those taxpayers who:

are directly affected by a natural disaster, or
cannot meet their obligations on time due to participation in relief or related support activities
are experiencing a personal disaster such as homelessness, family violence, financial coercion or threats of harm.

Where possible, the ATO will follow business as usual processes to assist the taxpayer.

Each individual taxpayer's circumstances will be considered and treated with sensitivity. For more information, see Natural disaster support.
Opens in a new window

If you believe the taxpayer is experiencing vulnerability, see Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability.
Opens in a new window

Tax agents

Tax agents and their taxpayers may also be affected by a natural disaster. The ATO does not want taxpayers affected by natural disasters to be worried about tax affairs during these difficult times. We have a policy of providing support to tax agents and their taxpayers affected by such disasters.

When talking to tax agents, take into account the difficulties they are experiencing:

understand that each tax agent contacting us may have different issues and concerns
advise them we are here to help and work with them, and
at all times demonstrate empathy with their concerns.
ATO Support

Support offered by the ATO may include:

allowing lodgment deferrals of activity statements or income tax returns without penalty
allowing additional time to pay tax debts without incurring general interest charges
making arrangements for payment of tax debts by instalments
removing the requirement for CTP information to justify the payment plan
remitting penalties that were automatically imposed during the disaster
fast-tracking refunds
allowing some farmers to use the simplified method for fuel tax credit calculations, regardless of how much they claim per year
in appropriate circumstances, varying pay as you go instalments may be an option
refunding amounts credited to existing debts where the taxpayer has entered into
a deferral to pay, or
an arrangement to pay their tax debts
conducting field visits to assist with reconstructing tax records and making reasonable estimates.

For more information about ABR Disaster Data provided to Australian Local, State and Federal Government agencies, go to ABR Disaster Data.
Opens in a new window

The following links can be sent via the 1 to 1 tool
Opens in a new window
 - Additional information templates:

Bushfire support
Opens in a new window
Support in difficult times
Opens in a new window
Mental health and wellbeing.
Opens in a new window
Lost records

If you have lost your taxation records, the ATO can:

give you extra time to get financial records
Opens in a new window
 that you need in order to lodge
send correspondence to an alternative address, or
replace specific ATO documents for you.

If you no longer have any or few records and are unable to get copies, we can:

help you or your tax agent with your return
use details included in past year tax returns
make a sound estimate of your assessment.
Field visit

A field visit can be arranged to assist taxpayers, to:

reconstruct their destroyed records
prepare and lodge activity statements or tax returns.

If requested, escalate by Siebel activity for a Record Reconstruction visit as follows:

Capability: Goods and Services Tax
Category: GST
Type: Advisory Visit
Priority: 2 - High
Description: where relevant, input a description based on the type of disaster and outcome of the enquiry. For example, 'Flood-IT Granted' or 'Fire-IT Lodgment Deferral'
Notes: add a note
Opens in a new window
 and include details of the

disaster that has caused the records to be lost or damaged
period/s impacted by the disaster
documents/lodgments impacted by the disaster
Status: UnAllocated
Assigned To: if the taxpayer is

carrying on a business and it is registered for GST

WA & SA: SMB43323
NSW: SMB43403
QLD & NT: SMB43405
VIC & TAS: SMB43353
carrying on a business and it is not registered for GST, assign the activity to SMB32853
not carrying on a business, assign the activity to IND38705.
ATO documents issued free of charge

The following replacement ATO documents are issued free of charge:

Activity statements
Notices of assessment
PAYG instalment letters
Role registration letters
ABN registration certificate, and
Statement of accounts.

Use the relevant procedures below to action the taxpayer's request ensuring self-help options are provided:

Opens in a new window
Reissue or view NoA online.
Opens in a new window
Counselling and mental health support

The Australian Government is offering free counselling and additional mental health support for those affected by the 2019-20 bushfires:

individuals
families, and
emergency services workers.

The Bushfire Recovery Access Program provides access for up to 10 free mental health support sessions. Medicare rebates are available through GPs for these sessions with a psychologist or other mental health professionals.

More information is available at:

Mental health and wellbeing
Opens in a new window
Additional help - Mental health support.
Opens in a new window
Accounting treatments

Depending on the strategy applied, taxpayers identified as impacted may have an active accounting treatment in ICP for Natural Disasters. A taxpayer may have one or more accounting treatments on their account.

If the taxpayer does not have an accounting treatment and depending on the strategy, an accounting treatment may need to be manually applied.

Before doing this:

check ICP for existing accounting treatments,
Opens in a new window
 then
determine required action.
Accounting treatment	Level	Impacts	Application


Natural Disaster - choose applicable:

Fire
Drought
Earthquake
Flood
Cyclone
	

Account

	
Remits all GIC accrued between the effective dates of the treatment.
Prevents the auto re-raise of non-pursued debts in the same account.
	
Tier 1 accounting treatments have been applied as a bulk process and should be applied to all accounts in affected postcodes.
If one is not present, then add accounting treatment.
Opens in a new window

If accounting treatments have been applied, advise the taxpayer:

the impacts listed in the table above, and
these will only occur between the effective dates (start and end dates) of the treatment.
Deferrals

It is important to encourage the taxpayer to lodge all activity statements, tax returns and Superannuation Guarantee statements by the due date, even if they cannot pay.

You should use business as usual (BAU) decision making processes to determine if a deferral is appropriate.

Any ATO liability will continue to be due and payable once the deferred due date has passed. Advise the taxpayer to repay their refund if they wish it to be offset against an ATO liability.

As part of your natural conversation, ensure you discuss the potential impacts deferrals may have on the taxpayer. This is applicable to all taxpayers, not just those listed in the current impacted postcode list areas.

An Emergency Support Infoline can provide additional assistance on 1800 806 218. Direct impacted taxpayers to call this number next time they call.

For additional information, please review the Bushfire Support FAQ.
Opens in a new window

Employers and Superannuation Guarantee Contributions

Employers may not be able to make their quarterly super guarantee contributions payments by the due date, if they are:

located in a disaster affected area, or
directly impacted by a natural disaster.

While taxpayers may be eligible for deferral of other liabilities such as activity statements and tax returns, the law does not allow us to extend the due date to:

pay the super guarantee contributions, or
lodge a Super Guarantee Charge (SGC) statement.

If the employer has been unable to pay super guarantee contributions by the quarterly due date, they will need to:

lodge the SGC statement, and
pay the charge.

If required, we can talk to the employer about entering into a payment plan.

Background

D-1, B-1

This page guides you in actioning a taxpayer's request for a deferral of their obligations for:

lodgment-only
lodgment and payment, or
payment-only.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions of lodgment enforcement action, refer to the Deferral Suspension guideline.

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

Exclusion checks

Deferral requests cannot be offered for the following:

where a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF) has been contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination
awaiting outcome of class ruling, private ruling, guidance request
taxpayers are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates
they can either lodge their returns
based on the current Australian Taxation Office (ATO) view of the law, or
how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)
Excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Vulnerability

Where you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. threats of self-harm, family and domestic violence or financial coercion) as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability,
Opens in a new window
 for:

telephony
warm transfer to Specialised Client Support via x92196
this is the end of the procedure
correspondence
escalate to Debt hardship as follows

Category: Debt - Specific
Type: Debt Hardship

Status: UnAllocated

 including the closed inbound activity ID if appropriate
Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q
this is the end of the procedure.
Escalations

Complete the relevant escalation checks.
Opens in a new window

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment
Opens in a new window

registered agents
Opens in a new window

individuals
Opens in a new window

non-individuals.
Opens in a new window

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.

 will apply on any associated debt

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.

D-1, B-1 - Guide to action a taxpayer's request for a deferral of lodgment and/or payment of their returns.

Background

D-1, B-1

This page guides you in actioning a taxpayer's request for a deferral of their obligations for:

lodgment-only
lodgment and payment, or
payment-only.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions of lodgment enforcement action, refer to the Deferral Suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Opens in a new window

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

Excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

telephony

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment
Opens in a new window

registered agents
Opens in a new window

individuals
Opens in a new window

non-individuals.

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.
Opens in a new window

 will apply on any associated debt

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.

D-1, B-1 - Guide to action a taxpayer's request for a deferral of lodgment and/or payment of their returns.

Background

D-1, B-1

This page guides you in actioning a taxpayer's request for a deferral of their obligations for:

lodgment-only
lodgment and payment, or
payment-only.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions of lodgment enforcement action, refer to the Deferral Suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

Exclusion checks

Deferral requests cannot be offered for the following:

where a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF) has been contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination
awaiting outcome of class ruling, private ruling, guidance request
taxpayers are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates
they can either lodge their returns
based on the current Australian Taxation Office (ATO) view of the law, or
how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)
Excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Vulnerability

Where you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. threats of self-harm, family and domestic violence or financial coercion) as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability,

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.
Opens in a new window

	Action



Lodgment

	

advise the taxpayer:

failure to lodge penalties will not apply if they lodge by the new due date

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.




Payment

	

advise the taxpayer general interest charges
Opens in a new window
 will not apply if paid by the new due date.




Low-risk

	

add a note
Opens in a new window
 using the Low-risk criteria template in the Notes fly-out tab. 

Suspension granted

Advise the taxpayer:

the ATO agrees not to take further compliance action on the overdue lodgment obligation until the agreed future date 

failure to lodge penalties continue to apply from the original lodgment due date, and general interest charge
Opens in a new window
 will apply on any associated debt

potential prosecution action will start without further contact if the document is not lodged by the agreed date.

Deferral Suspension Instruction
Deferral Suspension Instruction

How to action requests for deferral of the due date for lodgment or suspension of lodgment enforcement action.

Last published 10 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page instructs staff actioning non-lodgment work on how to action requests for deferral of the due date for lodgment (deferral) or suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

This procedure is to be used when directed from:

Non-lodgment case process
Opens in a new window
Non-lodgment actions overview
Opens in a new window
Call from an external collection agency.
Opens in a new window

When actioning agent deferral requests received via Online services for agents (OSfA), refer to Lodgment program deferral instruction - OSfA function.
Opens in a new window

For the purpose of this instruction, the term:

agent refers to a registered tax agent or registered BAS agent

Activity Statements (AS) can be read as any combination of the following

quarterly and monthly AS

annual GST returns where taxpayer is eligible and chooses to pay GST annually (GSTA)

annual GST returns where taxpayer is eligible and chooses to pay GST by instalments (GSTR)

pay as you go (PAYG) withholding annual reports

miscellaneous obligations refers to any obligation that cannot have a deferral/suspension keyed in ICP or the Deferral/Suspension Input module (DSIM). For example

quarterly TFN reports

superannuation annual withholding report

PSAR refers to PAYG payment summary annual report

WM refers to Siebel Work Management.

Recent updates
10/12/2025	

Update to the 'suspension' option in the table under 'ICP deferral/suspension'.

	SD-99949
Procedure Steps
Introduction

Deferrals are actioned in ICP for all obligations except for:

PSAR, which are actioned in the DSIM

miscellaneous obligations, for which there is no system functionality to process a deferral.

With the exception of staff actioning Lodgment program deferral WM activities, do not action requests for the deferral of a payment due date.

Requests for payment deferrals should be redirected, if received:

in writing, refer to Referral instruction
Opens in a new window

by phone, by advising the taxpayer to contact 13 11 42.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions, refer to Deferral suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Ensure PORO is conducted for all requests and all actions are recorded in line with the relevant procedures.

Actioning a deferral or suspension request

To action a deferral or suspension request the actions in the following table must be completed where appropriate.

If you are actioning a deferral approval request refer to Deferral approval below.

ICP deferral/suspension

Before keying an allowed deferral in ICP, you need to consider how you will communicate your decision to the taxpayer.

To apply a...	Then...
deferral	

process the deferral
Opens in a new window

return to step 6 of Actioning a deferral or suspension request to communicate your decision to the taxpayer.

When processing the deferral in ICP, ensure the:

new lodgment due date

does not fall on a weekend or public holiday, and

when this applies, is entered as the next working business day.
reason for deferring lodgment due date selected is Tax Agent Initiated. This will prevent ICP from generating an auto-letter.

If a letter is required, this will need to be issued manually.

On the Form Confirmation screen:

check it for accuracy

if ICP identifies any errors the relevant tab will be highlighted with a red stop symbol

you will not be able to process the deferral until the error is corrected.

Select Submit only (not Save and Suspend) to process the deferral:

if you are unable to correct any errors, cease the transaction, and

re-input if required after resolving the issue.


suspension	

process the suspension, refer to Client calendar - suspend lodgment action
Opens in a new window

return to step 6 of Actioning a deferral or suspension request to communicate your decision to the taxpayer.

A suspension is applied at the period level in ICP using an ALR accounting treatment. Refer to the Accounting treatment overview.
Opens in a new window

Use the ICP ALR Macro to key individual or bulk suspensions in ICP. This will ensure the suspension is keyed against the correct reporting role.

A maximum period of two years applies to suspensions input by Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist staff actioning non-lodgment work.

Negotiating Payment Options
Negotiating Payment Options

D-1 - Information on how to manage payment arrangements based on taxpayer circumstances.

Last published 12 May 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1

Taxpayers may experience short term cash flow difficulties that prevent them from paying on time and in full. Payment plans are a tool used to support and assist taxpayers in paying their debt. Refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles.

This page details how to:

gather taxpayer information to check what payment plans (if any) would be suitable
negotiate non-escalated debt cases
arrange payment plans
finalise payment plans based on the taxpayer's needs.

You can use the Payment plan guide
Opens in a new window
 to familiarise yourself with:

the self-help options available to taxpayer and tax agents
the types of payment plans
how taxpayers can manage future lodgment and payment obligations, and
important background information on payment plans and how to negotiate them.
Payment processing times

Payments:

need to reach us on or before its due date

can take up to 4 business days to be received by us and to appear on the taxpayer's ATO account

made on a weekend, Australian national public holiday or after the processing cut off time on business days, will be processed on the next business day

direct debit instalments scheduled during the Christmas closedown period will be delayed until the new year. As a result, two instalments may be debited on the same day in January.

Recent updates
12/05/2026	Added ATO fuel response payment plan section.
	SD-104698
06/05/2026	Update to 'ECA debt officer has requested a payment plan'.	SD-103194
01/05/2026	Update to 'Background' and 'Taxpayer engagement and experience' step 7 related to Phoenix.	SD-104600, 104295

ATO website
Help the caller go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

them using self-service in the future, and
a better experience for them.

Educate taxpayers and tax agents on available self-serve payment options via Online services:

payment services, including setting up a payment plan depending on account type and eligibility criteria
Opens in a new window

Opens in a new window
Businesses - Online services for business
Opens in a new window

Opens in a new window
view PRNs online
taxpayers can view their payment reference number (PRN) online,
Opens in a new window
 or
use the ATO app at any stage during the term for the payment plan
they must use the correct PRN to ensure their payments are allocated to the correct account
set up notifications
where the taxpayer has verbally agreed to receive payment plan information sent via email or SMS
follow the procedure steps on Delivery preference.
Opens in a new window

Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

 if required.

Warning

If the non-individual entity is deregistered, unless the caller is a director wanting to discuss a Director Penalty Notice, do not continue with negotiations. Refer to Deregistered company enquiries.

Section 13, s13 or ADJR requests

Does the correspondence request reference:

Section 13 or s13

Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act (ADJR Act)

Statement of reasons

Request for reasons for decision.

If:

yes,

refer the request to the authorised decision maker or case officer (if case-owned)

go to Section 13 AD (JR) Act request for debt related matters

no, continue with the below checks.

Escalations

If the taxpayer is requesting to enter a payment plan, or request additional time, staff must escalate the activity according to the checks below.

Complete relevant initial escalation checks:

all staff: Payment plan - initial checks
Opens in a new window

Strategic Recovery and Insolvency: Case review.
Opens in a new window

The checks above identify legal debt recovery actions including:

garnishee
director penalty notice
summons
judgment
bankruptcy notice
creditor’s petition, and
s459E.

If legal debt recovery action is in progress, frontline staff in Compliance and Debt Assist must review and consider the escalation steps.

Debts referred to external collection agency

Entering a payment plan into ICP will withdraw the taxpayer from the external collection agency (ECA).

Debts referred to the ECA are not eligible for ATO interim payment plans.

If you receive a call or correspondence from a taxpayer wanting to enter into a payment plan, check if they have been referred to an ECA.
Opens in a new window

If the taxpayer wants to enter a payment plan with the ATO

If the taxpayer wants to discuss payment in full (PIF), continue as per this procedure to input the payment.

If the PIF has been negotiated and keyed into the relevant systems, advise the taxpayer:

they will automatically be withdrawn from the ECA

that retrieval may take up to 14 days to process in our system, and

they may still receive contact or correspondence from the ECA during this time.

If the taxpayer is unable to PIF, payment plans need to be negotiated directly with the ECA. Continue to decide if the taxpayer has extenuating circumstances or meets the withdrawal
Opens in a new window
 criteria.

Extenuating circumstances could include but are not limited to:

hardship

vulnerability characteristics

disputed/objected debt

bankruptcy

legal disputes.

If:

yes,
continue with this procedure to negotiate a payment plan with the taxpayer if appropriate
if you are approving and have entered the arrangement into ICP, advise the taxpayer that
their case will be withdrawn from the ECA, and
can take up to 14 days to be retrieved in our system
if the taxpayer is experiencing serious financial hardship or vulnerability, go to Vulnerable taxpayers and taxpayers experiencing financial difficulties
no,
advise the taxpayer that their debt is being managed by the ECA and until the debt is paid
the account will remain with the ECA, and
the ECA will continue to collect the outstanding debt
offer to transfer the taxpayer to Recoveriescorp
Opens in a new window
 to continue negotiations regarding the debt
if the taxpayer is unable to be transferred at that point in time, provide them with the ECAs contact details
Opens in a new window
do not update systems but record the conversation and outcome in a Siebel note.
Opens in a new window

If you need to seek further advice, contact the NSN via the General Lodge and Pay enquiries support queue.
Opens in a new window

ECA debt officer has requested a payment plan

If you are contacted by an ECA debt officer regarding a payment plan and the reason is to:

enter a direct debit payment plan
advise the debt officer that the ECA Debt Officer can enter these plans into ICP themselves as per their Debt Management Services Guidelines (DMSG)
Opens in a new window
if they require assistance with entering the plan, transfer the call to the Frontline Services Support x92199
enter a payment plan via a different payment method, advise the ECA debt officer the payment plan is to be entered into their own systems
request approval of a payment plan, transfer the call to Frontline Services Support x92199.
Procedure Steps
Profile taxpayer and review debt
Hardship and vulnerability
Taxpayers experiencing financial difficulties or serious hardship

An individual or business can be considered to have financial difficulties where unexpected or extenuating circumstances leave them unable to pay their debt for a short period of time.

An individual is in serious hardship when payment of a primary tax debt will result in them not having the means to pay for basic needs, such as:

food
clothing
medical supplies
accommodation, and
education for themselves, their family or dependants.

If you are speaking to a taxpayer who is experiencing serious financial hardship:

refer to Taxpayer facing financial difficulties or serious hardship
Opens in a new window

gather capacity to pay information

warm transfer the taxpayer to the Hardship team on x92176

Taxpayer experiencing severe and or immediate vulnerability

Taxpayers may be experiencing extenuating circumstances that make it difficult for them to fulfil their tax obligations.

Vulnerability can impact taxpayers in different ways, it can be temporary, long term or a permanent state. It is difficult to define given the often complex and personal circumstances taxpayers might face.

Instead, we seek to understand our taxpayers through the factors or barriers that prevent them from paying, lodging and engaging. These factors may include, but are not limited to:

serious or debilitating physical and mental illness or injury
impacts from a natural disaster
family or domestic abuse (including financial, emotional, or elder abuse)
low levels of support or literacy.

As part of your natural conversations, discuss any potential vulnerabilities and seek to understand how they are affecting the taxpayer ability to engage, lodge and or pay. 

If you are speaking to a taxpayer who is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their ability to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. domestic and family violence, coercion, threats of self-harm):

refer to Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability
Opens in a new window

gather capacity to pay information (where possible)

warm transfer the taxpayer to the Specialised Client Support team on x92196.

Taxpayer and account review

Prior to discussing payment, ensure you have reviewed the taxpayer's profile and accounts. Review the following to inform your discussion with the taxpayers.

Review
	Action


Check all accounts to decide if the debt is due and payable

	

Use the Account overview macro
Opens in a new window
 and ECP
Opens in a new window
 to profile the taxpayer. For example:

view the Account Balance (Debt) gadget under the Payment tab

select the link to the Data Driven Insights for Payment Plans gadget

for details on using the Account overview macro, refer to the User Guide.
Opens in a new window

Ensure you understand the type of debt. If:

part of the debt is not yet due, continue to negotiate payment
credits exist, refer to Payment and credit transfer.
Opens in a new window

Payment plans must not be entered in ICP for Income Contingency Loan accounts (e.g. Higher Education Loan Program (HELP), Student Financial Supplement Scheme (SFSS)). Compulsory repayments are made through the income tax system. Taxpayers can make voluntary payments themselves, refer to Voluntary repayments.
Opens in a new window


Check the taxpayer segment and debt history
	

Select the Payment tab in ECP
Opens in a new window
 and view the updated Debt Snapshot & Taxpayer Segmentation ECP gadget for details of:

the taxpayer’s current debt segment
a history of movements across debt segments to better understand taxpayer behaviours
the current age of the taxpayer’s debt.


Have the taxpayers recently lodged a form?
And are due to receive a refund?
	

If the taxpayer advises:

the lodgment will clear all their debt, and there is a form in suspense review item

update the Siebel debt case Review Date for 30 days

if the refund has not finalised by then, the taxpayer will need to

call back

request for more time

they will receive a refund that will clear only some of the debt, continue the debt negotiations.




The status of any current payment plans

	

If payment plan has arrears or default status, review Defaulted payment plans
Opens in a new window
 and Late payment or catch up payment.
Opens in a new window

Ensure any discussions with the taxpayer considers active payment plans. You may need to cancel and renegotiate any active payment plans.

Note: If the payment method is direct debit via credit/debit card, check IIA tab for note dated in December 2025 with subject ‘Replace credit or debit card campaign’ or ‘Re-authorise credit or debit card campaign’. For Arrears, complete the actions on the IIA note and advise the taxpayer they need to make a catch-up payment for the missed payment obligation.




Overdue lodgments

	
Information

The existence of outstanding lodgments does not impede the approval of a payment plan.

Check for:

outstanding documents
Opens in a new window
demanded IT returns
Opens in a new window
an active lodgment case
Opens in a new window
concessional due date for electronic lodgment.
Opens in a new window

Overdue lodgments can include:

individual tax returns
trust tax returns (you do not have to check beneficiary returns as they are not required to be lodged)
activity statements (including annual GST reports)
PAYG withholding (PAYGW) annual reports or Taxable Payment Annual Report (TPAR)

Overdue TPAR and Payment Summary Annual Reports (PSAR) do not impact the debt balance.

Do not negotiate a date for lodgment if:

lodgment has been demanded, or
there is an active lodgment case.



Siebel notes

	

Review Siebel notes and consider outcome of previous interactions when making your decision. Siebel notes may relate to:

previously refused payment plans
additional time granted and information the taxpayer was required to provide.



Legal action accounts - Frontline Compliance (FC) and Debt Assist staff only

	

If a Legal action account exists, check if the postings should be bulk moved to the Integrated Client Account (ICA). If it is confirmed a bulk move is required, refer to ICP - reverse bulk move.
Opens in a new window

Postings should be moved where:

legal action accounting treatment end date has passed
there is no judgment accounting treatment
all ICA defended matters have been finalised.

To check for an active accounting treatment:

go to ICP
select Accounting treatment from Client menu
review end date of the accounting treatment.

If end date has not passed, or there is no end date:

accounting treatment is still active, and
a payment plan is to be keyed in the Legal Action Account.

Ensure GIC are updated before organising a payment plan.

Negotiation
Payment discussion

Payment in full should always be discussed as an option before agreeing to a payment plan.

To ensure a level playing field, the ATO's expectation and the taxpayer's responsibility is that lodgment and payment obligations are met on or before the due date. Taxpayers who lodge and pay late may incur GIC or penalties. GIC is compensation to the community for late payment.

To improve payment behaviours now and in the future, all payment negotiations should aim to have taxpayers pay in the shortest possible timeframe.

Our expectations are:

lodgment and payment obligations are met on or before the due date
a debt is paid in the shortest possible timeframe. Agreed Payment plans should reflect this
our taxpayers
plan for their future lodgments and payment obligations
are educated that
any late payments will attract GIC
the best way to reduce the GIC payable is to pay as soon as possible.

If the taxpayer specifically asks for an interest free or GIC free payment, review the Eligibility for a GIC free payment plan
Opens in a new window
 and complete the escalation checks.

To be eligible for a GIC free payment plan, the taxpayer must specifically ask for this and meet all eligibility criteria.

A Conversation Support Guide
This link will download a file
 has been developed to support the payment discussion.

Discuss
	Action


Can the taxpayer pay in full today?

yes,
the reason the debt was not paid by the original due date
how they are planning to meet their future lodgment and payment obligations
discuss how the payment will be made referring to Arrange a payment plan
no, check if they can pay in full within 30 days.



If payment in full is not possible, how much can the taxpayer pay today?

	

Establish the following:

how much the taxpayer can pay upfront within 7-14 days, to reduce the debt

the reason the debt was not paid by the original due date, and what plan did they have to pay on or before the due date

consider the outstanding debt amounts and tailor your conversation to suit

if the taxpayer has SG, PAYGW or GST debt

discuss with the taxpayer why the amounts were not withheld or remitted

refer to the Payment Plan Guide

 - Payment discussion for more details.

making an upfront payment will reduce any GIC payable

When using the Payment and GIC tool
This link will download a file
 consider the limitations of the tool:

Advise the taxpayer that the GIC amounts are estimates only and GIC rates change quarterly.
The tool cannot calculate SG interest and penalties.

The actual amount of GIC they pay could be more than estimated.

All debts (including debts not yet due but visible on the account) must be negotiated into payment plans.

It will cause any active plans to default if a debt within an account is not included in a payment plan.

Pre-serviceability checks

Decide if the taxpayers meet all the following criteria:

all lodgments are up to date

no active

legal action accounting treatments

judgment accounting treatments

Legal Action accounts

for

Straight Forward taxpayers, debts must be less than $200,000 for each applicable account

Sophisticated taxpayers, debts must be less than $5,000,000 totalled across all accounts

first payment is scheduled within

7 calendar days of the interaction (date of actioning request), or

14 days if direct debit

the upfront payment is greater than or equal to the lower offer in the Data Driven Insights for Payment Plans gadget

the payment plan duration does not exceed the lower offer in the Data Driven Insights for Payment Plans gadget

the likelihood of a payment plan being paid within 24 months (as per Data Driven Insights for Payment Plans gadget) is 50% or greater.

Serviceability check point

Setting up payment plans that are likely to default does not help the taxpayer or the ATO. In this decision-making process, you must consider:

taxpayer circumstances
factors to be taken into account in paragraph 61 of PS LA 2011/14.
Opens in a new window

Ensure you have discussed the following with the taxpayers to understand their ability to maintain a payment plan.

Income
Understand the taxpayer's employment or business situation. Check if they are
paid on a regular basis or self-employed, and
if tax is withheld.
Confirm with the taxpayer that
they have the income available, and
have factored in ongoing expenses to support the payment proposal.
Future obligations
Educate the taxpayers on the requirement to meet future lodgment and payment obligations when they are due.
Confirm they have factored this into their payment proposal. If not, discuss varying the proposal to ensure future tax is paid on time.
Taxpayers can make voluntary payment and pre-payments. Refer to the Payment plan guide
Opens in a new window
 to assist the taxpayer with steps they can take to manage future payment obligations.
Direct debit

Direct debit is the preferred payment method for payment plans. Confirm with the taxpayer if direct debit is an option, highlight:

direct debit allows you to 'set and forget'
scheduling direct debits to align with a regular salary or payment will ensure payments are made on time.

If the taxpayer agrees to direct debit, confirm the payment details for the direct debits.

If there is an upfront payment or a final balloon payment, this payment should not be via direct debit unless the taxpayer specifically agrees to it.

Is proposal appropriate

When considering the appropriateness of a proposed payment plan you could consider:

does the proposed payment plan meet all pre-serviceability checks criteria
how will the taxpayer afford the payment plan
has the taxpayer considered
their ongoing payment and lodgment obligations
any other ongoing household or business expenses
is the taxpayer
receiving regular income
aware of GIC accrues on the account? Have they considered other financing options?
repaying the debt in the shortest possible time? Is the taxpayer receiving regular income?
Outcome

Check if the taxpayer's proposal is appropriate. If:

yes,
advise the taxpayer
if the payment plan defaults or they cancel the arrangement
they may
face extra scrutiny and need to provide more detailed financial information
not be granted future payment plans
go to Arrange a payment plan
no,
go to Collect financial information to establish the taxpayer's financial position
consider if the taxpayer requires additional support. Refer to
Taxpayer facing financial difficulties or serious hardship
Opens in a new window

Collect financial information

Businesses and individuals can face cash flow issues. To decide if a payment proposal is sustainable and gets the taxpayer back on track, we request financial information. We work with taxpayers to put in place a plan which is manageable based on their circumstances, and is acceptable to the ATO. Your aim is to help the taxpayer pay in the shortest possible time and save them GIC.

A Conversation Support Guide
This link will download a file
 has been developed to support the collect financial information discussion.

Warning

Staff should not be providing financial advice on how funds are used or applied.

Tax professionals or financial counsellor/advisor

If you are speaking with a tax professional or financial counsellor/advisor who has already assessed their taxpayer's financial information, and they have considered all the information we require below, collecting detailed financial information may not be required:

what information they have considered when establishing a payment proposal
have they completed a budget or prepared a financial report
have they looked at the taxpayer's total income and expenses
will this proposal have the taxpayer repay the debt in the shortest possible time
will the taxpayer have some funds left over for any emergencies
is the taxpayer or agent aware that
GIC is accruing on the debt
the longer the payment plan the more GIC the taxpayer will need to pay.

 to include the details collected and what information you used to make your decision. Refer to the guidance in the Outcome section below.

you must collect further financial information to support your decision

Individual Taxpayers
	Taxpayers in Business	Further Information


Bank details including:

current account balance
any overdraft facility.
	

Bank details including:

current account balance

any overdraft facility or line of credit.

	

Knowing a taxpayer's banking information allows us to understand if there are funds available to make a lump sum payment or pay in full.

Consider if the amount available will:

substantially reduce their current debt
still allow future tax to be paid on time and in full.



Total income.

	

Total gross profit or gross income.

	

Knowing the total income and expenses allows us to understand:

the taxpayer's capacity to pay the proposed amounts by working out how much money is left once all expenses are paid
how the taxpayer is proposing to pay the debt.

Total income should include all income the taxpayer receives for:

individual taxpayers, ensure the taxpayers include income from
personal salaries
investments
welfare payments
trust distributions
other shared household income (if relevant)
taxpayers in business, taxpayers can refer to their accounting software or profit and loss statement for this information. Consider the nature of the business, such as whether it is seasonal. This is to determine if income and expenses fluctuate throughout the year.



Total expenses.

	

Total operating expenses.

	

Knowing the total expenses helps us to establish if the taxpayer has surplus funds available to pay the proposed payment plan.

Total expenses should include all ongoing expenses or business operating expenses for:

individual taxpayers, ensure the taxpayer factors in all expenses such as accommodation, utilities, transport, entertainment, food, education etc
taxpayers in business, ensure all operating costs have been considered, this should include costs such as salaries, insurances, rents, advertising etc.


Major assets and the ability to borrow or sell.
Current loans or other debts.
	
Major assets the business holds.
Are debtors and creditors paid on time?
	

Knowing what assets are held by the taxpayer can inform you if the taxpayer can sell or borrow against an asset to pay the debt. Business taxpayers may have debtors which owe them money or creditors which they need to pay back. For:

individual taxpayers
assets can include, property, cars, shares
ensure that any mortgage or loans against the assets are included and payment of those debts has been considered in their expenses
discuss if they can borrow against any assets, redraw from their home loan account or access a term deposit
taxpayers in business
assets which are 'non-operating' may be saleable, these can include unused land or equipment, vacant buildings
some assets held by a business may not reasonably be sold and allow the business to continue operating
debtors, this
is money owed to the business
could be unpaid or outstanding payments owed to the business for services they have provided or outstanding invoices
creditors, this
is lending the business has received
can be in the form of a bank loan or a director's loan.

Opens in a new window
 and the Business capacity to pay macro
Opens in a new window
 are not mandatory and provide more detail than needed for most taxpayer interactions. The macros can be used if needed when:

the taxpayer needs assistance establishing their total income and expenses
staff actioning firmer and stronger action believe that more detailed financial information is required.
Outcome

Generally, taxpayers offering approximately 70% or more of their surplus income would be looked upon favourably. However, all payment proposals should be considered on a case-by-case basis as taxpayers have differing circumstances.

If the taxpayer's financial information demonstrates they can:

pay significantly more

educate the taxpayer on the expectation of payment in the shortest possible time

advise the taxpayer that GIC is accruing on the debt daily and the longer they take to pay the more they will need to pay. Refer to the Payment and GIC Tool
This link will download a file
 to assist in your conversation

attempt to renegotiate a shorter payment plan based on the taxpayer's financial information. If the taxpayer does not want to renegotiate consider if the arrangement should be accepted or refused
service the payment arrangement and the taxpayer is proposing to pay a reasonable amount of their surplus income, go to Arrange a payment plan below.

When the taxpayer's capacity to pay shows they are not able to service the arrangement, discuss options to tailor the arrangement or consider refusal. Also consider the options available for support, such as:

Taxpayers facing financial difficulties or serious hardship,
Opens in a new window
 and
Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability.
Opens in a new window

Arrange a payment plan

Ensure you have completed the steps under Payment discussion in Negotiations to establish:

if the taxpayer can pay in full

why the taxpayer did not pay by the original due date

what plans are in place to ensure future obligations will be paid on time and in full.

Taxpayers may experience short term cash flow difficulties that prevent them from paying on time and in full. Payment plans are a tool used to support and assist taxpayers in paying their debt. Refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles.

Payment plans are part of a whole of taxpayer approach. Where multiple payment plans are entered at the same time (to address multiple debts) and one will finish earlier than the other, consider varying the remaining instalments. Instalments should be varied to the capacity the taxpayer has proposed.

If the taxpayer requires a number to call back on, refer to the table below to provide the correct contact number.

Campaign
	Phone number

does not exist: 1300 466 859.
Payment plan not granted
Information

If an activity is received and approval has been refused, and the taxpayer is not on the phone:

contact the taxpayer,
Opens in a new window
 then
go to Discuss with the taxpayer.

Discuss with the taxpayer:

the legal warnings
Opens in a new window
 for overdue amounts, and

their review rights
Opens in a new window
 are available on the ATO website.

If appropriate, provide lodgment warnings. This should be based on your discussion and what you believe may influence the taxpayer's behaviour. Tailor the warnings to their circumstances, considering:

their compliance history

previous lodgment agreements, and

your conversation.

Sophisticated taxpayers refused with pre-serviceability can be informed:

their offer is not appropriate, and
to review their financial circumstances.

Where Sophisticated taxpayers can provide a more suitable offer, they can contact us again.

Do not cancel pending SGE payments or SGE payment plans where the taxpayer is eligible for SG Amnesty.

Activity statements - correcting mistakes and RBAS
Activity statements - correcting mistakes and RBAS

D-1, I-4, B-1, B-2 - Procedure on how to correct mistakes or errors and RBASs.

Last published 27 August 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1, I-4, B-1, B-2

This page contains information about correcting mistakes on activity statements and revision BAS (RBAS).

Recent updates
27/11/2024	

Added 'Xero taxpayers - issue affecting some BAS' content.

	

 SD-88327

Self Help
ATO website
Success
alert:
Help the caller go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

the taxpayer using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.

Taxpayers can obtain more information about how to correct an activity statement
Opens in a new window
 from the ATO website.

Information
alert:
Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

Scenarios
Effective questions

Has the activity statement been lodged?

Which period was the activity statement for?

How did you lodge?

What kind of mistake is on the activity statement?

Could you have lodged using an incorrect ABN or TFN?

Do you want to cancel an RBAS?

GST Limited Registration Entity

Go to Simplified GST lodgment, registration and payment enquiries.
Opens in a new window
 GST LRE is a separate process from GST activity statements.

Incorrect ABN or TFN
If...	Then...


the request is due to:

incorrectly registered entity

change of entity

compromised TFN cases or

ABN swaps/moves

	

escalate your activity and the request as follows:

Capability: Operations

Category: REGISTRATIONS

Type: Duplicate TFN

Template: Add correct escalation template to new Activity/Service request in Siebel (except for Change of Detail General type)

Description: <fill in appropriate details>

Status: UnAllocated

Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q.

This is the end of the procedure.


the request is related to GST grouping	

escalate your activity and the request as follows:

Capability: Operations

Category: REGISTRATIONS

Type: LMG grouping

Complexity: Low

Priority: Medium

Status: UnAllocated
Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q.

This is the end of the procedure.


taxpayer has 4 or less Activity statements lodged incorrectly	

the taxpayer will need to revise the affected Activity statements.

If payments or credits need to be transferred, refer to Payments, refunds and transfers.
Opens in a new window

This is the end of the procedure.




taxpayer has 5 or more Activity statements lodged incorrectly, or none of the above apply

	
Information alert:
The ATO can only correct or review Activity statements within the 4 year period of review.
Opens in a new window

escalate to Processing as follows: 

Capability: Operations
Category: Processing
Type: AS transfers
Description: Insert the relevant escalation topic
Attachments view: Complete the AS Transfer Request Form
This link will download a file
 and attach
Opens in a new window
 to the activity
Status: UnAllocated
Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q.

This is the end of the procedure.

In Detail
Xero taxpayers - issue affecting some BAS

An issue has been identified by Xero affecting some business taxpayers using their software. The issue is reported to affect over 200,000 invoices across the 2021-2024 financial years.

The impacted taxpayers:

will be notified by Xero

may contact the ATO about

incorrect calculation of GST on some invoices

revising their affected activity statements.

The taxpayer will need to revise their BAS. Assist the taxpayer using the relevant information below.

Time limits

Time limits apply to amending an activity statement. See Activity Statements - time limits.
Opens in a new window

GST errors

A GST error is a mistake the taxpayer made in working out the GST net amount on the BAS (that was not correct at the time) that resulted in them:

reporting or paying too much GST (credit error)
reporting or paying too little GST (debit error).

Not claiming a GST credit in an earlier BAS is not a GST error.

Taxpayers who meet the conditions to correct the error on a later BAS do not need to revise the earlier BAS in which the error occurred.

If... 

	

Then...




revising an earlier activity statement

	

taxpayers can only revise the earlier BAS if the period of review has not ended, refer to Activity Statements - time limits.
Opens in a new window

If the revision request is to increase the claim for GST or fuel tax credits, it must be processed and finalised by the ATO within the 4-year credit time limit.

If processed and finalised outside the 4-year credit time limit, the entitlement to a GST or fuel tax credit has ended. This applies even if the request was made within the 4-year credit time limit or within the period of review.

Additional or unclaimed GST or fuel tax credits do not have to be claimed by revising the earlier BAS. Taxpayers can claim the credits in the next BAS provided it is lodged (processed and finalised) before the 4-year credit time limit ends.  




correcting errors on a later activity statement

	

check if the correction results in a debit or credit error of the GST:

Activity Statements - Debit errors
Opens in a new window

Activity Statements - Credit errors.
Opens in a new window

If the GST error does not meet these conditions, the taxpayer needs to revise the earlier BAS in which the error occurred. 




GST instalments

	

taxpayers need to lodge an RBAS by the activity statement due date. We will accept variations after the due date if:

the taxpayer has not lodged the annual GST return and

the RBAS is finalised before the generation of the next quarter activity statement.

Taxpayers cannot vary GST instalments after the generation of the next activity statement. Overpaid GST instalment amounts must be reconciled on the GST return for that year.




GST information report

	

RBAS cannot be generated for a GST information report. If the taxpayer wants to revise the report escalate to Activity statement correspondence.
Opens in a new window




Deferred GST

	

if the taxpayer disagrees with the deferred GST amount, see Information on a BAS for deferred GST amount.
Opens in a new window

PAYG withholding errors
If the error relates to...	And...	Then...


non-large withholders

	

W1 or W2 labels need to be corrected on a finalised activity statement

	

RBAS should be issued.




large withholders

	

W2-W4 labels need to be corrected and the taxpayer has an STP role

	

escalate to Operations - Large business.
Opens in a new window




pre-filled W1 and W2

	

pre-filled amounts do not match the employer's records

	

employer can adjust the pre-filled amounts to match their records. See STP and activity statements - ATO PAYGW pre-fill.
Opens in a new window

If the activity statement is finalised by the ATO, as all amounts were known, adjustments can be made by revising the activity statement.




PAYGW annual report

	

mistakes need to be corrected on the PAYGW annual report

	

go to Payment summary amendments.
Opens in a new window




W3

	

a taxpayer has incorrectly reported amounts at W3 - Other amounts withheld (excluding any amount shown at W2 or W4)

	

the taxpayer will need to:

confirm W3 amounts are incorrect

revise the activity statement.

Once revised, the taxpayer will no longer need to include the incorrect amounts in relevant PAYG withholding annual reports.




W4

	

a taxpayer has incorrectly reported amounts at W4 - Amounts withheld where no ABN is quoted

	

the taxpayer will need to:

confirm W4 amounts are incorrect

revise the activity statement

ensure no payment summaries have issued for incorrectly reported amounts.

Once revised the taxpayer will no longer need to include the incorrect amounts in a PAYG Withholding where ABN not quoted - annual report (NAT 3448).

PAYG instalment errors

Taxpayers must correct PAYGI label errors on an RBAS by the activity statement due date. If the due date has passed, we can accept a variation to the activity statement if:

it was made before the generation of the next quarterly activity statement and

the tax return has not been lodged.

Annual instalment payers can lodge a late variation up to 30 November if they have not lodged the tax return.

Taxpayers exited from PAYGI can vary the most recent finalised activity statement. A revision may be accepted after the due date if:

the revision relates to a period during the year the taxpayer exited

the tax return has not been lodged and

they are not a member of a consolidated group.

FTC, LCT, WET and FBT errors
If the error relates to...	Then to correct the error...


FTC

	

taxpayers will need to change their FTC entitlements. See:

GST registered taxpayers
Opens in a new window

Non-GST registered taxpayers.
Opens in a new window




LCT

	

taxpayers will need to lodge an RBAS if they made a mistake on the LCT label. An adjustment to LCT can be made on the next activity statement. See Luxury car tax (LCT) adjustments.
Opens in a new window




WET

	

taxpayers can correct a credit or debit error on their current activity statement, if they meet the conditions specified in:

WET - Debt errors
Opens in a new window
WET - Credit errors.
Opens in a new window

If they do not meet these conditions, the taxpayer will need to lodge an RBAS. See Correcting WET errors.
Opens in a new window
  




FBT

	

taxpayers will need to lodge an RBAS by the activity statement due date. If the due date has passed, the taxpayer can make a variation on the next activity statement. See How to vary FBT instalments.
Opens in a new window
 

ATO errors and exceptions

Transfer the following errors to Activity Statement Product Exceptions:
Opens in a new window
  

scanning errors

keying errors by staff

auto finalised activity statement with a GST instalment and a cancelled GST role.

Paper activity statement errors

Taxpayers can fix errors on paper activity statements not yet lodged using tape whiteout. If the pre-printed details at the top of the activity statement are incorrect, it can still be lodged. The document identification number will be used to match it to the account.

A paper activity statement can be revised by lodging an RBAS. 

RBAS

There is no due date to lodge an RBAS. The RBAS will have a new document identification number. Only the labels which have changed need to be revised. If changing one label leads to a change on another label, this label must also be changed.

Taxpayers can revise an activity statement through SBR/PLS, Online Services for Business, ATO online services for individuals or via paper. See:

SBR and PLS
Opens in a new window
 

Online Services for Business - activity statements
Opens in a new window

Lodge, view and pay activity statements
Opens in a new window
 on ATO online services.

Issue/terminate RBAS

Issue a revision activity statement - RBAS
Opens in a new window

Terminating activity statements.
Opens in a new window

Payment processing times

Payments:

need to reach us on or before its due date
can take up to 4 business days to be received by us and to appear on the taxpayers ATO account
made on a weekend, Australian national public holiday or after the processing cut off time on business days, will be processed on the next business day.
Outcomes
Refunds and payments of revised amounts

If the RBAS results in:

an amount to pay, the due date for payment is the same date as the original activity statement. Payment should be made as soon as possible to reduce the general interest charge. This will be applied from the original due date

a reduced debit, the difference between the new amount and the amount already paid will be refunded. The service standard for refunds is 14 days.
