Add or remove tax agent to taxpayer link
Add or remove tax agent to taxpayer link

How to add or remove a tax agent to taxpayer link in ICP.

Last published 30 March 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides steps to add or remove a taxpayer's registered agent link in ATO systems.

Recent updates
30/03/2026	Updates to Scenarios.	SD-102923
Self Help
ATO website
Help the caller to go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

them using self-service in the future, and
a better experience for them.

Callers can obtain more information from the ATO website on:

Update your personal contact details
Opens in a new window
 - individuals and sole traders
Profile in Online services for business
Opens in a new window
 - companies, partnerships and trusts.

Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

Scenarios
Agent nomination

The following taxpayers must nominate an agent
Opens in a new window
 in Online services for business (OSB) before their agent can access their accounts and act on their behalf:

all entities with an ABN (excluding sole traders)
some business entities without an ABN. These entities can be identified by an agent nomination note in the Internal Initiated Action (IIA) tab on the taxpayer's Siebel record.

These taxpayers need to nominate their agent when:

engaging a new registered agent (that is, a tax agent, BAS agent or payroll service provider for Single touch payroll)
providing additional authorisation to an existing registered agent.

Taxpayers will see the agent nomination in the Agent details page in Online services for business.
Opens in a new window

Once nominated, an authorised agent can establish a new link to the taxpayer accounts in Online services for agents (OSfA) or practice management software.

If the taxpayer is...
	Then...



required to nominate an agent

	

refer to Agent nomination (Taxpayer-to-agent linking).
Opens in a new window

If you are unable to access the SPA Hub Tool, the agent link can be manually added, go to task 1.




not required to nominate an agent

	

advise the caller to contact their agent who can add the link on their behalf.

If the caller insists the agent link to be manually added, go to task 1.




requesting the agent link to be removed

	

go to task 1.



Agent cannot add the taxpayer

If the agent is unable to add a taxpayer using online services, complete the checks in the table below.

Check if	Action


Deceased indicator is present on the taxpayer's record.

	

Agents are not authorised to access a deceased taxpayer's information in OSfA unless the legal personal representative (LPR) has appointed the agent to help manage the estate's tax affairs. When appointed by an LPR, an agent can:

add the deceased taxpayer to their taxpayer list
access their records using OSfA.

Check if the agent has:

provided a declaration they are the legal personal representative (LPR), or
been appointed by an LPR who has probate or letter of administration.

If:

yes, the agent is authorised to access the deceased taxpayer's information
no, advise the agent they cannot link to the taxpayer at this time.

More information on appointing an LPR is available on Deceased estates.
Opens in a new window




Active lockdown suppression is present on the taxpayer's record.

	

Go to Taxpayer register - lockdown suppressions.
Opens in a new window

The agent will not be able to link or access the taxpayer's record in OSFA if an active lockdown suppression exists. If it is appropriate, you can link the agent to the taxpayer's record before the lockdown suppression is released.




Taxpayer is an Income Tax Consolidated (ITC) Group Member.

	

IT return lodgment for ITC Group members is accounted for by the ITC Group Head. The IT role for group members is a non-reporting role.

Check if the IT role is non-reporting
If yes,
modify the non-reporting role
Opens in a new window
 to No
add the registered agent link in accordance with the procedure steps below
modify the non-reporting role
Opens in a new window
 to Yes.
Add a note
Opens in a new window
 detailing the name of the person requesting the update and actions taken before finalising the activity.

Note: To check if an IT role is reporting or non-reporting, in ICP select:

Roles from Client menu
IT role type from the Role type column
If Role is non-reporting field is
No, the IT role is reporting
Yes, the IT role is non-reporting.



Taxpayer is a former temporary resident intending to lodge a DASP claim via an agent.

	

Determine if the agent has written authority from the taxpayer authorising them to submit a DASP claim on their behalf. Verbal confirmation can be accepted. If:

yes, the agent is authorised to access the taxpayer's information
no, advise the agent to call back once they have written authority.

Agent wants to link to a taxpayer’s Global and Domestic Minimum Tax (GDMT) account,
Opens in a new window
 and the account does not exist on ICP.	

Escalate by creating a service request (SR) activity with the classification:

Capability: Operations
Category: REGISTRATION
Type: Pillar Two Acct Role Creation
Status: UnAllocated
Assigned To: Enterprise Q.

Include the following information in the request:

Account type
Role type
Role start date
Financial year end
Effective start date.
Procedure Steps
Task 1 - Identify intermediary account and registered agent's ABN
Step
	Action


1

	

Establish PORO
Opens in a new window
 (if required).




2

	

From Siebel, open ICP.




3

	

Identify the intermediary account to be linked:

select

Taxpayer search from left-hand side menu
Individual or Non-Individual
for Taxpayer Id type select TAN
type the TAN in the Taxpayer Id field
select

Search
Taxpayer Id to go to the Client home page
from the

Taxpayer Id column, note the
intermediary account number against the TAN
ABN for the registered agent entity. This may be needed to add a registered agent link.

4	

Check the next action. If a registered agent link is to be:

added, go to task 2
removed, go to task 3.
Task 2 - Add registered agent link
Warning

Complete this update against the TFN or ABN of the:

registered agent, or
payroll service provider.
Step	Action



1

	

Select:

Client home
Taxpayer links
Add taxpayer link.
Information

Remove any existing agent before adding a new one. The new agent link must commence at least one day after the prior link ends.




2

	

Check if the agent is providing payroll services for Single Touch Payroll purposes only. If:

yes, go to step 3
no, go to step 4.



3

	

Complete the required fields to add a payroll service to provider link.

Link details:

Link type: Payroll Provider to Taxpayer
Apply to: Role
Account: Intermediaries account
select Intermediary-Tax agent from the drop-down menu
Start date: Enter the effective start date
End date: Leave blank unless an effective end date has been supplied.

Linked entity:

Id type: Select ABN from the drop-down menu
Taxpayer Id: Enter the ABN of the entity to be linked
select Next.

Linked entity details:

Apply to: Role
Account: Select the appropriate ICA
Role: PAYGW
Reason: Tax agent initiated
select Save.

This is the end of the procedure.




4

	

Complete the required fields to add a registered agent to taxpayer link.

Link details:

Link type: Tax agent to Taxpayer
Apply to: Role
Account: select the identified intermediary account
Role: Intermediary - Tax Agent or Intermediary - BAS Agent
Start date: enter the effective start date
End date: leave blank unless an effective end date has been supplied
Link is offsettable: must be set to No
Link is acknowledged: must be set to No.

Linked entity:

Id type: select the identifier of the entity to be linked (e.g. TFN or ABN from the drop-down menu)
Taxpayer Id: enter the corresponding identifier of the entity to be linked
select Next.

Linked entity details:

Apply to: select the appropriate level
Taxpayer, where the agent is authorised for all taxpayer interactions with the ATO
Account, where the agent is authorised for selected interactions with the ATO (e.g. IT or ICA only)
Account: select the appropriate account (if Account was selected in the Apply to field)
Input reason: select the appropriate reason from the drop-down menu
select Next.

Link additional details screen:

Authorised representative delivery preference: leave as default
select Save.



5

	

Determine if the form successfully processed. If:

yes,
add a note
Opens in a new window
 on the Siebel activity including the full name of the person requesting the update
this is the end of the procedure
no, was the error 'Period cannot be forecasted'? If
yes, refer to ESCP 989 - Cannot update taxpayer links/unable to process ITR
Opens in a new window
 (BIM 27281)
no, refer to support officer for assistance.
Task 3 - Remove registered agent link

Follow the steps below to remove a registered agent from a taxpayer's account.

Advise the caller they should inform the registered agent they are no longer authorised to represent them.

Note: Where an agent is no longer acting for a taxpayer:

if present on the taxpayer's record, the registered agent's financial institution account (FIA) details should be end-dated as per the Update financial institution account (FIA) details
Opens in a new window
 procedure
the taxpayer's address details should be
updated to their last known address for any accounts showing the registered agent's address
modified
Opens in a new window
 where a valid address cannot be obtained
check the taxpayer’s other contact details and update if related to the former agent. This includes telephone numbers, email addresses, authorised contacts and outbound preferences. 
Step
	Action



1

	

From Siebel, open ICP.




2

	

Select:

Taxpayer links from the Client menu
the link that needs to be modified from the Link type column.



3

	

Select Modify client link.




4

	

Modify the required fields:

Link details: End date: enter the end date (the end date cannot be before the link start date)

Linked entity:

Status: select Active from the drop-down menu
Input reason.



5

	

Select Next.

The Link additional details screen will display.




6

	

Select Save.

A message will appear 'Taxpayer contact details have been successfully created'.


7	

Check the taxpayer’s Contact Details tab in ICP and update any details related to the former agent:

Addresses - refer to Taxpayer details - address
Opens in a new window
Telephone numbers - refer to Taxpayer details - Phone number
Opens in a new window
Electronic addresses - refer to Taxpayer details - Update electronic address
Opens in a new window
Authorised contacts - refer to Update associates and contacts
Opens in a new window
Outbound preferences - refer to Delivery Preference.
Opens in a new window



8

	

Add a note
Opens in a new window
 on the Siebel activity including:

the full name of the person requesting the update
a summary of the changes made.

Backdate lodgment cycle and due date

How to backdate lodgment cycle and due date in ICP.

Last published 8 January 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page explains how to backdate a lodgment cycle and due date in ICP.

Recent updates

08/01/2026

	

Update to 'Procedure Steps' sections.

	

SD-101000

Procedure Steps
Backdate lodgment cycle
Step	Action


1

	

In ICP, select:

Roles from the Client menu
Add role.



2

	

Complete the following fields:

Role type: select the relevant role type
Account: select the account the role will be added to
Role is non-reporting: select Yes if the role has no reporting obligations
Role start date: enter the start date for the role
Financial year end: select the relevant financial year end date (for example, FBT financial year end date is 31 March)
Effective start date: enter the start date the role is to become active in (for example, if you are adding a GST role from 20/10/2024, the BAS period start date is 01/10/2024)
Effective end date: if no end date is specified by the taxpayer, this field will default to 31/12/9999 when the transaction is saved
Input reason: select the relevant reason for adding the role.

Select Save.

is finalised, issue a revision activity statement

 for that period with the labels for the role
for Annual GST Reporters, the statements are generated in July of the following financial year

STP deferrals and exemptions function

B-1 - Guidelines for single touch payroll deferrals and exemptions.

Last published 11 October 2024

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

B-1

This page explains how the deferrals and exemptions through STP functions work in ATO Online services.

Recent updates
11/10/2024 	Removed bulk request section, updates made to Quarterly reporting Concession section alert and bullets.	SD-86453 


17/03/2022

	

Additional content added under 'Online services for agents'.

	

SD-46348

In Detail
Before you begin

Employers and agents can request deferrals and exemptions through the STP Deferrals and exemptions function of ATO online services.

They can also notify us when there are no payroll events to report.

All requests from Large Business and Government
Opens in a new window
 employers must be submitted using this function.

Online services for business

log into Online services for business

select:

Employees from the menu

STP deferrals and exemptions

complete the form

tick the declaration and Submit.

If the Employees option is not visible, the Payroll Event form permission has not been enabled in Access Manager.

Once the form is submitted, the user will receive an ATO receipt ID and will be advised if the request has been accepted or been sent for assessment.

For more information, refer to Single Touch Payroll- exemption and deferral requests
Opens in a new window
 on ato.gov.au.

Online services for individuals

log into Online services for individuals

select:

Employment from the menu

Manage employees

STP deferrals and exemptions

complete the form

tick the declaration and Submit.

Once the form is submitted, the user will receive an ATO receipt ID and will be advised if the request has been accepted or been sent for assessment.

For more information, refer to Single Touch Payroll- exemption and deferral requests.
Opens in a new window

Online services for agents
Single requests

log in to the Online Services for Agents

select:

the taxpayer

Business

STP deferrals and exemptions

complete the form and Submit.

If the Business option is not visible, the Payroll Event form permission has not been enabled in Access Manager. Only those with Administrator level permissions can update permissions in Access Manager.

Once the form is submitted, the agent will receive an ATO receipt ID and will be advised whether the request has been accepted or has been sent for assessment.

For more information refer to the Online services for agents user guide on the ATO website.

Completing the form

When completing the form, the user will be asked to provide their contact details. This is so the ATO can contact them if more information is required to process their request. The contact details provided will not update any contact details on their tax record, if the taxpayer needs to update their email or contact phone number, this needs to be completed separately.

Refer to the following information if the taxpayer has questions about the form labels/fields.

Defer the date to start reporting through STP

Defer the date to start reporting through STP Phase 2

Unable to report for a period of time

Unable to regularly lodge on or before the pay date (including employers using shadow payroll)

Unable to report via STP

Not paying anyone for a period of time

Quarterly reporting concessions

Additional time required to finalise payroll for end of year

Defer the date to start reporting through STP

Deferral/exemption type: Transitional deferral

Completing the form:

All fields are mandatory

The user will need to select the date they were required to start reporting

1/7/2018 - for substantial employers

1/7/2019 - for small employers

Other - for those not in the above scenarios i.e. new to employing. If this option is selected the user will need to manually enter their required start date. (This would be the date they commenced employing staff for those new to employing)

When Other special circumstances is chosen, a text box appears requesting more information. User must describe their special circumstances in the textbox

There is also an Attachment supporting documentation option if the user would like to include additional evidence to support their request. 

Defer the date to start reporting through STP Phase 2

Deferral/exemption type: Delayed transition to STP expansion

Completing the form:

All fields are mandatory, except for Attach supporting documentation. This field is always displayed and is optional

User selects the most appropriate reason from the dropdown options

When reason of Other special circumstances is chosen, a text box will display and is mandatory. User must describe their special circumstances in the textbox

User must enter the

date the entity is expected to commence STP phase 2 reporting

Number of employees currently on payroll. If the user does not know the exact number, they can make a reasonable estimate.

Information
alert:
If the employee count is greater than 5,000, the user must provide:

a detailed schedule and plan
evidence they will meet their requested deferral extension date.

They may be contacted by an ATO officer to discuss this.

Unable to report for a period of time

Deferral/exemption type: Operational deferral

Completing the form:

All fields are mandatory

The user types in the date they can re-commence reporting

Any combination of weekly, fortnightly and monthly payroll cycles to a total of six can be chosen and one other payroll cycle

When payroll cycle of other is chosen

the number of other pay events field will appear and an amount needs to be entered

the date of first missed payroll event field needs to be completed

When reason of other special circumstances is chosen, a text box appears requesting more information.User must describe their special circumstances in the textbox.

Unable to regularly lodge on or before the pay date (including employers with inbound assignees)

Deferral/exemption type: Recurring deferral

Information
alert:
The employer should attach a copy of their internet bill showing the business name and address. Other documents such as a phone bill or rates notice, stating the business name may be accepted.

Completing the form:

All fields are mandatory, except for Attach supporting documentation, see below

The user types in the start and end date for the deferral period

When the reason of

Intermittent internet connectivity is chosen, the Attach proof of address field will display, and is mandatory

Inbound assignees is selected, an additional field will need to be completed with how many inbound assignee are currently on their payroll. An on-screen message will appear to inform the user they will be allowed up to an additional 62 days to lodge each STP report.

Other special circumstances is chosen

a text box will display and is mandatory. User must describe their special circumstances in the textbox

Attach supporting documentation field also appears, but it is not mandatory. This is available if users wish to provide more information.

Unable to report via STP

Deferral/exemption type: Exemption

Competing the form:

Information
alert:
The employer should attach a copy of their internet bill showing the business name and address. Other documents such as a phone bill or rates notice, stating the business name may be accepted. 

All fields are mandatory

The user types in the end date for the exemption period

When Remote access / poor internet connectivity is chosen, Attach proof of address field appears and is mandatory.

When Other special circumstances is chosen,

a text box appears and is mandatory. User must describe their special circumstances in the textbox

Attach supporting documentation field appears however it is not mandatory. This is available if users wish to provide more information

When completing the form, mandatory From and To date fields will appear.

Not paying anyone for a period of time

Deferral/exemption type: No requirement to report

Completing the form:

Employers are not obligated to notify the ATO if they will have a period of non-reporting however the option to notify the ATO is available to them should they wish to do so.

When completing the form, mandatory From and To date fields will appear.

Quarterly reporting Concession

Deferral/exemption type: Concessional reporting

Completing the form:

Reporting Concession (Micro employer) requests can only be submitted by the employer's registered agent. For concessions commencing after 1 July 2021 the employer will need to meet eligibility criteria and the guidelines for exceptional circumstances.

All fields are mandatory

The user will need to select which concession they are applying for from the reason drop down

Micro - Seasonal employer

Micro employer - Remote/Poor internet

Other special circumstances

The user will need to include the number of employees currently on their payroll. For those who select Seasonal employer as the reason, will also need to provide the number of seasonal employees.

The payroll software used to submit the STP reports will need to be entered.

Depending on the concession is selected, a free text box will display and the user will be asked to provide additional reasoning or detail what industry they are in.

Attach supporting documentation will only be an option when micro employer is selected as the reason.

Additional time required to finalise payroll for end of year

Deferral/exemption type: Finalisation declaration deferral

The employer should attach a copy of their internet bill showing the business name and address. Other documents such as a phone bill or rates notice, stating the business name may be accepted.

Completing the form:

The financial year and the finalisation date are mandatory.

Select the Financial year the deferral is for.

Select the date the report will be finalised. Where greater than 14 days a reason must be provided.

Attach supporting documentation where appropriate.

Complete Contact details. Tick the declaration and Submit.

A print-friendly confirmation with an ATO receipt ID will display. Where

greater than 14 days is requested, the request is sent for assessment. A response can take up to 14 days.

less than 14 days is requested, the deferral is automatically accepted.

STP deferrals
STP deferrals

B-3 - Guide for single touch payroll (STP) deferrals.

Last published 6 May 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

B-3

This page provides information on:

qualifying earnings

different types of Single Touch Payroll deferrals available to employers 

effects of a deferral

requesting a deferral

confirming a deferral

service standard for processing a deferral

informal review of a deferral decision.

An employer may need to apply for a deferral if they:

experience exceptional or unforeseen circumstances, such as a natural disaster or circumstances outside the control of the employer, or
are unable to meet their STP reporting obligations by the due date.

Deferrals may be approved in real time or may require manual assessment.

Recent updates

06/05/2026

	

Updates to Procedure Steps 'Qualifying Earnings (QE)' added.

	

SD-104550

Self Help
ATO website
Help the caller go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

the taxpayer using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.

Taxpayers can obtain more information from the ATO website on:

Deferrals
Opens in a new window
STP expansion (Phase 2) delayed transitions.
Opens in a new window

Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Go to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

Procedure Steps
PORO

Establish PORO,
Opens in a new window
 if required.

Types of deferrals

Different types of deferrals are available depending on the employer's circumstances. If an employer is not eligible for one of the deferral types listed below, they may be eligible for one of the reporting concessions
Opens in a new window
 or exemptions.
Opens in a new window

Qualifying Earnings (QE)
Delayed transition to STP expansion (Phase 2)
Transitional deferral
Operational (OP) deferral
Recurring deferral
Finalisation deferral.
Qualifying Earnings (QE)

From 1 July 2026, employers will use qualifying earnings
Opens in a new window
 (QE) as the base to calculate their employee’s super as part of their new PDS requirements.

QE includes ordinary time earnings (OTE), salary sacrifice contributions and other amounts that are currently included in an employee's salary or wages for super guarantee.

Currently, STP employers report super liability and/or OTE amounts for each employee. From 1 July 2026, employers will be required to report both QE and super liability in STP.

The ATO is working with digital service providers (DSPs) to update their STP products to enable employers to commence reporting QE from 1 July 2026. Employers are not required to apply for a deferral for more time to commence QE reporting, this is because the ATO will still accept Super liability and/or OTE until 30 June 2027.

If the employer asks for more information, advise:

The ATO recognises that some employers may be unable to start reporting qualifying earnings (QE) on 1 July 2026. It is important that you begin reporting QE and Super liability as soon as possible after that date. When you do start reporting QE, you will need to include year-to-date (YTD) amounts in both qualifying QE and Super Liability.

The ATO will still accept reporting of Super Liability and/or ordinary time earnings for pay events until 30 June 2027. Noting that not reporting the required amounts (QE and Super Liability) during the 2026–27 financial year could attract ATO attention. Information on our compliance approach
Opens in a new window
 is available on ato.gov.au.

For this reason, you do not need to request a deferral for STP reporting at this time. You should ensure that you are using a compliant product for STP reporting by 1 July 2027.

Although reporting QE in STP may commence after 1 July 2026, you must start paying super guarantee on pay day from 1 July 2026.

From 1 July 2027, if you haven’t started reporting both QE and superannuation liability for pay events, your STP reports may be rejected.

For more information refer to How to report super in Single Touch Payroll.
Opens in a new window

Delayed transition to STP expansion (Phase 2)

The mandatory start date for Phase 2 reporting was 1 January 2022.

This is specific to those employers who are already reporting through STP and require additional time to transition to the updated software solution.

Information

Remind employers who have been granted a Delayed transition to STP expansion they:

must still lodge via their current STP enabled solution during their deferral period
can begin reporting Phase 2 information before their deferral period ends, if their software becomes ready.

When an employer's current Phase 1 STP enabled solution is being decommissioned, consider an Operational deferral.

 Transitional deferral

Information
alert:
Transitional deferrals will only be considered if the taxpayer has exceptional circumstances or is new to business.

A Transitional deferral is for employers who have not yet started reporting through STP. If an employer is not ready to report through STP, they may be eligible for a transitional deferral. This will defer their reporting start date.

Operational (OP) deferral

If the employer has started reporting through STP but is unable to submit their payroll data on or before a pay event for a specific time, they can apply for an Operational (OP) deferral. OP deferrals are for a set period of time and not recurring.

Warning

When an Operational deferral is granted due to the decommissioning of Phase 1 software, the employer must resume lodgment via an STP enabled solution by the end of this deferral period. This is so they continue to meet their STP reporting obligations.

Recurring deferral

If the employer has started reporting through STP but needs more time to submit their payroll data on a recurring and regular basis, they can apply for a Recurring deferral.

Finalisation deferral

If the employer requires additional time to finalise their end of year payroll data, they can apply for a Finalisation deferral.

Effects of a deferral

Employers that are granted a deferral must continue to comply with their existing PAYG withholding obligations, including:

reporting and paying their PAYG withholding liability

issuing payment summaries to their employees

lodging a Payment Summary Annual Report (PSAR).

Requesting a deferral
Employers and registered agents

All employers and their agents can apply for deferrals, exemptions and concessions using the STP deferrals and exemptions form via:

ATO Online services for business, or

Online services for agents.

For information about how to apply online, refer to STP deferrals and exemptions function.
Opens in a new window

If the taxpayer is experiencing exceptional or unforeseen circumstances outside of their control or is new to business and they are unable to self-serve, go to STP deferral and exemption for Frontline Operations telephony.
Opens in a new window
 These circumstances could include, but not limited to:

impacted by natural disaster

no internet connectivity in a remote location.

Request to delay transition to STP expansion (Phase 2)
Situation	Requirements


Employers with 5,000 or more employees.

	

To request more time, employers must provide the following additional information as an attachment with their online application:

a schedule and/or a detailed plan outlining their circumstances, and

steps they are taking to prepare for their transition.

Confirming an employer deferral

Outcomes
Service standard

The service standard for deferral requests that have not been submitted using the STP deferrals and exemptions function on ATO Online services is 28 days.

If the service standard has passed, refer to STP escalations.
Opens in a new window

For requests submitted by the STP deferrals and exemptions function, refer to STP deferrals and exemptions function.
Opens in a new window

Informal review

Taxpayers may request an informal review if they disagree with the deferral decision. The request must be made in writing and sent via:

whitemail: post to

	GPO BOX 999
	SYDNEY NSW 2000

Online services for agents by selecting the following secure mail message topic
Topic: General questions / problems / help
Subject: Single Touch Payroll deferrals.

The secure mail message option is not available in Online services for business. Employers will need to use the whitemail option.

The request should include:

reasons why they disagree with the deferral decision
the Our reference number located on their deferral decision letter
additional supporting information which was not previously provided in their original request.

For more information, refer to Objections, amendments and reviews
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.

Single Touch Payroll (STP) exemptions

B-3 - Lists scenarios where an exemption for Single Touch Payroll (STP) may be possible.

Last published 14 November 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

B-3

This page lists scenarios where an exemption for Single Touch Payroll (STP) may be possible.

Employers may be exempt from reporting payroll data through STP. These exemptions include reporting:

a particular financial year

certain payments.

Some employers may be covered by a class exemption (e.g. an employer with a Withholding Payer Number (WPN)) or an exemption based on their individual circumstances.

For more information, go to Exemptions from STP reporting
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.

No longer applicable from 1 July 2019

The following exemptions are no longer applicable from 1 July 2019:

foreign employees

insolvency practitioners

seasonal workers.

No longer applicable from 1 July 2021

From 1 July 2021 closely held payees exemptions are no longer applicable.

Employers no longer covered by this exemption will have 3 options for reporting payments to their closely held payees. They are:

reporting actual payments each pay event (Pay day reporting)

reporting actual payments quarterly

quarterly reporting of a reasonable estimate - Reporting 25% of the total salary and wages and/or Directors fees and PAYGW using the latest payment summary or finalised STP report amounts as a basis for the estimate.

Recent updates
14/11/2025	

Update to 'Withholder payer number (WPN) holders' section.

	SD-98954
Scenarios
Closely held employees

For the 2019-20 and 2020-21 financial year, employers with closely held employees will be exempt from reporting them through STP.

A closely held payee is one who is not at arm's length. They are directly related to the entity they receive payments from, for example:

family members of a family business

directors or shareholders of a company

beneficiaries of a trust.

If the employer elects to use the closely held employee's exemption, they should keep records that support their decision. They do not need to notify the ATO.

From 1 July 2021, closely held employees will need to be reported through STP.

Find out more about STP pay events.
Opens in a new window

This exemption is different to the Closely held lodgment concession
Opens in a new window
 available to tax agents lodging the Payment Summary Annual Report.

Withholder payer number (WPN) holders

WPN holders are exempt from reporting through STP for all financial years from 2018-2019 to 2032-33. This is a class exemption so there is no need to lodge an exemption application. WPN holders must start reporting through STP from 1 July 2033.

From 1 July 2026, employers with a withholding payer number (WPN) who voluntarily report in Single Touch Payroll (STP) will only be able to lodge through an authorised representative, such as a registered tax agent or BAS agent. These taxpayers should be directed to web content outlining the changes and what they need to do, Withholding payer number holders reporting through STP.
Opens in a new window

For more detail refer to:

Setting up Single Touch Payroll (STP)
Opens in a new window
Notifying the ATO of an SBR-hosted software service.
Opens in a new window
Employers with internet issues

Where an employer is unable to start reporting through STP due to connectivity issues, they may be able to apply for STP reporting relief by requesting an exemption.

Requests are assessed on a case by case basis.

If the employer uses a Payroll Service Provider (PSP), the PSP can request an exemption on their behalf.

Long service leave and redundancy scheme administrators

For the 2018-19, 2019-20 and 2020-21 financial years, administrators of long service leave and redundancy schemes who do not use an STP payroll solution to manage payments to members are exempt from reporting those payments.

Employers in certain industries, such as building, construction or cleaning, may make regular contributions to a long service leave or redundancy scheme for their employees. Those employees, who are members of the scheme, may be entitled to a payment of long service leave or termination of payment if certain conditions are met.

If the employer elects to use this exemption, they should keep records to support that decision. They do not need to notify the ATO.

From 1 July 2021, these payments need to be reported through STP.

Exceptional or unforeseen circumstances

Exemptions can be considered if the taxpayer has exceptional or unforeseen circumstances, such as:

the taxpayer has been impacted by a natural disaster

advanced age of the employer

serious illness of an employer or family member, sole practitioner agent in a small firm.

This list is not exhaustive; there may be other circumstances beyond their control. Find out more information about taxpayers impacted by natural disaster.
Opens in a new window

In Detail

Employers that are granted an exemption will not need to start reporting through STP for the period they are covered by the exemption. The employer must continue to comply with their existing PAYG withholding obligations, including:

reporting and paying their PAYG withholding liability

issuing payment summaries to their employees

lodging a Payment Summary Annual Report (PSAR).

Applying for an exemption

Employers or their representatives should submit their exemption request through the STP deferrals and exemptions internal tool through ATO online services.

Find instructions on STP deferrals and exemptions for Frontline Operations telephony
Opens in a new window
 if the taxpayer insists on requesting an exemption over the phone.

Notification of outcome

If a taxpayer applies for an exemption, the ATO will issue written notification of the outcome. For those requests accepted in full, the letter will be issued automatically by ICP.

Objections

If the employer has requested an exemption and their request has been denied, they can lodge an objection.

To request an objection, the request:

must be submitted within 60 days of the refusal letter

can be submitted using the online form function of Online services for business or Online services for agents or in writing

must include the reference number of the refusal letter.

Find out more about Objections, amendments and review
Opens in a new window
 on ato.gov.au.

Other Lodgments
STP pay events
STP pay events

B-3 - Employer reporting through STP and the minimum requirements for each STP payroll event.

Last published 22 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 

Background

B-3

This page provides information on the rules for reporting through STP and the minimum requirements each time an employer submits a STP pay event. It includes information on the 2018 pay event service and additional information that is available through the 2020 pay event service.

Warning alert:
For payroll solution functionality enquiries, the employer should talk to their Digital Software Provider.
Recent updates
22/12/2025	

Updates throughout the page.

	

SD-100758

Self Help
ATO website
Success alert:
Help the caller go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.

Taxpayers can obtain more information from the ATO website on:

STP reporting guidelines for employers
Opens in a new window

the expansion of STP (Phase 2).
Opens in a new window

Taxpayers can view their pay, update and adjustment events via:

Employees function of Online services for business
Opens in a new window

Employment > Manage employees in Online services for individuals
Opens in a new window

STP reporting function of Online Services for Agents.
Opens in a new window

Information alert:
Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

In Detail
Confirm lodgment of STP reports

If the taxpayer wants to confirm pay, update or adjustment events for:

up to 3 events, go to STP employer payroll data
Opens in a new window

more than 3 events, the taxpayer should self-serve.
Opens in a new window

Pay events

The service standard for processing a pay event is 72 hours.

A pay event:

occurs every time a file is generated by STP-enabled software which includes payments subject to withholding. An authorised person will need to declare the data is true and correct

can be generated at the following levels:

ABN

Branch, or

Business Management software identifier (BMS) ID.

must have at least one employee record and can only have one record for that employee. However, they may have separate unique payroll identifiers which will result in multiple statements viewable in myTax

must have the following correct employee information before submitting

personal details

payroll, and

superannuation.

Pay day reporting

Pay events must be reported on or before the pay day. The pay day is either the:

payment date stipulated in the electronic transaction to the employer's financial institution, or

date the employer intends to make the payment to the employees financial account.

Information alert:
The above pay day reporting does not apply to small employers with Closely held payees. These employers have different reporting concessions that may apply.

For more information refer to STP - closely held reporting.
Opens in a new window

Out-of-cycle payments are reported on the regular pay day. The employer can lodge multiple pay events (the payroll solution will generate a time stamp which is used to identify the latest record) for the same day.

If the employee is paid:

out of two payroll systems, there will be two corresponding employment income statements for each payroll record updated

more than once on a particular day, the employer may provide a single pay event for that employee including the latest up-to-date figures.

Cancellation of STP data

The ATO will only cancel pay events in particular circumstances.

For more information, refer to STP corrections - Cancellations.
Opens in a new window

Missed pay event

Where the employer has missed a pay event, they will need to fix the Year to Date (YTD) figures on the next pay event where the affected employees have continuity of employment.

For more information, refer to STP corrections - Employers self-correcting their payroll reports.
Opens in a new window

Payroll solutions
Transitioning to STP reporting

Where an employer has started reporting part way through the year, they have a choice of what payroll data they include in their first pay event.

If they start reporting with...	Then...
a zero year to date balance for all employees	

any amounts not reported through STP, will need to be included on:

a payment summary issued to the employee, and

an annual payment summary report issued to the ATO.


year to date amounts paid to all employees	

if the complete YTD information is reported through STP, there is no need for:

 a payment summary, or

 payment summary annual report.

For more information, refer to Transitioning to Single Touch Payroll
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.

Using multiple payroll solutions
Your payroll solution will generate your STP report for your pay cycle by ABN, branch and BMS ID. We use this combination of information, together with the Payroll IDs you report, to identify when we need to display a separate income statement to a taxpayer. We will display a separate income statement for each combination.
Refer to what happens
Opens in a new window
 in the Single Touch Payroll employer reporting guidelines on the ATO website.
Changes within a financial year

Some payroll solutions may have an option to include employers previous BMS ID or
the employees previous Payroll IDs in an update event.

This function will: 

make it easier for the employer to transition their employees year to date where they have changed their:
business structure, or 
software subscription 
help the ATO reduce and fix issues where duplicate income statements display for employees in the ATO Online.

If the employers payroll solution does not have this functionality, the employer will still be able to zero out the previous BMS or Payroll ID combination.

This is an optional functionality of the 2020 Pay event service and not all STP enabled solutions will offer this.

Refer to Changing your payroll solutions or employees' Payroll IDs
Opens in a new window
 in the Single Touch Payroll employer reporting guidelines on the ATO website.

The employer should contact their payroll provider if they:

 are unsure of what options are available to them through their payroll solution, or
if they have questions on how to use the functions within their payroll solution.


PAYG withholding branches

If the business is:

branched for PAYG withholding, it must report via STP for each branch that is required to be registered for PAYG withholding
Opens in a new window

not impacted by branching and their software requires them to enter a number into the branch field, taxpayers can include the number 1.

Information alert:
The employer should not include leading zeros before the branch code. For example, if the branch is one, they should simply put number 1 and not 001.

The employer should be referred to their Digital Software Provider (DSP) on how to setup branching in their payroll solution.

Payments

Under STP, there are a number of payment types
Opens in a new window
 that need to be reported that are:

mandatory

voluntary, or

cannot be reported.

For a list of mandatory, voluntary and payments that cannot be reported, refer to Payments you must report 
Opens in a new window

Out of cycle

Employers may make payments (bonuses, commissions, payments in advance or back payments etc) that fall outside of the regular pay day cycle. They may be reported by either:

submitting a pay event on or before the pay day the payment is made

include the payment in the next pay cycle pay event unless it crosses financial years.

Information alert:
If the next regular pay cycle is in the following financial year, the employer must report the payment by 30 June in the year the payment was made before they finalise.
Not required to report

In circumstances where there has not been a withholding event, employers will have no requirement to report. The following scenarios are examples where an employer will not have a requirement to report, if they

are temporarily not employing staff, or

no longer employ staff.

Where an employer has not paid any employees in a regular payroll cycle, they will have no requirement to submit a STP pay event report. This could be for one or multiple payroll periods. As an example, a business may have temporarily closed due to a natural disaster and did not pay any employees during that period. These employers will have no requirement to report.

If an employer has no requirement to report, they do not need to advise the ATO of their STP reporting circumstances. However, some employers may wish to notify us for their own record keeping purposes. If an employer chooses to notify the ATO that they did not pay any employees for a period of time, they can do so by completing the STP deferrals and exemptions
Opens in a new window
 form available in ATO Online Services.

Information alert:
If a taxpayer advises that they have no employees at all, they do no need to notify the ATO and should not apply for deferral or exemption. If the employer has a PAYG withholding role, they should consider cancelling the role. For more information, refer to Cancel or modify PAYG withholding role.
Opens in a new window
Year to date (YTD) information

When lodging a pay event, the report also needs to include the YTD values.
Opens in a new window
 These amounts can be manually adjusted on each pay event.

The YTD amounts cannot be negative but may be:

less than a previous report (such as recovery of an overpayment)

zero amounts.

The information should be updated:

within 14 days of the need for a correction is identified, or

in the next pay event where the affected employee has continuity of employment.

Employers failing to make a fix within the required period are liable for penalties.
Opens in a new window

Finalisation Declaration

Once an employee's data has been finalised for the financial year, the employer makes a finalisation declaration by providing a finalisation indicator in the pay event. It declares the employer:

has provided all the required information for that financial year, and

will become Tax Ready for the employee to use as pre-fill in myTax.

Finalisation Declarations are included on the pay event (child) form and only appear on an employee's record.  It is not viewable on the pay event (parent) form on the employer's record.

Employers do not need to wait until the end of financial year to finalise. Dependant on their software functionality they may be able to finalise employees at cessation date.

For more information, refer to STP End of year finalisation.
Opens in a new window

Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability
Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability

Guidance for identifying taxpayers experiencing hardship or vulnerability and connecting them with appropriate support.

Last published 8 May 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page helps staff identify when a person may be experiencing vulnerability and requires extra support. It also explains how to escalate a referral for tailored help.

The Tailored Support Hub ensures fairness in how we engage with taxpayers who are experiencing vulnerability. While our core role is to collect tax, we recognise that life circumstances can make it difficult for people to meet their obligations. For example:

illness
financial hardship
family violence, or
digital exclusion.

Vulnerability is often complex and unique. Our response must be tailored, empathetic, and coordinated.

The Tailored Support Hub brings together several teams, including the Specialised Client Support Team. We aim to:

provide the right support at the right time
deliver the right experience for the right outcome, and
support those who need help and their advocates.
Recent updates
23/03/2026	Updates throughout page.	SD-102261, 102967, 102761
Self Help
Support options

Taxpayers can visit Financial difficulties, disasters and vulnerability support
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website for support options available.

If a taxpayer is in immediate danger, advise them to call 000 immediately.

If a taxpayer is struggling with their mental health and needs immediate support, they can phone at any time to:

Beyond Blue on 1300 224 636

Lifeline on 13 11 14.

Scenarios
Advocate help desk

The advocate help desk is a dedicated service for financial counsellors, national tax clinics and other advocates. This gives them a direct phone line and email address to the ATO for support.

If you are speaking to a financial counsellor who needs specific immediate assistance: 

warm transfer them to x92439
advise they can also call direct on 1300 137 295, between 9am and 5pm in their local state.

For non-urgent requests, they can email AdvocateHelpDesk@ato.gov.au, and:

include taxpayer specific details, or
if they are not a current authorised contact, attach the Change of details for individuals (NAT 2817)
Opens in a new window
 form signed by the taxpayer.
Warning

The Advocate help desk phone number and email address are for Financial Counsellors and National tax clinics ONLY. Do not provide these details to a taxpayer directly.

Indigenous helpline

The Indigenous helpline is a dedicated service for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples. This service provides a tailored cultural interaction with staff specialising in helping Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people.

If you are speaking to a taxpayer who identifies as Indigenous and requires a culturally tailored approach:

transfer them to x92215

advise they can also call the Indigenous helpline direct on 13 10 30, between 8am and 6pm in their local state.

Hardship

Hardship trained staff within the hub, assist taxpayers who may be experiencing financial difficulties or serious financial hardship.

An individual or business can be considered to have financial difficulties where unexpected or extenuating circumstances leave them unable to pay their debt for a short period of time.

An individual is in serious hardship when payment of a primary tax debt will result in them not having the means to pay for basic needs, such as:

food

clothing

medical supplies

accommodation, and

education for themselves, their family or dependants.

If you are speaking to a taxpayer who is experiencing financial hardship or financial difficulties:

refer to Taxpayer facing financial difficulties or serious hardship
Opens in a new window

gather capacity to pay information

warm transfer the taxpayer to the Hardship team on x92176. If unable to complete a warm transfer,

provide the taxpayer with the external number 1300 788 347, and

offer to place in the queue.

Specialised Client Support

The Specialised Client Support team provides extra support to people experiencing vulnerability, who have limited or no capacity to engage, lodge and/or pay on time. 

Support options are tailored to meet the needs of taxpayers who: 

are experiencing vulnerability and require ongoing taxpayer management
have immediate and severe vulnerable circumstances, and
cannot engage, lodge and/or pay on time.

They make intentional decisions to support taxpayers experiencing vulnerability and their advocates including:

tailored payment plans

release from tax debts

deferral of lodgment and payment due dates, including compulsory repayments

varying PAYG Instalments

General Interest Charge (GIC) remissions

Requests not to offset

priority processing.

The Specialised Client Support team includes staff who are trained and supported to assist taxpayers experiencing:

family or domestic violence

coercion

financial abuse.

If a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability or extenuating circumstances that makes it difficult for them to meet their tax obligations, refer them to the Specialised Client Support team. They will be triaged to receive the appropriate level of support.

Warm transfer the taxpayer to the Specialised Client Support team on x92196 if they are experiencing any of the following:

extreme or immediate vulnerability

family or domestic violence

coercion or financial abuse

they are incarcerated and require additional support, or

they have made immediate threats of self-harm.

If unable to complete a warm transfer:

provide the taxpayer with the external number 1300 788 347, and

offer to place in the queue.

In Detail
Definition of vulnerability
What is Vulnerability?

Vulnerability can impact a taxpayer in different ways. It can be temporary, long term or a permanent state. It is difficult to describe because of the complexity and personal circumstances a taxpayer is facing.

We must seek to understand our taxpayers through the factors or barriers stopping them from paying, lodging, and engaging. These factors may include, but are not limited to:

 Factors	These might include...


life events: which are significant personal events that impact one's ability to manage tax responsibilities.

	

illness

relationship breakdown

domestic violence

addiction

bereavement

natural disasters.


access barriers: which refer to challenges related to accessing necessary resources or information.	

remoteness

cultural and linguistic diversity

incarceration

lack of digital access

challenges accessing support.




economic barriers: which hinder tax compliance

	

low financial literacy and knowledge

homelessness

financial hardship

financial coercion.




psychological barriers: which could affect a person’s capacity to meet their obligations

	

mental health issues

fear

shame

guilt.

Experiencing these factors does not automatically indicate someone cannot engage, lodge, or pay. Taxpayers experiencing these barriers deserve our empathy and may need additional support.

Determining if a taxpayer is experiencing vulnerability

To help you determine if a taxpayer is experiencing vulnerability, refer to the Vulnerability model
This link will download a file
 and consider the following:

Capacity to engage

Does the person have cognitive challenges stopping them from understanding their tax obligations?

Does the person have mental health challenges stopping them from engaging, registering or monitoring their tax and super?

Capacity to lodge

Does the person have no or limited access to a computer and internet?

Is the person experiencing a natural disaster?

Does the person live in a remote area of Australia with limited access to services?

Capacity to pay

Does the person have no income or not expect to have income into the future?

Is the person experiencing financial hardship, finding it hard to meet basic needs such as food and shelter?

Does the person have no disposable assets?

Conversation guide

A taxpayer's mental health and wellbeing should always come first.

It is important to take a whole of taxpayer approach to ensure safety of the taxpayer and their information.

You should not take on any role that should be handled by a qualified professional (e.g. psychologist, financial advisor) as this could potentially become a barrier to the taxpayer obtaining appropriate support.

People can often try to hide their vulnerabilities. This makes it important that you're asking the right questions and are on the lookout for cues. A taxpayer you're helping might be facing a situation that makes them vulnerable, and they might need your help.

A caller will not always say:

'I am the victim of domestic violence'. They might say, 'My partner is going to flip out at me if they find out I have a debt'.

'I am homeless'. They might say, 'That address is my old address. I can't give you a new one, I'm in between places right now'.

Demonstrating empathy

How we respond when we recognise vulnerability is important. Words are important. Use plain English and be collaborative and open.

The words that we use can affect a person’s sense of self and lead to more disadvantage and harm. Appropriate language is a vital component in communicating a sense of self-determination, because feeling powerless can be overwhelming, especially when decisions seem to be, or are, in the hands of others.

Here are some tips.

Do...	Don't...


put people first

For example: say 'the person experiencing mental health condition'

	

label people.

For example: 'the mentally ill person'.




use positive affirmative language that supports and promotes recovery

For example: 'I’m here to help and we can sort this out for you today?'

	

use condescending, patronising, tokenistic, intimidating or discriminating language.

For example: terms such as 'afflicted with', 'suffers from' or 'is a victim of'.




ask if the person feels they have been consulted and listened to

For example: 'Does all of that make sense?'

	

use jargon, or unfamiliar terms.

For example: 'FAWL' or 'DPN'.




validate a person’s experiences

For example: 'I hear how difficult this is for you'

	

minimise a person’s experience in the urgency of managing issues.

For example: 'I don’t need to know all of that detail'.




allow people the time to find the words and express what they need to say

	jump in and speak for someone.

involve people in the development of treatment or approach
	

make assumptions or assume you know what’s best.

If a taxpayer is in financial distress or experiencing vulnerability:

they may become distressed or emotional during calls

you do not have to tolerate threatening, abusive or inappropriate language.

For more information, refer to:

Emotionally charged taxpayer interactions
This link will download a file

Handling angry / abusive taxpayers & unprofessional agents.
Opens in a new window

Suggested actions

Consider the taxpayer's circumstances and refer to the table below for the assistance that may be provided.

Assistance	Details


Taxpayer is experiencing financial difficulties or serious hardship

	

Refer Taxpayer facing financial difficulties or serious hardship
Opens in a new window
 to determine if the taxpayer:

is experiencing financial difficulties or serious financial hardship, and

may benefit from

a payment arrangement

additional time to pay

deferral, or

help with lodging.

If yes,

warm transfer to the Hardship team on x92176

provide the taxpayer with the Hardship team's contact number 1300 788 347, Option 1 (hardship) in case the call drops out.

If taxpayer has disclosed immediate vulnerability, please follow vulnerability escalation below.




Taxpayer's capacity to engage, or lodge and pay is impacted as they are experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors, including:

threats of self-harm
family and domestic violence, or
financial coercion
	

If the taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability:

warm transfer to Specialised Client Support on x92196. The taxpayer will be triaged for specialised support. If unable to complete a warm transfer,

provide the taxpayer with the external number 1300 788 347, and

offer to place in the queue.

if you are working in an internal team that is not telephony enabled, and need to refer a taxpayer for extra support

forward the taxpayer's details to the Specialised Client Support team  at SpecialisedClientSupportHub@ato.gov.au

Warning

This is an internal email address. Do not provide this email address to the taxpayer.

Activity Statements - registered agents quarterly due dates
Activity Statements - registered agents quarterly due dates

D-1, B-1 - This page explains the registered agent lodgment program due dates for quarterly activity statements.

Last published 15 July 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1, B-1

This page explains the registered agent lodgment program due dates, for electronic lodgment and payment of quarterly activity statements.

Recent updates

15/07/2025

	

Updates made throughout the page.

	

SD-95265

Self Help
ATO website
Help the taxpayer to go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the taxpayer about self-help options, which may lead to:

using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.
 Agents can obtain information about Activity statements
Opens in a new window
 from the ATO website.

Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

In Detail

When a quarterly activity statement generates, a lodgment due date is forecast and recorded in:

ICP Client calendar,
Opens in a new window
 and
IPS.
Lodgment program due dates for electronic lodgment

The Lodgment program provides registered agents an extra 4 weeks for lodgment and payment of activity statements (except for quarter 2) where:

an electronic activity statement outbound preference is recorded in ICP, and
there is an active agent link at the

Taxpayer level, or
ICA level.

Quarter 2 statements are due 28 February due to the Christmas period lodgment concession.

Information

The extra 4 weeks will still apply if the taxpayer has an active Single Touch Payroll (STP) role and:

they lodge the activity statement instead of the linked agent, or

a paper outbound preference and active agent link is recorded in ICP.

Activity statements not eligible for additional time

Lodgment program due dates do not apply to taxpayers who:

lodge a quarterly

PAYG instalment notice (Form R),

GST instalment notice (Form S) or  

GST and PAYG instalment notice (Form T). These are only required to be lodged where the taxpayer is varying the instalment amount

lodge a PAYG instalment consolidated head entity activity statement

have a monthly cycle for GST or PAYG Instalments

lodge activity statements with only a monthly PAYG Withholding cycle.

Taxpayers who operate on substituted accounting periods (SAP) are only eligible if their quarters align with the standard quarters.

Due dates and concessions

Agents can refer to Quarterly activity statements
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.

Lodgment obligation	Original due date	Registered agent lodgment program due date


Quarter 4, 2024–2025

	

28 July 2025

	

25 August 2025




Quarter 1, 2025–2026

	

28 October 2025

	25 November 2025

To be confirmed when Lodgment program 2026–2027 is developed.

Help Completing Income Tax Returns
When to lodge and pay
When to lodge and pay

Information on when to lodge and pay.

Last published 3 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides information on income tax return lodgment due dates, including when:

lodging with a registered agent

making payment

unable to lodge by due date

disputes by registered tax agents

lodging an early tax return.

Recent updates 
03/12/2025	Minor updates made throughout page. 	

SD-99401

In Detail
When to lodge

Taxpayers must lodge their tax return by 31 October. A penalty may apply if not lodged on time.

Unable to lodge by due date

If the taxpayer is unable to lodge by the due date, they may need a deferral to lodge their tax return, go to Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Lodging with a registered tax agent

The due date may differ if the taxpayer uses a registered tax agent to lodge their return. They must see their registered tax agent before 31 October to qualify for their tax agent lodgment dates. Refer to:

Tax returns by taxpayer type,
Opens in a new window
 and

Individuals - Lodgment program tax return due dates.
Opens in a new window
Payment

The due date for payment will be no earlier than 21 November. The Notice of Assessment will show if they have to pay additional tax for the year, as well as the due date for payment.

Taxpayers must lodge even if they think they will have difficulties paying any associated debt. The ATO can discuss payment arrangement options if they are unable to pay in full. If the taxpayer requests a:

payment deferral, go to Deferrals and suspensions
Opens in a new window

payment plan, advise them to lodge their tax return prior to requesting a payment plan by

using online services for individuals
Opens in a new window

using the ATO app
Opens in a new window
 (for individuals only - including sole traders), or

calling the debt Infoline 13 11 42.

Prepayment

Taxpayers can choose to make prepayments towards an anticipated tax bill.

For more information, refer to Making prepayments
Opens in a new window
 at the ATO website.

Lodging an early tax return

To be eligible to lodge an early tax return, the taxpayer must:

be a non-resident or ceasing to be a resident of Australia for tax purposes, and

not receive any further Australian sourced income after leaving Australia, excluding dividend, royalty or interest income.

An early tax return can be lodged by a tax agent (using SBR enabled software), or by paper. Refer to Lodge you tax return before leaving Australia
Opens in a new window
 for more information, including instructions on how to correct a prior year paper form to reflect current financial year dates.

Additional information the taxpayer should be aware of

If normally eligible to submit a refund of franking credit (RFC) application, they will need to complete a tax return. The RFC form can only be used when they are an Australian resident for tax purposes for the whole financial year.

Taxpayers should not expect their return to be processed before their departure, unless hardship provisions apply.

The tax return will be sent to the postal address provided by the taxpayer.

It is preferable, but not compulsory, that taxpayers lodge their returns before leaving Australia. Returns sent from overseas will still be accepted and processed.

They will need to consider several factors regarding income and offsets:

a pro-rata tax-free threshold

a pro-rata Medicare levy

Medicare levy surcharge

tax withholding on dividends

capital gains tax

applicable tax offsets, and

tax withholding on interest and royalties, and

HELP, SSL, ABSTUDY SSL, AASL, SFSS or VSL debt and outstanding lodgments or payments.
Opens in a new window

Working Holiday Maker (WHM) individuals

WHM individuals will have an ICP indicator, located on the Additional taxpayer details - Individuals screen. Agents with access to ATO online will be able to view the indicator when pre-filling the tax return. The indicator is a non-editable field. If the taxpayer or agent requests the indicator be removed, the taxpayer's WHM status must be confirmed with the Department of Home Affairs. The indicator can only be changed by data transfer from the department.

A WHM will not be required to lodge an income tax return if:

all of their income was earned as salary or wages while they were a working holiday maker, and

the total of their taxable income for the income year was less than $45,001.

Disputing payment and lodgment due dates

To check due dates, go to Due dates in ICP.
Opens in a new window
 If the taxpayer is disputing, the following pages may assist:

Disputing lodgment or payment date
Opens in a new window
 (for June balancers)

Disputing SAP or taxpayer is a June balancer
Opens in a new window
 (substituted accounting periods balancer)

Disputing overdue prior year returns,
Opens in a new window
 or

Disputed debt.
Opens in a new window

Lodgment due dates
Activity Statements - Quarterly
Activity Statements - Quarterly

D-1, B-1 - Lodgment due dates for quarterly activity statements.

Last published 11 June 2024

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1, B-1

This page provides the lodgment due dates for quarterly activity statements, including:

statutory due dates for lodgment and payment

substituted accounting period (SAP)

electronic lodgment concession for self-preparers

head company of a consolidated group.

Recent updates

15/11/2023

	

Updated 'Concession due dates' section.

	

SD-76400

Self Help
ATO website
Success alert:
Help the caller go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

the taxpayer using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.

Taxpayers can find more information from the ATO website on:

Activity statements - Lodgment and payment due dates
Opens in a new window
 (for registered agents)

Due dates
Opens in a new window
 (for taxpayers with registered agents)

Due dates for lodging and paying your BAS
Opens in a new window

Activity statements
Opens in a new window

Quarterly GST reporting.
Opens in a new window

Information
alert:
Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

In Detail
Statutory due dates for lodgment and payment

Quarter 1: 28 October

Quarter 2: 28 February

Quarter 3: 28 April

Quarter 4: 28 July.

Substituted accounting period

SAP taxpayers balance on a date other than 30 June.

Early balancing SAP taxpayers have financial years which end earlier than if they were June balancers.

Late balancing SAP taxpayers have financial years which end later than if they were June balancers.

SAP lodgers must lodge and pay by the 28th day of the following month after their quarter ends, except in a 'standard' quarter 2 where the due date is 28 February.

If the taxpayer wants more information, go to Substituted Accounting Period (SAP) applications
Opens in a new window
 at Income tax returns.

Electronic lodgment concession for self-preparers

When a quarterly activity statement is generated, a lodgment due date is forecast and:

set in the ICP Client calendar, and

reflected in IPS.

Self-preparer concessions do not apply to:

monthly activity statements

annual activity statements

monthly GST payers with other quarterly roles

quarterly instalment notices - Forms R, S or T

quarterly pay as you go (PAYG) instalments for head companies of consolidated groups

SAP taxpayers

identified as a 'large business taxpayer', or

whose periods do not align with the standard quarters

any other taxpayers who do not have an original due date of the 28th.

Eligibility

The activity statement will receive a 2 week electronic lodgment and payment concession where:

the taxpayer (including a registered agent who lodges their own activity statement) has an electronic activity statement outbound preference in ICP, and

an active agent link does not exist at either the taxpayer or Integrated Client Account (ICA) level.

The exception is for quarter 2 where:

a lodgment concession already applies for the Christmas period

the due date is always 28 February.

Provided the taxpayer has an active Single Touch Payroll role, the 2 week concession will still apply:

if the taxpayer has a paper activity statement outbound preference is in ICP, and

no active agent link is recorded in ICP.

SAP taxpayers

Eligible SAP taxpayers will also receive the self-preparer concession. This means they will lodge and pay quarterly activity statements 6 weeks after the quarter ends.

The lodgment method is recorded in:

ICP - in the form summary Input by field, and

IPS -  User ID field.

Activity statements lodgment codes in ICP and IPS 
Lodgment method	Lodgment code


Tax agents via Online services for agents

	

TXGNT




BAS agent via Online services for agents

	

BSGNT




Online services for business

	

BNIND




ATO online for Individuals

	

INDIV




Business Portal (post 2017)

	

PRTLN




Business Portal (pre 2017)

	

EA5

Concession due dates

Quarter 1: (July - September) due 11 November

Quarter 2: (October - December) due 28 February 

Quarter 3: (January - March) due 12 May

Quarter 4: (April - June) due 11 August.

For more details, refer to Two week lodgment concession - terms and conditions
Opens in a new window
 on ATO website.

Re-forecasting of lodgment and payment due dates

The activity statement lodgment and payment due dates will be re-forecasted to the 4 week electronic agent concession:

if an active agent link is subsequently added in ICP prior to lodgment at either the taxpayer or ICA level, and

the electronic activity statement outbound preference remains.

The re-forecasted due dates will be displayed in:

ICP within 3 weeks

IPS after the activity statement is lodged and processed.

Head company of a consolidated group

The head company of a consolidated group will:

receive a consolidated activity statement to report quarterly PAYG instalment, and

will continue to receive an activity statement for any obligations they have.

The lodgment date will be the 21st day of the month following the end of the cycle.

Taxpayer cannot pay on time

Even if a taxpayer cannot pay their activity statement on time, they still must lodge it by the due date. General interest charge will apply to any amount not paid by the due date. Failure to lodge a document by the due date may result in a penalty ranging from 1 to 25 penalty units, depending on:

the entity size, and

how long the document is overdue.

ICP-Add a Return Not Necessary
ICP-Add a Return Not Necessary

Actions to update roles to have return not necessary (RNN).

Last published 5 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides the procedure to add a Return Not Necessary (RNN) in ICP for:

income tax (IT) returns

fringe benefits tax (FBT) returns, and

Taxable Payments Annual Reports (TPAR).

Trust beneficiary account returns are not considered an expected lodgment. Refer to Add Income tax - trust beneficiary account and role.
Opens in a new window

Recent updates

Update in 'Add an RNN for a not for profit organisation'

	

SD-100087

Added 'Add an RNN for a not for profit organisation'.

 if required.

Opens in a new window

Lockdown suppression

If a lockdown suppression exists on their account, an RNN cannot be input. Advise the taxpayer to complete a non-lodgment advice paper form.
