Tax Practitioner Lodgment Program
Lodgment program OSfA deferral instruction
Lodgment program OSfA deferral instruction

Instructions for Frontline Services (FS) staff on lodgment program deferrals submitted via OSfA deferral function.

Last published 28 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page instructs Frontline Services (FS) staff how to action registered tax or BAS agent requests for a deferral of the due date for:

lodgment, or
lodgment and payment.
Warning

For payment only deferrals, refer to Payment-only deferrals for registered agents.
Opens in a new window

Before you begin

This instruction is only to be used by FS and Outsource staff assigned to this work.

Lodgment program deferral requests are submitted via Online services for agents (OSfA).

Use the table below to determine your next action.

Activity classification for...
	Then...



registered agent deferral requests submitted via the deferral function in OSfA is:

Capability: Compliance

Category: Intermediaries

Type: Agent deferral
	continue below and ensure you understand the information under the Scenarios section.


request for review of a lodgment program deferral decision is:

Capability: Compliance

Category: Intermediaries

Type: Review of deferral decision
	refer to Lodgment program deferral review of decision.
Opens in a new window
Recent updates
28/04/2026	Update to task 1.	SD-103913


17/04/2026

	

Change made in task 8 step 5.

	

SD-103613

Scenarios
What is a deferral

Where the circumstances of the taxpayer or the agent affects their ability to lodge by the due date, agents can request a:

deferral of the due date for

lodgment, or

lodgment and payment

suspension of lodgment compliance action (suspension).

A deferral:

extends the due date of obligations and provides taxpayers with more time to lodge, or to lodge and pay, without being penalised

may be requested due to circumstances impacting the agent, taxpayer, or both.

The ATO recognises unexpected issues can temporarily prevent a taxpayer or registered agent from meeting their obligations. A deferral provides a balance between the taxpayer's obligations and their circumstances.

A deferral extends the original due date of a lodgment and/or payment to a particular date in the future. If the taxpayer meets their obligations by the deferred due date, they will not receive any:

failure to lodge on time (FTL) penalties, or
general interest charge (GIC).

The extended time you allow is calculated from the day after the date that the document was originally due to be lodged. The original due date is either the:

legislative due date, or

concessional due date for

electronic lodgers, or

taxpayers represented by a registered agent under the lodgment program.

The ATO may vary or refuse any request. This includes refusing the request or approving part of the request (partial). Examples of a partial approval are:

approving a deferral for some but not all taxpayers

where the requested deferral timeframe is reduced, or

lodgment only is allowed when payment was also requested.

Where the circumstances are not appropriate to allow a deferral, a suspension can be considered.

Under legislation, the payment due date for an individual or trust income tax (IT) return is 3 weeks after the lodgment due date. If the lodgment due date is deferred, the payment due date will be automatically adjusted. There is no need to request or to allow a payment deferral to the lodgment deferral date for these taxpayers.

When considering a registered agent's lodgment and payment deferral, the payment deferral must be considered independently to the lodgment request.

Consider the following when making your decision on a payment request:

do the reasons demonstrate an impediment to paying by the due date?
some taxpayers are required to pay before lodging their return, go to the table in the Income Tax Return due dates
length of the deferral time requested. 
What is a suspension

A suspension is a decision to hold compliance action on an overdue lodgment until a future date. Unlike a deferral, a suspension does not change the actual due date of the lodgment obligation. FTL penalties and/or GIC can still accrue from the lodgment due date.

The ATO recognises taxpayers sometimes cannot meet their obligations on time. Where a taxpayer is not eligible for a deferral, the ATO may consider a suspension instead.

Lodgment program

The lodgment program:

recognises the important role registered agents play in helping taxpayers meet their tax and superannuation lodgment obligations
helps registered agents to manage their workload by allowing them to lodge obligations progressively over a 12-month period. For example, rather than having all income tax returns due on 31 October, taxpayers represented by a registered agent may have their income tax returns due on 15 January, 31 January, 28 February, 31 March or 15 May.

The Commissioner also provides a concessional due date of 5 June for eligible income tax returns with a due date of 15 May. Lodgment program due dates also apply to other obligations including:

fringe benefits tax (FBT) returns, and
quarterly business activity statements.

Agents who lodge quarterly activity statements electronically are also eligible for a 4-week lodgment and payment concession.

Information

When a due date falls on a Saturday, Sunday or any state or territory public holiday, lodgment and payment is due on the next business day.

For further information, refer to Prepare and lodge.
Opens in a new window

Income tax return due dates

Lodgment program due dates for income tax returns are not updated immediately in ATO systems on 1 July. It can take up to 3 weeks (from the beginning of July and from the end of October) for ATO systems to be progressively updated with the lodgment program due dates. We advise agents to wait until the end of July and the end of November to review the lodgment program due dates in:

OSfA, or
Practitioner lodgment service (PLS).

A taxpayer's income tax lodgment and payment due dates may change from year to year if their circumstances change. For example, a taxpayer who has had a due date of 15 May for previous income tax returns may have a due date of 31 March for the current year if there was an increase in the income and tax payable reported in their last lodged return compared to previous years.

To be eligible for lodgment program due dates for income tax returns, the taxpayers represented by the agent must, by 31 October, have:

an active taxpayer to agent link, and 
lodged all prior year returns.

These criteria must be met by 31 October even if 31 October falls on a Saturday, Sunday or any state or territory public holiday.

Lodgment due date
	Payment due date
	Applies to taxpayers

Individuals and trusts: refer to Payment of individual and trust income tax returns not due 15 May.

taxpayers who have been prosecuted for non-lodgment.

are not eligible for the 5 June concession.
Taxpayers not eligible for 15 May income tax return due date

Taxpayers will not be eligible for the 15 May lodgment program due date for their income tax return if they are required to lodge by an earlier due date, that is:

31 October (refer to reasons detailed in the table above)
15 January, 31 January, 28 February or 31 March due to their entity type or their income and tax payable in their prior year return.
Payment of individual and trust income tax returns not due 15 May
If the tax return is...
	Then...



lodged on or before the lodgment due date

	

payment will be due on the later of 21 days after the:

relevant lodgment due date, or
notice of assessment is deemed received (which is 3 days after issue).



lodged late

	

any tax is payable 21 days after the due date for lodgment.




not lodged at all, and a default assessment is issued

	

any tax is payable 21 days after the due date for lodgment.

Staggered payment dates for 15 May income tax returns 

Staggered payment date arrangements exist for individuals and trusts whose tax returns have a lodgment due date of 15 May. These are the latest possible due dates for payment.

If the income tax return is lodged...
	Then the payment is due no later than...



up to and including 12 February

	

21 March or next business day.




from 13 February to 12 March (inclusive)

	

21 April or next business day.




from 13 March

	

5 June or next business day.

5 June concessional date for 15 May income tax returns

A concessional arrangement applies under the lodgment program for some income tax returns with a lodgment program due date of 15 May. The concessional arrangement provides for the income tax return to be lodged by 5 June without penalty for: 

individuals or trusts when payment is also made by 5 June 

companies and super funds where all of the following eligibility criteria are met

they are not a large and medium taxpayer or a head company of a consolidated group
the latest return lodged was non-taxable or a credit assessment
the current year return is non-taxable or receiving a credit assessment.

The 5 June concession is not applied in ATO systems until lodgment of a current year return that meets the eligibility criteria.

Information

Agents:

do not need to apply for a deferral to receive the concession date. 
cannot request a deferral from the 5 June concession date as it is not a due date under the lodgment program.
Entities with substituted accounting period

The ATO can grant entities a substituted accounting period (SAP). These entities are not 30 June balancers and must meet the specific lodgment requirements.

The below due dates apply to all entities with a SAP and apply equally to a self-preparer and those represented by a tax agent.

Entity type
	Lodgment due date
	Payment due date

Last day of 4th month

Last day of 4th month

	

As per NOA

Last day of 4th month

	

Not applicable

1st day of 6th month

	

As per NOA




Super funds

	

15th day of 7th month

	

1st day of 6th month




SMSFs

	

15th day of 7th month

	

1st day of 6th month

Due dates are after the balancing period ends. For example, a March balancing has a lodgment due date of 31 July (last day of 4th month after 31 March).
Early December balancing companies, super funds and SMSFs may be entitled to a concessional date for lodgment of 31 July (refer below).
The lodgment concession available to SAP entities does not apply where the SAP entity is not a full self-assessment taxpayer. 
Concession for early December balancer SAP

A lodgment concession is available to 31 July for early balancing December SAP entities if they lodge electronically.

A full self-assessment company or super fund that is an early December balancer must lodge a tax return no later than the 15th day of the seventh month after the end of their year of income. The due date for lodgment for these entities is 15 July.

However, we will grant a lodgment concession to 31 July if the tax agent lodges the tax return electronically by 31 July.

This is a lodgment concession only. Payment, if required is still due on the first day of the sixth month after their year of income, which is 1 June.

Consolidated groups under an SAP

Head companies of consolidated groups that operate under an approved SAP must lodge and pay (where applicable) following the SAP rules.

FBT return due dates

The statutory due date for lodgment and payment is 21 May. The due dates for lodgment of FBT returns for all tax agents are:

25 June if the return is lodged via PLS
21 May if the return is lodged by paper.
Submitting deferral request
Eligible obligations

Agents can request a lodgment program deferral for:

income tax returns

FBT returns

monthly and quarterly activity statements

annual GST returns

PAYG withholding annual documents (also known as PAYG payment summary annual reports)

taxable payments annual reports (TPAR).

How to submit

Lodgment program deferrals
Opens in a new window
 provides information to the agent on submitting a request for a deferral.

Agents are only able to submit deferral requests using the lodgment deferral function
Opens in a new window
 in OSfA. The function provides agents with:

a single entry point to request all deferral types

the ability to request deferrals for up to 40 TFNs in one request
the ability to view the status of the deferral request in OSfA

where the request meets low-risk guidelines

processing via the OSfA deferral function within 48 hours

deferred dates updated in ICP within 48 hours

visibility of the request and the outcome in OSfA practice mailbox.

Requests that do not meet low-risk guidelines are escalated to FS for manual assessment following Making a decision.

Activity classification

Agent deferral requests are recorded in the Siebel activities in the table below.

Go to Payment only deferrals for registered agents.
Opens in a new window

Requests for payment only deferrals are not processed using this procedure.

Making a decision

The ATO considers tax practitioners as trusted advisors, therefore the information they provide is taken in good faith. You do not need to call agents to validate the information they have provided.

Use the following information when a deferral request requires a decision based on the circumstances provided on whether to allow the request. These deferral requests would include requests for:

obligations that do not meet the low-risk guidelines

prior year periods (you can only allow a suspension)

previous deferral allowed.

When considering an agent deferral request that includes payment, you should consider if the reasons demonstrate an impediment to making payment.

You should consider if the reasons or circumstances justify the timeframe requested. If not, it may be appropriate to allow a partial deferral for a shorter timeframe.

You should review and understand the reasons and timeframes in the agent's request and decide either to allow:

the request in full 

a partial deferral, or

a suspension.

Refer to your support staff for advice if you are not sure.

For guidance to support your decision, refer to:

PSLA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals

PSLA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles.

The Commissioner will generally allow a deferral where:

exceptional or unforeseen circumstances have been provided, and

it is considered fair and reasonable to do so after reviewing all the facts including

the reasons provided
length of time requested, and
if previous deferrals have been approved for the same obligation or period.
If the request is...
	Then...

received early (more than 3 months before the original lodgment due date)	

early requests generally do not meet the unforeseen circumstances criteria. The request should be discussed with your support officer. Refuse the request.




to align the lodgment due date with other entities with a later due date

	

aligning an entity's lodgment due date with other entities is not considered reason for a deferral.

The lodgment program provides staggered dates to help agents to manage their practice with progressive lodgments throughout the year.




to request an income tax lodgment due date to 15 May from:

31 October

15 January 

31 January

28 February, or

31 March

	

consider granting a partial deferral to a date prior to 15 May if there are extenuating circumstances. Otherwise, refuse the request.

Taxpayers' lodgment due dates are specific to their lodgment circumstances when linked to an agent.

To receive the 15 May lodgment program due date, agents must meet specific conditions.

Taxpayers receive the 31 October due date when:

all prior year returns have not been lodged before 31 October, or
they are not linked to the agent by this date.

The 15 January, 31 January, 28 February and 31 March lodgment program due dates apply to taxpayers who meet specific criteria. This is based on entity type and prior year income and tax payable. These taxpayers are not eligible for the 15 May lodgment program due date.




submitted after the lodgment due date

	

requests for deferral by agents should generally be submitted before the lodgment due date
Opens in a new window
 in the lodgment program.

A request submitted after the due date may still be actioned based on the circumstances of the taxpayer or agent.

You should review the request based on the reasons provided and deferral period requested.

You may need to obtain reasons why the request was late, if relevant to your decision.

Supported lodgment program

A supported lodgment program
Opens in a new window
 can help:

registered agents if they experience difficulties managing their lodgment program
when unforseen circumstances affect their whole practice.

We work with the agent to tailor solutions to help them get their lodgment program back on track. Agents can request this support by sending a message through Practice mail in OSfA.

The Tax practitioner lodgment program team in Individuals and Intermediaries (IAI) process SLP requests.

Task 4 - Authorisation

Check if the deferral request for lodgment and payment is outside of your authorisation.
Opens in a new window
 If approval is:

required, go to task 5

not required, go to task 6.

Warning

You should not refuse or reduce a deferral to a shorter time because it exceeds your authority.

Do not advise agents of staff delegations or authorisation limits.

Authorisation levels

Your authority to allow a deferral begins the day after the date the document was originally due to be lodged, even if the agent is making the request after the due date. 

If the deferral request is for multiple documents, each document may require different authorisation levels. Check if you have the authorisation to allow a deferral for every document in the request. If no, the entire request must be escalated to the appropriate level. The escalation includes deferral of documents within your authorisation. This is to ensure consistency of decision making.

The period of deferral is calculated from the day after the date an obligation was originally due.

Deferral type
1.8.1.

Deferral or extension of time to lodge any obligation excluding:

activity statements (AS)
annual GST returns
annual PAYG income tax withholding (ITW) stationery
consolidation choices.

The authorisation period is calculated from the day after the date the document was originally due to be lodged.

High Impact and Agent deferrals
Lodgment program deferrals - for sophisticated taxpayers
Lodgment program deferrals - for sophisticated taxpayers

Instructions for Firmer and Legal Recovery staff actioning deferrals submitted by registered agents.

Last published 2 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page instructs Firmer and Legal Recovery staff on how to action deferral requests from registered agents submitted via online services for agents (OSfA).

Information
alert:

Information

If you are actioning a request, escalated from Frontline, refer to Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Recent updates
02/12/2025 	Update made to task 4.	SD-94954
Scenarios
Registered agent requests 

Registered agents can submit requests via OSfA for deferral of:

lodgment only or lodgment and payment, using the lodgment deferral function
Opens in a new window
payment only, by completing Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents.
Opens in a new window

Requests submitted via OSfA deferral function for Significant Global Entities (SGEs) and Large withholders will be delivered as:

Capability: Compliance
Category: Intermediaries
Type: Agent Deferral Complex
Interaction: Tax Agent Portal. 

Payment only deferral requests submitted via OSfA on the Payment deferral application
Opens in a new window
 form where at least one of the taxpayers listed is a sophisticated taxpayer, will be escalated to FC Firmer and Legal Recovery for actioning. These activities will be delivered as:

Capability: Debt and Lodgment
Category: Debt - Specific
Type: High Impact Deferrals
Interaction: Tax Agent Portal.

Information alert:

Information

If the request is for one taxpayer only, the taxpayer details will already be added. If the request is for multiple taxpayers, staff will be required to action all taxpayers listed.

Eligibility

It is generally considered fair and reasonable to grant a deferral where the inability to lodge and/or pay by the due date is reasonably attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances. These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension) or payment plan.

For more information on considering deferrals of lodgment/payment and suspension, refer to: 

PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals
Deferral Suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Information
alert:

Information

Where the taxpayer is affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case. 

Exclusions

Deferral requests cannot be offered for the following:

a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF), contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination
taxpayers awaiting outcome of class ruling, private ruling, guidance request
are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates
can either lodge their returns

based on the current ATO view of the law, or
how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)
Excess contributions tax and Division 293.

Information
alert:

Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit. For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 
concessional due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment
Opens in a new window
registered agents
Opens in a new window

Information
alert:

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, it must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Low value activity guidelines

The following are not considered low risk:

deferral requests made by Sophisticated taxpayers, where low value activity guidelines criteria have already been applied to one request within the last 12 months
SGE
Large PAYG Withholders and Large Taxpayers
PG Top 100 or PW Top 500 Taxpayers.

Payment-only Deferrals for Registered agents
Payment-only Deferrals for Registered agents

D-1 - Procedure to action payment-only deferral requests received through Online Services for Agents (OSfA).

Last published 24 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1

This page:

provides the procedure to advise Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist staff on how to action registered tax or BAS agent payment-only deferral requests received through Online Services for Agents (OSfA)
is only to be used by staff assigned to this work.

Agents may request a deferral where the taxpayer's circumstances affect their ability to pay by the due date.

Where the circumstances are not appropriate to grant a deferral, you must consider negotiating a payment plan.

Use the table below to decide the relevant procedure to follow.

For...
	Then...



requests for payment-only deferral excluding:

High Impact Deferrals
form type Combined Global and Domestic Minimum Tax Returns (CGDMTR).
	

continue with this procedure.




Frontline Compliance staff skilled in Agent Deferrals

	

go to Lodgment program deferrals - for sophisticated taxpayers.
Opens in a new window




OSfA requests for:

lodgment deferral
lodgment and payment deferral
suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Excluding form type:

CGDMTR
OSfA request for CGDMTR or payment deferral
	

go to

Lodgment program deferral instruction - OSfA function
Opens in a new window
CGDMTR deferral or suspension request.
Opens in a new window

OSfA request for CGDMTR lodgment or payment deferral
	go to CGDMTR deferral or suspension request.
Opens in a new window




all other deferral requests received outside OSfA

	

go to Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Submitting a deferral request
Criterion
	Requirement



Circumstances supporting the request

	

Agents can lodge a payment-only deferral application if:

there are exceptional or unforeseen circumstances beyond their taxpayer's control, and

the exceptional or unforeseen circumstances are consistent with those outlined in law administration practice statement PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 General debt collection powers and principles.




Required information

	

For the request to be considered, the agent must:

provide

the requested payment deferral date

full details of their taxpayer's circumstances that have or will prevent them from paying by the due date

the steps the taxpayer took to mitigate the effects of those circumstances

details of any payment arrangement relating to the debt (if applicable)

demonstrate that the taxpayer

can make full payment once the circumstances have been resolved

will pay their continuing tax-related liabilities on time, once the circumstances are under control.

Go to Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.




How to submit the request

	

Agent must submit their request via OSfA on the Payment deferral application form.
Opens in a new window




Eligible debts

	

The Payment-only Deferrals for Registered Agents instruction allows the agent to request a payment-only deferral for the following debts if the relevant document is lodged:

Income Tax

Liabilities arising following lodgment of monthly and quarterly activity statements

Fringe benefits tax (FBT).




Other debts

	

The Payment deferral application form does not provide the option to request a payment deferral for other debts including:

GIC/FTL/SIC relating to eligible documents

Super Guarantee Charge

Excise

Excess Contribution tax

Div293

Jobkeeper overpayments

Judgment interest or costs.

Do not use this procedure to process payment-only deferral requests for other debts. Refer to the relevant procedure to process those requests.

Checks
When making a decision
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted for an existing liability based on a future credit including lodging an amendment. For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

The Commissioner will generally allow a deferral where exceptional or unforeseen circumstances have been provided and it is considered fair and reasonable to do so having considered all the material facts.

Payment deferral:

requests are considered high risk and should only be allowed where the circumstances are consistent with those outlined in paragraphs 32-42 of PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
are generally a short-term measure to provide immediate assistance in extenuating circumstances.

If payment is unlikely to be made in full by the deferred due date, a tailored payment arrangement may be more appropriate.

Where you need additional information to make your decision, contact the agent.

Deferral request cannot be offered for:

taxpayers are expected to lodge and pay their returns by the due dates

they can either lodge and pay their returns

how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)

an existing liability based on a future credit posting, including lodging an amendment. For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit to cover the existing debts and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Referred to ECA

Category: Collection negotiation
Type: Referred to Mercantile Agent 2
Interaction: Internal - case for routing
Description: Recoveries Corp
Status: UnAllocated

Taxpayer tab, go to the Taxpayer Indicator
Identify whether there is an active External Collection Agency indicator

If you have identified an active case or ECA indicator in ECP, this means the taxpayer is still with the ECA.

pay their continuing tax-related liabilities on time

Procedure Steps
Task 1 - Previous request

Check ICP - View deferral
Opens in a new window
 and notes for previous deferral requests. Use this information to guide your decision.

Consider whether special consideration for ADF personnel and taxpayers affected by war
Opens in a new window
 applies.

Note if agent contact is required.

Warning

Do not grant another deferral for the same period if the taxpayer's previous request was:

allowed, unless the taxpayer's circumstances warrant a further extension

disallowed, unless the taxpayer has provided further information that warrants a review of the original decision.

Task 2 - Review information

Review the deferral request and all attachments. Determine if additional information is required to make the decision for all listed taxpayers.

For the payment-only deferral request to be considered, the taxpayer must:

be, or have been, experiencing extenuating or unforeseen circumstances beyond their control that reasonably contributed to the delay in payment which affects or affected their ability to pay by the original due date. For example

death or serious illness of person responsible for paying where there is no other person that can, or could, make the payment
inability to access funds because of a legal impediment (e.g. probate not being granted or access to funds being denied by the order of a Court)
entity or person responsible for payment had funds embezzled by their tax agent, solicitor or other third party
natural disaster, such as flood, fire, drought, earthquake and the like
other disasters that have, or may have, had a significant impact on the person responsible for payment
where appropriate, be able to demonstrate steps were taken by the taxpayer to mitigate the effects of those circumstances
be able to demonstrate the ability to
make full payment once the circumstances have been resolved
pay their continuing tax-related liabilities on time.

Reference: PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 General debt collection powers and principles.

Information

We must consider payment-only deferral requests relating to instalments only (PAYGI or GSTI). However, when speaking with the agent requesting a payment deferral for an instalment liability, provide education about varying instalments prior to falling due. Go to How to vary your PAYG instalments
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.

If the agent wants to proceed with the deferral, consider the request.

Agent contact may be required to:

obtain more information to make a decision

if not granted, and where appropriate, have a payment conversation and consider

Negotiating payment options
Opens in a new window

education about Tax support for individuals, businesses, not-for-profits and tax professionals.
Opens in a new window

Information

Example: A taxpayer did not meet the deferral requirement and was not eligible for a payment deferral. However, the reason taxpayer provided was that they are selling an asset and can pay the debt in full within 6 months.

You should call to negotiate an appropriate payment plan.

	Action

 and select the appropriate outbound message. If: there is not an existing approved message to explain the decisions to the agent:

Determine if there is more than one outcome. If:

yes

return to step 3, and

include all message outcomes in the secure message reply
no, go to step 5.

 5	

Ensure you check for any errors, before issuing the outbound correspondence. These include:

incorrect names
spelling errors
punctuation errors
inaccurate information
content that does not make sense
formatting (spacing, alignment, font colours and chevrons not removed). 

6 	

Send the message:

select Send Correspondence
the Portal Channel Delivery pop-up box will appear. Type the following text into the Subject field. For
granting, refusing or partially granting the request: 'Payment-only deferral - Attn <agent name as per transmission form> - Receipt ID <receipt ID from the original activity>'
request for more information: 'URGENT response required – Payment-only deferral - Attn <agent name as per transmission form > - Receipt ID <receipt ID from the original activity>'.

Do not finalise the activity. The status of the activity will:

remain as 'In Progress' until the message is sent
automatically set to 'Done' when the message is sent. 

7 	

Check if the message transmission was successful following task 4 of Siebel - reply via ATO online services.
Opens in a new window
Then return to this procedure.

Task 7 - Add Siebel note

Add a note
Opens in a new window
 using the Notes fly-out tab with the relevant note template from the below scenario table.

Warning

For Outsource staff (not providing the 13 11 69 call back option), you must include in your Siebel note: 'Unable to leave a direct contact number.'

Siebel - initiate a secure message via ATO online services
Siebel - initiate a secure message via ATO online services

Procedure on how to initiate a secure message to a taxpayer or registered agent.

Last published 8 September 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page shows how to send a secure message via Siebel to a:

registered agent's practice mail in Online services for agents (OSfA), or
taxpayer's secure mail in Online services for business (OSB).
This applies when you need to: 

initiate a secure message where no prior inbound activity exists

respond to an inbound activity that is not an inbound online services message. For example, the inbound activity was the result of a phone call or letter.

Recent updates
08/09/2025	Updated 'taxpayer' to 'taxpayer' where applicable throughout the page.	SD-96753
Checks
Check inbound activity
If...	Then...


responding to a request for copies of tax documents

	

the request is:

from a Legal Practitioner

go to Legal practitioner requests for COTD Reply via ATOO
Opens in a new window

this is the end of the procedure

not from a Legal Practitioner

go to COTD main - Reply via ATOO
Opens in a new window

this is the end of the procedure.




responding to a lost super search enquiry

	

go to Lost super search enquiry - online services reply.
Opens in a new window

This is the end of the procedure.




the Interaction of the inbound activity is Tax agent Portal or Business Portal

	
if actioning an OSfA deferral activity with a Type: Agent deferral, go to Lodgment program OSfA deferral instructions
Opens in a new window
for all other requests

go to Siebel - reply via ATO online services
Opens in a new window
 
this is the end of the procedure.



the Interaction of the inbound activity is not Tax agent Portal or Business Portal, or

you do not have an inbound activity

	

 go to task 1.

Procedure Steps
Task 1 - Create outbound activity

ATO initiated secure messages from Siebel are sent from either the:

relevant agent's Tax Practitioner Consolidated View (TPCV), or
taxpayer's Taxpayer's Consolidated Taxpayer View (CCV).
Use the table to determine your action.
If...
	Then...

initiating an interaction, and
there has been no previous request relating to your correspondence
	

go to step 1




responding to a request received by a channel other than online services (e.g. letter, phone call)

 
	

if you are:

sending a message to an agent

go to the taxpayer's ICP account and view Taxpayer links

confirm the taxpayer has an active Taxpayer to Tax Agent link to the agent at the Taxpayer, Account or Role level

confirm the registered agent's unique identifier in the Tax Agent Number (TAN) field

go to step 2

not sending a message to an agent, go to step 2. 



Step	Action


1

	

Conduct a Siebel search
Opens in a new window
 for the relevant registered agent or taxpayer record.




2

	

Create a new activity on the relevant TPCV or CCV.

Where you have a related activity, link by a Service Request
Opens in a new window
 (SR).




3

	

Classify the new activity
Opens in a new window
 with the below values:

Capability: as appropriate

Category: as appropriate

Type: as appropriate

Interaction: Outbound Correspondence

Case ID/Project ID: leave blank

TAN: If sending a message to a registered agent via OSfA, enter the relevant registered agent number, otherwise leave blank.

Information
alert:
If you are sending correspondence using OSfA, the TAN field must be populated or the correspondence will not send.

Adding the TAN will change ownership of the activity from the taxpayer to the registered agent.

You may need to confirm the correct registered agent number if there are multiple available.

Task 2 - Construct message

Information
alert:
If all details are not visible, hover over the two arrows in the top-right corner of the screen.

This displays the Show More button in the Activities applet.

Step	Action


1

	

Update the Destination Channel to the correct online services channel, such as:

TAP for OSfA, or

BP for OSB.

There are two Destination Channel fields in Siebel. These are viewable under the:

Correspondence heading in the main section of the Siebel activity, or

Correspondence tab.

Only update one of these fields. The other will auto populate when the update is saved.




2

	

Go to the Unstructured Mail Message field.




3

	

Type the required message. Refer to:

your business procedures for approved standard responses, where applicable

Siebel - secure message guidelines and principles
Opens in a new window
 for examples and message protocols, including guidelines for

gender neutral salutations

signatures, and

attachments.

Before sending, check the message for:

incorrect taxpayer or agent name

spelling errors

punctuation errors

inaccurate information

content that does not make sense

formatting issues (spacing, alignment, font colours)

chevrons < > indicating variable text (these should be removed).




4

	

Save the record.




5

	

Determine if an attachment is required. If:

yes, go to task 3

no, go to task 4.

Task 3 - Add an attachment
Step	Action
1	

Ensure the attachment to be added has been checked by a peer for any errors and is corrected where required. Errors may include:

incorrect taxpayer or agent name

spelling errors

punctuation errors

inaccurate information

content that does not make sense

formatting issues (spacing, alignment, font colours)

chevrons < > indicating variable text (these should be removed).




2

	

Attach and publish
Opens in a new window
 all attachments required for the message.

You must ensure the correct attachment is attached to the activity to restrict privacy breaches.

All read only attachments are to be sent in PDF format. There is a limit of six attachments per message at 6MB per attachment.




3

	

Highlight the row containing the new attachment in the Attachments list.




4

	

Select Publish.

Siebel will publish the selected attachment. The Document Id field in the Attachments list will populate with the new unique Document ID.

If the Document ID field has not automatically populated within a few minutes, refresh the screen.




5

	

Save the record.

Task 4 - Send the secure message
Step	Action


1

	

Select Send Correspondence in the Activity Details view.

Information
alert:
The Portal Channel Delivery screen will be displayed. If this does not display, do not attempt to send the message again. Siebel will not correctly send the correspondence.




2

	

Determine if the required mailbox address is available from the drop-down menu at the Taxpayer mailbox field.

For agents, the mailbox will default to the general practice mailbox. If:

sending the message to the individual agent's mailbox address, type the surname first into the Search field, then select the correct mailbox address
the agent does not have an individual mailbox address, select the default general practice mailbox and advise the agent the response will be sent to that mailbox address.

If:

the required mailbox address is available, go to step 3

no mailbox address is populated, it is advisable to confirm with the agent or taxpayer that they use online services. If

yes, enter PRACTICE MAILBOX or BUSINESS MAILBOX in the Taxpayer mailbox field as applicable

no, go to step 5. The taxpayer or agent does not have an established mailbox address and cannot receive messages by online services.




3

	

Enter a subject heading in the Subject field, then select the OK button.

The secure message is sent to online services. The status of the activity will:

remain as In Progress until the message is transmitted

automatically set to Done when the message is transmitted.

To verify the message has been sent, manually refresh the activity and check the activity has changed to Done.

If the transmission is unsuccessful, the status of the activity will remain as In Progress.


4	

Check if the message transmission was successful. If related receipt ID (RRI) starts with 

10, and receipt ID is 
different to RRI then outcome is failed 
same as RRI then outcome is successful 
50 and receipt ID starts with 10 then outcome is successful.

You can also access the staff view of OSfA or OSB to confirm if the secure message appears in the relevant inbox as follows

go to ATOO for the agent or taxpayer
view the relevant inbox to confirm the secure message has been received.

If: 

yes, this is the end of the procedure
no, and the issue 
can be resolved,
take corrective action, then 
this is the end of the procedure 
cannot be resolved,
record a note
Opens in a new window
 to advise the transmission was unsuccessful, and 
go to step 5.



5

	

Source an alternative way to send the message to the agent or taxpayer. The activity must be finalised by:

recording a relevant narration in the Comments field

updating the Status to Created in error.

Refer to business procedures to finalise the correspondence item.

ADF personnel and affected by war enquiries

Information to assist ADF personnel and immediate family including guidance for calls.

Last published 12 November 2024

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides information to assist:

Defence Force (ADF) Personnel and immediate family members when the taxpayer is on deployment, or

other taxpayers impacted by war.

Recent Updates

15/03/2021

	

Content updated to new page layout.

	

SD-31513

In Detail

Refer to the scenarios below to determine the action required.

ADF (and related) on deployment

Assistance is available to Defence Force Personnel (DFP) deployed overseas and their spouse or partner, if requested. You can also assist the following people:

spouse

partner, or

immediate family member.

If a spouse, partner or immediate family member make contact, but are not authorised on the account, PORO
Opens in a new window
 procedures must be applied as though the caller is listed, and a note
Opens in a new window
 advising of the contact should be recorded on both the caller and the taxpayer's account.

Details can be accepted from the non-authorised contact, however taxpayer details cannot be supplied to any party that is not the relevant taxpayer or authorised contact.

Requests from a senior officials of the Australian Defence Force are escalated by email to LodgmentProgram@ato.gov.au.

Warning

Under no circumstances should contact be made with the Australian Defence Force unless the enquiry originates from one of their bases.

Assistance available

Defence Force Personnel (DFP) who are eligible for assistance may receive more time to lodge and pay without penalties or interest.

An income tax return lodgment deferral can be granted from the time active deployment began, until the Defence Force member returns from duty. Where a return date is unknown, deferrals should be granted for a period of up to five months. Should the Defence Force member not return after this period has lapsed, a further deferral may be granted.

Where an income tax return has been lodged and a debit assessment issues after the deployment, a payment deferral can be granted if requested.

Any existing arrangements can be re-negotiated. Interest and penalties imposed during this period can be remitted. 

Activity statements

Each request will be considered on its merits, in accordance with PS LA 2011/15,
Opens in a new window
 with a view to being fair, reasonable and appropriate.

Generally, where no other person or representative is able to meet the lodgment obligations, a lodgment deferral can be granted.

Taxpayers affected by war

In situations of war, other taxpayers may also be adversely affected. These include those who are detained in the region because of work or capture, or those who are suffering financial trading difficulties because their business has been directly affected.

Each request must be considered on its merits in accordance with PS LA 2011/15.

Concessional treatment does not apply to taxpayers whose claims are general in nature (e.g. general economic downturn or fluctuations of currency exchange rates caused by a war). These factors are more likely to affect the whole community. Taxpayers must demonstrate the war specifically affected their ability to lodge and/or pay.

Requests from a spouse or partner of a taxpayer who is detained overseas or adversely affected by war, (for themselves) must be considered in accordance with PS LA 2011/15 and the merits of the case involved.

Each case must be considered individually, taking into account the extent of the impacts of the war, particular circumstances of the taxpayer and PS LA 2011/15.
Opens in a new window

Assistance available

Those eligible for assistance may receive more time to lodge and pay without penalties or interest.

Generally, taxpayers located in Australia will not receive concessional treatment, as it is expected that they should be able to maintain their lodgment obligations.

If a taxpayer has been detained overseas and no other person or representative is able to address their income tax lodgment obligations, a lodgment deferral can be granted until the affected taxpayer returns. Where a return date is unknown, deferrals should be granted for a period of up to five months. Should the affected taxpayer not return after this period has lapsed, a further deferral may be granted.

Where an income tax return has been lodged, and a debit assessment issues after the detainment, a payment deferral can be granted if requested.

An existing arrangement can be re-negotiated. Penalties and interest imposed during this period can be remitted.

Activity statements

Generally, taxpayers located in Australia will not receive concessional treatment as it is expected that they should be able to maintain their activity statement lodgment obligations.

If a taxpayer who is integral to a business has been detained overseas and no other person or representative is able to meet the obligations, a lodgment deferral can be granted. Interest and penalties imposed during this period can be remitted.

Concessional treatment may be sought by business owners who are financially affected by war. In these cases, a payment deferral can be granted and penalties remitted, where it can be shown that the business has been directly affected by war. Examples include:

Trading receipts not received from a trading partner in affected region

Goods in transit damaged by acts of war and not covered by insurance, or

Receipts not received due to a banking system collapse

Action the call
Information

Action up to two requests only.

If the taxpayer has...	Then...


two requests or less

	

go to Deferrals and Suspensions.
Opens in a new window




three or more requests

	

the taxpayer must submit a written request by mail or fax to Operations - Correspondence.
Opens in a new window

Low-risk criteria

This page will assist staff to determine eligibility for low-risk GIC remission requests and lodgment deferrals.

Last published 29 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page outlines the criteria for low-risk general interest charge (GIC) remissions and lodgment deferrals. Each request type has specific criteria which must be met for the request to be considered favourably.

For more information refer to:

PS LA 2011/12
Opens in a new window
 - Remission of General Interest Charge
PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals
Recent updates
29/04/2026	Minor update to 'Lodgment deferrals'.	

SD-104224

Checks
Operation Protego

Operation Protego activities impact the taxpayer advice and how they are treated. You must check if the taxpayer is identified for Operation Protego activities before proceeding.

Check for a 2022 Operation Protego Siebel Alert. If:

yes, go to Operation Protego Conversation Support Guide,
Opens in a new window
 to take the appropriate action
no, continue.
In Detail
GIC remissions
Warning Alert
Warning
The Telephony GIC remission application form
Opens in a new window
 must be completed and attached to the Siebel activity, for all telephony GIC remissions.

The intent of this criteria is to provide a simpler and quicker GIC remission process for taxpayers that are normally good compliers but have had a small one off payment issue where $2,500 or less in GIC was accrued.

Info Alert
Information
failure to lodge (FTL) remission is not eligible for low-risk remission
there are no partial remissions under this approach
trust Beneficiary returns are not considered an outstanding lodgment.
A low-risk remission should be applied where the taxpayer meets all 5 criteria below:
The total GIC remission request is for $2,500 or less.
The GIC is on the Integrated Client Account (ICA), Income tax account (IT) or fringe benefits account (FBT).
There are no overdue lodgments.
The total combined outstanding account balance (excluding amounts not yet due) is $2,500 or less.
No previous GIC remission requests have been granted in the last 12 months on the IT, ICA and FBT accounts (excludes auto remissions).

The following are not considered low-risk:

Superannuation Guarantee Charge (SGC) remissions. Due to the possible financial disadvantage to employees, a request to remit the GIC on SGC will generally be refused. The Commissioner may remit the GIC under limited circumstances.
FTL penalty remission requests
Operation Protego GIC remission requests because they have demonstrated a poor compliance. These taxpayers and can be identified by an Operation Protego Siebel alert.
Remission decision

If the taxpayer meets all the low-risk criteria, you can grant GIC remission of $2,500 or less (combined total - not per account posting).

Where the taxpayers request does not meet the low-risk criteria: 

the request for remission should not be refused purely on that basis
continue to consider the remission request in accordance with PS LA 2011/12
External Link
 - Remission of General Interest Charge.

When a remission request is granted, advise the taxpayer verbally. If:

the remission request was received via Online services, reply via Online services
Opens in a new window
you consider written confirmation is needed, notify the taxpayer of the outcome in writing.
Lodgment deferrals
Info Alert
Information

For deferral requests from registered agents via telephony or submitted via Online services for agents (OSfA), do not follow this procedure.

Refer to Registered agent requests.
Opens in a new window

Compliance history

A taxpayer is considered to have a low-risk compliance history for lodgment deferrals if they meet the following criteria:

2 or fewer instances of previous FTL penalty remission within the last 12 months (go to ECP
Opens in a new window
 and go to Failure to Lodge Penalty & Remission History Summary gadget)
3 or fewer instances of FTL penalties incurred within the last 12 months (multiple penalties incurred at the same time are considered one instance)
no outstanding compliance activities - audit, CVC or Lodge and Pay non-lodgment work.
Active audit and enforcement cases

Check the Siebel Case Summary screen for an:

active Audit and Enforcement case - sub type Obligation Enforcement, or
Investigate and Prosecute case - sub type Prosecution.

If there is an active case:

do not
negotiate lodgments or grant any lodgment deferrals or suspensions
issue an FTL penalty warning if there is an active Prosecution case
an FTL penalty warning can be given at any stage without affecting an Obligation Enforcement (lodgment) case
advise the taxpayer of any overdue income tax returns and activity statements
continue debt action as normal.

If the case is allocated to a primary owner:

advise the primary owner by email or phone that you have spoken to the taxpayer and what was discussed
if the taxpayer wants to discuss the lodgment issue
ask them to call 13 28 69, and
provide them with the primary owner's name.
Low-risk lodgment deferral

Check:

the request is being made on or before the statutory due date
the taxpayer has
a low-risk compliance history and deferral period is less than the maximum deferral period allowed. Lodgment deferrals are considered low-risk for taxpayer requests if the
income tax deferral is 3 months or less
monthly activity statement deferrals are 3 weeks or less
quarterly or annual activity statement deferrals are 5 weeks or less
provided a reason why they are not able to lodge by the due date and this is deemed fair and reasonable.

The following are not considered low-risk:

deferral requests made by Sophisticated taxpayers, where low-risk activity criteria have already been applied to one request within the last 12 months

Significant Global Entities

Large PAYG Withholders and Large Taxpayers

PG Top 100 or PW Top 500 Taxpayers

Sophisticated taxpayers who are Emerging Private or Medium Private. Follow these steps to check ECP:
Opens in a new window

select the Group icon on the toolbar

select the GroupId commencing with 'UHC' to open the Profiling Dashboard 

in the top right corner of the Profiling Dashboard navigator bar

select Experience

select PW - PW Profiling Dashboard

under Indicators look for

Population - PW

Emerging Private Group

Medium Private Group

Medium Business.

Info Alert
Information

A taxpayer is considered Sophisticated where the Taxpayer Experience segment in ECP is:

Public Multinational Business
Privately Owned and Wealthy Groups
APRA-Regulated Superannuation Fund, or
Not-For-Profit.
Deferral decision

Consider the taxpayer's circumstances

A lodgment deferral may be granted if it is fair and reasonable to do so after considering all the relevant circumstances.

For more information, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due date and deferrals.

Does the taxpayer meet the low-risk and good compliance history under these guidelines? If:

yes, grant the deferral
no
the request for deferral should not be refused purely on that basis
continue to consider the deferral request.
Opens in a new window

If a final notice or intent to prosecute letter for any lodgment obligation has issued in the last 24 months:

review the Siebel case notes to determine the lodgment case officer/team or prosecution officer/team
contact
the prosecutor at the first instance or
lodgment case officer located under Siebel Case tab
request instruction on how to proceed with the lodgment deferral request.  

Refer to Escalation checks for deferrals and suspensions
Opens in a new window
 - Obligation enforcement case.

When a deferral or suspension is granted, notification should be provided to the taxpayer, either verbally or in writing. Any decision made, whether the deferral request is granted or refused, must be a fair and reasonable outcome for the taxpayer.

Requests that do not meet the low-value guidelines can still be considered using Deferral and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Recording notes

For low-risk inbound activities, less detail is required compared to other inbound activities. The expectation is that notes will provide just enough information to justify the reason for your decision.

Use the Notes fly-out tab to record this note:

select the relevant Low-risk activity note template, and
edit/copy/paste as required.

For lodgment deferrals, the following details must be recorded in your note:

Type: This request is considered a low-risk activity
record the appropriate abbreviation to explain why it is low-risk based on the table below.
Legend	Abbreviation to be noted
Income tax deferral
	(ITDEF<3mths)

Monthly activity statement deferral
	(MASDEF<3weeks)

Quarterly activity statement deferral
	(QASDEF<5weeks)

Annual activity statement deferral
	(AASDEF<5weeks)


Refer to the Quality notes
Opens in a new window
 guidelines for more information.
