Deferrals and Suspensions
Deferral Suspension guideline
Deferral Suspension guideline

Information on due date deferral for lodgment and payments or suspension of enforcement action of an approved form.

Last published 3 March 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist staff with information about:

lodgment due date deferrals and associated payments (deferral), and
suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension) on an approved form or other prescribed form.
Warning

Do not use this page for payment only deferrals.

For the purpose of this guideline, the term:

agent refers to a registered tax agent and registered BAS agent

due date refers to either the end date assigned under the agent lodgment program or the due date for lodgment

Online services refers to

Online services for agents (OSfA)

Online services for business.

Recent updates

03/03/2026

	

Minor update to 'Circumstances beyond or not beyond taxpayer control'.

	

SD-102281

In Detail
What is a deferral or suspension

A deferral or suspension may be granted when a taxpayer needs additional time to:

lodge an approved form
Opens in a new window
 or other prescribed form, or
to pay the associated debts.

Generally, a deferral request should be made on or before the due date. A suspension is usually considered when the reasons given for additional time are not sufficient to allow a deferral.

Requests for additional time for lodgment and payment must be considered independently of each other. Staff actioning Lodgment program OSfA deferral instruction
Opens in a new window
 requests from agents may action payment deferrals when this forms part of the request.

Characteristics
Deferral	

The legislative basis for the Commissioner to defer the time for lodgment of approved forms is under subsection 388-55(1) of Schedule 1
Opens in a new window
 of the Taxation Administration Act 1953 (TAA).

The requirements of approved forms are set out under section 388-50 of Schedule 1
Opens in a new window
 to the TAA.

Commissioner's discretion to grant a deferral of time to lodge other prescribed forms is in the various provisions requiring the lodgment of prescribed forms.

The lodgment due date is moved to a future date (deferred due date).

Failure to lodge on time (FTL) penalties will not be applied if lodgment is met on or before the deferred due date. Where FTL penalty has been applied before a deferral is processed, the FTL penalty will need to be cancelled.

Lodgment deferral may not defer the associated payment due date. General interest charge (GIC) will still apply from the original due date unless a payment deferral is automatically or separately granted - see Payment obligations and the effect of lodgment deferrals.

For:

obligations in ICP, input to the taxpayer record by modifying the expected lodgment date

activity statements, a re-forecasted lodgment due date
Opens in a new window
 will not override a deferral that has already been input for a later due date.




Suspension

	

The ATO agrees not to take further compliance action on the overdue lodgment obligation until an agreed future date.

The lodgment due date of the obligation does not change.

FTL penalties and GIC continue to apply from the lodgment due date.

Generally considered when the reasons given are not sufficient to allow a deferral.

For obligations in ICP, input to the taxpayer record using assessed low risk (ALR) accounting treatments.

Lodgment program deferrals (agent deferrals)

Taxpayers that have a registered agent authorised to act on their behalf for their lodgment obligations may be included in their agent's Lodgment program. The Lodgment program is a concession for agents to have further time to lodge beyond the statutory due date. Refer to the current lodgment program
Opens in a new window
 to determine the applicable due date for the relevant form and entity type.

Agents can also request additional time beyond the Lodgment program concession due dates when circumstances for their taxpayer or themselves has impacted their ability to lodge or lodge and pay by the due date.

If an agent needs to request a deferral for a taxpayer, the taxpayer must appear on the agent's current taxpayer listing for the roles specified.

Deferral requests lodged in accordance with Lodgment program deferrals
Opens in a new window
 are generally considered low risk. The ATO has established a principle that Lodgment program deferral requests will be granted unless the request is considered high risk, based on a risk assessment.

Agents need to submit their deferral request using OSfA. Go to:

Lodgment program deferrals
Opens in a new window
Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents.
Opens in a new window
Considering a deferral or suspension request

Deferral/suspension requests should include the:

type of document and the year/period it relates to

taxpayer's details, including ABN or TFN

circumstances that prevent the taxpayer from lodging the approved form or other prescribed form by the due date

steps the taxpayer has taken to mitigate those circumstances

proposed date of 

lodgment 
payment.

Each request for a deferral or suspension should be considered on its own merits, taking into account:

whether there is other relevant ATO activity
Opens in a new window

the circumstances preventing the taxpayer from lodging and paying

the taxpayer's compliance history as a whole, that is, compliance with both lodgment and payment obligations

the length of additional time requested

the risk to revenue

the value of the information contained in the document

steps taken to mitigate the circumstances, e.g. to lessen the severity of the circumstances or enable future tax obligations to be met as they fall due

the size and structure of the entity, e.g. large businesses may be more likely to be able to overcome circumstances affecting their ability to lodge

the principles in relevant practice statements and other administrative guidelines that have been issued, for example practice notes

for suspensions only

the stage lodgment enforcement action has reached

the likelihood of the taxpayer lodging within the period

the taxpayer's contact with the ATO to date

any other relevant information.

None of these factors should be considered in isolation. The decision to approve a deferral or suspension request should be based on all these factors together, as a whole. Use all the information provided to make your decision.

Specific deferral exclusions have been identified by business. These are listed under Exclusion checks on Deferrals and Suspensions.
Opens in a new window
 These exclusions apply when there are no other reasons or factors to support the deferral request.

The final decision must be fair and reasonable.

Requests should generally not be considered when a final notice has issued. This is because potential prosecution action may be compromised. Go to Final notice issued or case referred for prosecution action for further information.

Your decision must be supported by the relevant practice statements and other administrative guidelines issued. Principles and guidelines to be factored into your decisions include the following:

PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals

PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles

relevant Frontline Operations Practice Note.
Opens in a new window

Go to Deferral requests in specific circumstances for further consideration when the deferral request is for:

a trust beneficiary statement
inability to pay a liability that may arise from lodgment
defence force personnel and war
delay obtaining information from a third party
a taxpayer experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability.
Factors affecting agents

An important consideration when assessing the reasons for deferral is whether the agent circumstances were unforeseeable and unavoidable.

Each request should be considered on its merits having regard to some or all of the following factors:

past lodgment performance

agent management of lodgment obligations

reporting period

approved form or other prescribed form type

number of deferrals

size of the practice

deferred due date

value of the information required

risk to the revenue.

Circumstances beyond or not beyond taxpayer control

A lodgment deferral will generally be granted when a taxpayer can demonstrate the inability to lodge by the due date is reasonably attributed to circumstances beyond their control (unforeseeable and unavoidable).

You must also consider deferral requests for taxpayers with a poor compliance history:

when the inability to lodge was caused by circumstances beyond their control, or

if it would otherwise be fair and reasonable to grant the deferral.

When the reasons may not be considered exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are considered. For example:

in conjunction with the taxpayer's good compliance history and the length of time requested

we would grant a reasonable deferral when a taxpayer advises they cannot lodge by the due date because of a planned trip overseas and the

ATO has not been able to generate an activity statement before the taxpayer's departure from Australia

taxpayer has genuine grounds for not having other arrangements in place to fulfil the obligation.

Circumstances	Taxpayer examples	Agent examples


Unforeseeable and unavoidable.

	
Natural and other disasters.
Serious illness of the taxpayer or family member.
Advanced age or youth of the taxpayer.
A problem due to language difficulties
The taxpayer is experiencing vulnerability, such as:

homelessness
family violence
financial coercion.
	

Natural and other disasters.

The serious illness of a sole practitioner or agent in a small firm.

Prolonged but unexpected staff absences.

Prolonged and expected staff absences but where other factors have prevented replacement by suitably qualified staff.




Foreseeable but unavoidable
i.e. may not be able to overcome the effects in the short term.

	

Impeded access to records.

	

Records seized during a police search or retained as evidence in a court matter.

Delays due to external auditor availability.

Despite making an active effort to achieve lodgment for a period, a small number of forms will not be lodged by the due date.




Foreseeable and avoidable.

	

The taxpayer forgot.

Anticipated absences from home or place of business or being overseas.

Installation of a new computer system.

Holiday shutdown.

Renovations to premises.

	

Taking of annual leave.

Effect of public holidays.

Final notice issued or case referred for prosecution action

Granting a deferral or suspension will compromise potential prosecution action. Due to this risk, requests will generally not be considered where either:

a final notice has been issued, or

the case has been referred for prosecution action.

Requests for a deferral or suspension should be referred to the Prosecution case owner to action when:

the request is after a case has been allocated to a prosecutor, or

a summons/court attendance notice/complaint has issued. 

If you consider a...	Then...
deferral should be granted after a final notice has issued	

the final notice will be either invalidated or unable to be used for prosecution

the final notice must be re-issued after the deferred due date has passed before prosecution action can proceed

consult your support team or team leader to check the decision is made by an officer with the appropriate authorisation for the deferral.


suspension should be granted after a final notice has issued	

it would be practical to withdraw the notice
Opens in a new window

an option would be to reissue a final notice with an agreed date to comply rather than suspend lodgment enforcement action.

Deferral requests in specific circumstances
Deferral request is for...	Then...
a trust beneficiary statement	

some closely held trusts are required to report on beneficiaries that are 'presently entitled' to one or both of the following:

a share of the trust income

a 'tax-preferred amount' of the trust.

These trusts are required to complete a trust beneficiary statement as part of the trust income tax return.

Deferral or suspension submitted for a trust beneficiary statement should be interpreted as a request for deferral or suspension of the trust's income tax return.

In Frontline Operations, trust beneficiary statement deferral requests from:

an agent are actioned by the lodgment program deferral teams when the trust's income tax return has not been lodged

a taxpayer are actioned by teams actioning debt general activities.


inability to pay the liability that may arise from lodgment	

encourage the taxpayer to:

lodge on time so they will not incur FTL penalty

negotiate payment options - go to Payment plan or payment in full.
Opens in a new window

Consider all factors in determining if it would be fair and reasonable to grant a lodgment deferral.


defence force personnel and war	

refer to Defence Force Personnel and War
Opens in a new window
 for additional information and factors to consider for deferral requests.


a delay in obtaining information from a third party	

for:

Partnerships and Trusts returns,

deferral request are generally not granted

where information to complete the return is held by a third party (in particular, a third party agent), the tax agent or taxpayer must have taken reasonable steps to get the information before the due date. They must demonstrate this for a deferral to be considered

Individual and Trust taxpayers may be subject to special considerations. Speak to your support staff or team leader to determine the treatment for this particular time

Research & Development (R&D) grant applications,

a deferral should be granted for 6 weeks from the date the application was lodged with AUSIndustry or the ATO (whatever is applicable)

where an agent has only recently taken a new taxpayer with R&D claims, additional considerations should be given. This is because the amount of time required to review the business and identify any R&D claims. Established companies will be more complex than a single purpose start-up company. 

 




a taxpayer experiencing severe and immediate vulnerability

	

if you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (for example, domestic and family violence, coercion, threats of self-harm) and as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability.
Opens in a new window

For:

Telephony, warm transfer to Specialised Client Support on x92196

Correspondence,

escalate the correspondence to Debt hardship

update the activity

Capability: Debt and Lodgment

Category: Debt - Specific

Type: Debt Hardship

Interaction: do not update

Status: UnAllocated

Description: Hardship

Notes: as appropriate

Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q.

Releasing held activity statements

An activity statement that has been generated can be held and not automatically released if there are problems with the taxpayer's record or ATO systems, for example a returned unclaimed indicator on file.

A deferral may be granted for lodgment and payment when a held activity statement is released because of the delay in issuing the activity statement. This applies to lodgment and payment requirements for any activity statement, whether monthly, quarterly, or annual.

The deferral must be input before re-generating the activity statement to allow the deferred due date to be printed on the activity statement.

The following table outlines deferrals that can be granted to different taxpayer types. If a taxpayer requests additional time beyond the timeframe in the table, they need to demonstrate that it is fair and reasonable to be given additional time.

Scenario	Action


All taxpayers with a PAYG instalment role using the ATO instalment amount

	A deferral of 26 days for lodgment and payment is to be granted from the date of release of the activity statement.


Large/medium taxpayers with either:

annual total income in excess of $10 million

annual GST turnover in excess of $20 million

annual withholding payments in excess of $1 million, or

an entity in a group of companies where at least one member of that group has an annual total income in excess of $10 million

	

For:

payments, unless the taxpayer has a PAYG instalment role, and is using the ATO instalment amount, payment is expected to have been made on time

lodgments, a deferral of 9 days to lodge the activity statement should be granted from the date of release.




All other non-large taxpayers, who do not fit the definition of large

	

A deferral of 26 days to lodge and pay the activity statement should be granted from the date of release of the activity statement.


Deferred GST (DGST) taxpayers	

The activity statement should be referred to DGST for action, go to Referral instruction.
Opens in a new window

Deferrals and regenerating activity statements

Before terminating and regenerating an activity statement, check for a granted deferral.

You may need to re-input the deferral when the activity statement is regenerated.

Payment obligations and the effect of lodgment deferrals

The taxable payments annual report is not eligible for payment deferrals. 

Legislation allows the Commissioner to defer the time for lodgment of any approved form or other prescribed form. Separate legislation allows deferral of time for payment of any amounts due. The relevant provision is section 255-10
Opens in a new window
 of Schedule 1 to the TAA.

The Lodgment Program details the specific lodgment and payment deferrals the Commissioner has granted to certain classes of taxpayers where the return is lodged by a registered agent.

Granting a lodgment deferral will defer the payment due date for individual or trust income tax returns. Under a long standing administrative practice the payment is generally due 21 days after either the deferred lodgment date or the issue of the notice of assessment, whichever is the later.

For further information on lodgment and payment due dates, refer to Lodgment and payment due dates.
Opens in a new window

Authorisation for deferrals/suspensions
For...	Points to note...
Deferral	

Tax legislation gives the Commissioner the discretion to defer the time within which an approved form or other prescribed form is required to be given. The Commissioner has delegated this power to the Deputy Commissioners who in turn authorise certain officers to grant deferrals.

You need to have the appropriate authorisation in order to grant additional time to lodge an approved form or other prescribed forms.

The authorisation for a deferral of time to lodge is calculated from the day after the date that the document was originally due to be lodged. However, for activity statements and payment summary annual reports (PSAR), the authorisation is calculated from the later of the original due date or the deferred due date granted to a class or classes of taxpayers.

For further information on authorisation on deferral of time to lodge refer to Authorisations and delegations guideline.
Opens in a new window

Where you do not have the authority to grant the deferral, you are still able to make recommendations and seek approval from an officer who has the appropriate authorisation.

Any request for a deferral that is beyond the authority of your support staff or team leader must be referred to a more senior officer for approval. The request must be referred via your support staff or team leader and should only be escalated if supported.

No specific authorisation level is required to deny a deferral request. Business procedures will specify if an administrative approval is required.


Suspension	

There are no specific authorisation guidelines for approval of a suspension. However, generally a suspension should not be granted for a period greater than four weeks.

If it appears the taxpayer's circumstances support a suspension beyond four weeks, the case should be discussed with your support staff or team leader and appropriate narrations recorded.

Each case should be based on its merits and the factors listed above taken into consideration for a period of suspension to be granted.

The maximum period an ALR accounting treatment (suspension) can be applied for is 2 years. This is a business decision applying to Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist teams actioning non-lodgment work.

Communicating and recording your decision
When...	Then...


communicating your decision

	

the type of request and your decision determines how you communicate the decision to the taxpayer. Generally, you will advise the taxpayer of your decision where a deferral or suspension is:

allowed, by phone, secure message reply or letter

disallowed, by letter (letter can be attached to a secure message reply)

refer to your business procedures for the requirements to be fulfilled and details of what the taxpayer must be advised

a manual deferral letter may be required depending on the type and circumstance of the request. For example, the:

request is for multiple taxpayers

letter is to be sent to an address not on ATO systems

taxpayer requested written confirmation as a result of the phone contact

where the request was lodged via Online services, the deferral decision may be communicated by a secure message.
Opens in a new window
 The letter can be attached to the unstructured Siebel secure message

where a deferral or suspension request is disallowed or varied, you must document the factors considered in making the decision and communicate those reasons to the taxpayer

when communicating your decision the taxpayer must be advised of their review rights when a deferral or suspension is disallowed or varied.


recording your decision	

you must document factors considered when making your decision and the reasons. You must follow quality narrations and decisions
Opens in a new window
 when recording your note. This is because:

it is consistent with legislation, procedures and expectations of ATO officers, who are accountable for their actions and decisions

external organisations such as the Tax Ombudsman, the courts and the Administrative Review Tribunal may review ATO records

taxpayers may also obtain copies of ATO records via the Freedom of Information Act 1982

taxpayers may seek statements of the reasons for decisions as part of Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977 (ADJR) processes

the taxpayer record should be updated in line with, for

lodgment program deferrals, Lodgment program OSfA deferral instruction
Opens in a new window

other requests, Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Review rights
Decision type	Review rights
Deferral	

The ATO's preferred method for reviewing adverse decisions on lodgment deferral requests is for the taxpayer to:

discuss the decision with the original decision maker

discuss the decision with a more a senior officer, if still dissatisfied.

If the taxpayer is still dissatisfied with the decision, they should be provided with the information on contacting ATO complaints.
Opens in a new window

The informal approach is the preferred method for taxpayers seeking a review but cannot be enforced. You are expected to advise taxpayers of the relevant avenues for review. Taxpayers also have formal external review rights available to them. Preferably they would only take this action after seeking an internal review.

Where a right of review is provided for in legislation, it is considered a formal review right. Such rights provide for ATO decisions to be reviewed independently or externally to the ATO.

Taxpayers cannot object to a deferral decision. However, a deferral decision is reviewable by the Federal Court
Opens in a new window
 or Federal Circuit Court under the Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977.

The grounds for review of a decision include a breach of the rules of natural justice, an improper exercise of power or an error of law.

While it is not common for a taxpayer to seek a judicial review, the existence of this formal review right means that all adverse decisions must be capable of being defended in the Federal Court.

Taxpayers may also lodge a complaint with the Tax Ombudsman. Generally, it is expected taxpayers will seek to resolve the issues directly with the ATO before approaching the Tax Ombudsman.


Suspension	

Refusing a suspension is not a reviewable decision under the Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977 (ADJR Act).

Taxpayers are not able to object to a suspension decision.

During a phone conversation, if the taxpayer expresses dissatisfaction with the decision to deny a suspension of lodgment action, advise them:

of their alternative avenues of redress (as per the 'Keep you informed' section of the ATO Charter).
Opens in a new window
 These avenues of redress may include

discussing the decision with a more senior officer
contacting ATO Complaints
Opens in a new window
lodging a complaint with the Tax Ombudsman. Generally, it is expected taxpayers will seek to resolve the issues directly with the ATO before approaching the Tax Ombudsman
they can issue a letter containing the decision and their review rights.

ICP-Grant a deferral

How to grant a deferral in ICP.

Last published 11 October 2024

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page contains the procedure for granting a deferral in ICP.

Excluding Payment-only deferrals for Large SGE taxpayers, all other tax practitioner deferral requests must be submitted via Online services for agents (OSfA).

Agent Deferral skilled staff (excluding FC Firmer and Legal Recovery) need to refer to Registered agent requests
Opens in a new window
 if the agent deferral request is for:

lodgment only
lodgment and payment, or
review of previous decision (excluding payment-only deferral).
Recent updates
11/10/2024	Update made to step 4. 	SD-86430 


24/09/2024

	Update to Background, step 2, step 7 and step 9.	

SD-85765

Procedure Steps
Grant deferral

Complete the below steps to key in the deferral for the taxpayer.

Step	Action

1	

Navigate to Client home in ICP and select the relevant role.


2	

Select Client calendar. 

If the lodgment you are deferring is in the Expected lodgments tab make a note of the period start date and end date for the period to be updated.

Activity statements:

if you are granting a deferral for the Activity Statement period 01/01/2024 - 31/03/2024, look for 01/03/2024 - 31/03/2024 

activity statements will not be in the Expected lodgments tab if the due date falls 3 or more months in the future. ICP will need to generate the lodgment before a deferral can be submitted 

Tax returns: 

identify if any of the following have due dates linked to the due date of the tax return

returns

statements, or notices

the due date of these must be changed to the deferred due date (for example, an annual GST return).


3	

Select:

the link in Form type expected for the required role or account

Defer lodgment and/or payment due date.


4	

Update the required details to grant a deferral:

Received date - the date the deferral request was received in the ATO

Input reason from the drop-down box (do not use the non-pursuit status reason).

If the taxpayer has a tax agent concessional due date and advises they now wish to self prepare and require a deferral, select Commissioner Discretion from the Input reason field.

Warning

Do not change any fields in the lodgment details tab, this may cause processing on the incorrect taxpayer role. If a suspense error occurs, you must select the role that matches the expected lodgment.


5	

Select:

Due date to be deferred tab

the appropriate type of due date to be deferred from the drop-down menu

Information alert:
Select Both if the deferral is for a period that was not in the original period list and was only added by following the steps for Add a period.

Next.


6	

Determine if you are entering a deferral for a payment due date only. If:

yes, go to step 9
no, 

lodgment due date only, or
both lodgment and payment due dates
go to step 7.
Information alert:
If the deferral is for a period that was not in the original period list and was only added by following the steps for Add a period, go to step 7.

7	

Select Defer lodgment due date and complete:

New lodgment due date: ensure it

does not fall on a weekend or public holiday, and
when this applies, is entered as the next working business day

Lodgment due date type

Concessional manual - select if deferral was granted due to a Commissioner's concession

Deferred - select if granting a deferral request

Reason for deferring lodgment due date: select Tax Agent Initiated. This will prevent ICP from generating an auto-letter

If the taxpayer has a tax agent concessional due date and advises they now wish to self prepare and require a deferral, select Demanded for the lodgment due date type.


8	

If:

you are granting a lodgment deferral only, or a lodgment and payment deferral and the taxpayer is not being granted more than the automatic 21 days to pay

select Form Confirmation

go to step 10

the payment due date needs to be deferred, go to step 9.


9	

Select Defer payment due date and complete the following:

New payment due date

Payment due date type

Concessional manual - select if deferral was granted due to a Commissioner's concession

Deferred - select if granting a deferral request

Reason for deferring payment due date: select Tax Agent Initiated. This will prevent ICP from generating an auto-letter

If the taxpayer has a tax agent concessional due date and advises they now wish to self prepare and require a deferral, select Demanded for the payment due date type.

Select Form Confirmation.


10	

Determine if an ICP suspense item has been created. If:

yes, go to ICP-Correct suspended form
Opens in a new window

If the form suspends with the exception 831, seek assistance from your coach if you are unsure of how to action the suspense reason.

Do not select the Form save & suspend button as this will create a new activity. This activity will then be routed to another operative who will not have appropriate information to correct the error.

no, select Submit.

Grant a suspension in ICP

How to grant a suspension in ICP.

Last published 15 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page shows you how to grant a suspension in integrated core processing (ICP).

Recent updates

15/12/2025

	

Added new step 1.

	

SD-99950

Procedure Steps
Action required
Step	Action
1 	

If the suspension request is for:

an individual, 
use the ICP ALR Macro
Opens in a new window
, or
go to step 2
a bulk request, use the ICP ALR Macro.
Opens in a new window

2	

Go to ICP Client home screen.


3	

Go to the Client role summary and select the Periods for the relevant role.

Documents types lodged under role
ITR - Income Tax Role: Income Tax Returns
AS - Activity Statement Reporting Role: Quarterly or monthly Activity Statements
GSTA/GSTR - GST Annual Reporting: 
Annual GST returns
Annual GST information report (GAIR): 
PAYG Annual Reporting: Annual PAYGW (ITW (Annual) (this role is for Closely Held Trusts only)
PAYGI Annual Reporting: Annual PAYGI (Annual ITI)
FBT - Fringe Benefits tax Role: Annual FBT returns
TPAR - Taxable Payments Annual Reporter: Taxable payments annual report
Assess Low Risk Accounting Treatments

 These can only be added to the following roles on Integrated Taxpayer Accounts:

Activity Statement Reporting Role

PAYGI Annual Reporting

PAYGW Annual Reporting

GST Annual Reporting

GST Return Reporting.

Warning
alert:
Do not add an Assessed Low Risk accounting treatment to the Goods & Services Tax role, as it will not capture the required suspension.


4	

Complete the following actions. Select:

appropriate Period start date

Accounting treatments in the right-hand menu

Note: If you are granting a suspension for the period 01/01/2018 - 31/03/2018, look for 01/03/2018 - 31/03/2018.    


5	

To grant a suspension, an Assessed Low Risk accounting treatment must be applied at the period level.

Is there already an Assessed Low Risk accounting treatment at the period level you are actioning? If:

yes, and the

agreed suspension date is earlier than the end date for the current accounting treatment, no action is required. This is the end of the procedure

current accounting treatment end date is not sufficient to cover the period of the suspension, go to step 6

no, go to step 7.


6	

Modify the accounting treatment.

Use the ICP ALR Macro
Opens in a new window
 or: 

select 
the Assessed Low Risk (ALR) accounting treatment 
Modify accounting treatment (An accounting treatment with a status of Cancelled cannot be modified) 
key the Start date (date the suspension of lodgment was granted) 
key the End date (agreed date the lodgment is to be made)

You must enter an end date.

select 
appropriate Input reason. Do not use input reason No reason able to be provided or Other 
Save. If an error message is displayed, view the message and correct the error. 

This is the end of the procedure.


7	

Add the accounting treatment.

Use the ICP ALR Macro
Opens in a new window
 or: 

select 
Add Accounting Treatment from the page level navigation bar 
Assessed Low Risk (ALR) from the Treatment category drop-down menu 
Assessed Low Risk (ALR) from the Treatment type drop-down menu 
None from the Treatment arrangement drop-down menu 
key the Start date (date the suspension of lodgment was granted)
key the End date (agreed date the lodgment is to be made) 
select 
Active from the Status drop-down menu 
Tax Office initiated from the Input reason drop-down menu. Do not use Input reason No Reason Able to Be provided or Other. 
Save. If an error message is displayed, view the message and correct the error.

Client calendar - correct lodgment or payment due date

Procedure to correct a deferred lodgment or payment due date where the original deferral date was entered in error.

Last published 21 October 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This procedure describes how to correct a deferred lodgment or payment due date, where the original deferral date was entered in error.

Where an incorrect due date is entered when granting a deferral:

correspondence relating to the transaction needs to be cancelled, and

a new deferral date entered.

Due to system constraints, you cannot modify a due date to a date before the current deferred due date using the same Reason for deferring lodgment due date value. Use this procedure where the same Reason for deferring lodgment due date value needs to be displayed.

This workaround will enable you to:

backdate the deferral due date using a different reason code

forward date the deferral using the correct reason code, and

determine if failure to lodge (FTL) penalties or general interest charges (GIC) need remitting.

Recent updates

10/12/2024

	

Minor update throughout the page. 

	

SD-88820

Procedure Steps
Before you begin

You must have the required authorisation and training to approve the deferral of an expected lodgment or payment.

This procedure begins when you have:

determined changes to the taxpayer record are required, and

navigated to the ICP Client calendar for the taxpayer. View will default to the Expected lodgments tab.

Task 1 - Consider FTL penalty
Step	Action


1

	

Note the following details:

Agreed date: date originally agreed the taxpayer would have an extension of time until to lodge

Keyed date: date keyed in error as the New lodgment due date

Lodgment date: date the taxpayer made their lodgment, and

Current projected payment due date.




2

	

Check if the actual lodgment date is after the keyed date. If:

yes, go to step 3

no, go to step 5.




3

	

Check if the keyed date is before the agreed date. If:

yes, go to step 4

no, go to step 5.




4

	

Remit any FTL penalties
Opens in a new window
 accrued between the keyed date and agreed date.




5

	

Select Received payments and check if a payment has been received for the lodgment period in question. If:

yes, go to task 2

no, go to step 6.




6

	

Check if the agreed payment date is less than or equal to today plus 21 days. If:

yes

contact the taxpayer
Opens in a new window
 to renegotiate the payment dates

go to task 3

no, go to task 4.

Step	Action

Keyed date: date keyed in error as the New lodgment due date or calculated by the system, and

Payment date: date the taxpayer made their payment.




2

	

Check if the actual payment date is after the keyed date. If:

yes, go to step 3

no, this is the end of the procedure.




3

	

Check if the keyed date is before the agreed date. If:

yes, go to step 4

no, this is the end of the procedure.

ICP, go to Cancel remit - remit auto GIC

yes, go to task 3

no, this is the end of the procedure.

Period end date.

Task 6 - Backdate lodgment due date
Step	Action


1

2

For example, if the required date is 01/03/2020, the due date entered should be 28/02/2020 in the New lodgment due date field.

The new lodgment due date must be a business day and cannot fall on a weekend or public holiday. Use the next working day after the weekend or public holiday.

For:

companies or superannuation funds, deferring an income tax lodgment due date does not automatically defer the payment due date. You may need to defer the new income tax form payment due date for a company or a superannuation fund

all other obligations, a lodgment deferral does not automatically extend the payment due date.

Deferral or suspension - DSIM
Deferral or suspension - DSIM

Procedure to input details and background on the Deferral/Suspension Input Module (DSIM).

Last published 21 November 2024

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

When you grant a deferral or a suspension on a PAYG withholding annual report or PAYG payment summary annual report (PSAR), you must record the details in the Deferral/Suspension Input Module (DSIM).

This ensures that penalties are correctly applied and cases are not prematurely downloaded for non-lodgment follow up action.

Recent Updates
21/11/2024	Minor update to 'Background' and 'Checks'.	

SD-87845

Checks

Before you can input a deferral, you must check if the document has been lodged and any payments have been received. A deferral or suspension of action entry must be made before the document is lodged.

If the DSIM is updated after the document has been lodged, or any payments are received, the taxpayer may be incorrectly exposed to a failure to lodge on time (FTL) penalty or general interest charge (GIC).

Procedure Steps
Input deferral or suspension
Step	Action
1	

Access the Deferral/Suspension Input Module (DSIM).


2	

Determine if you need to view the taxpayer's deferral/suspension history. If:

yes, go to step 3

no,

select Cancel on the Search screen

go to step 5.


3	

Select the appropriate Rcvbl type from the drop down list.

Tick the Search for both Lodgment and Payment box, if appropriate.


4	

If the taxpayer has an ABN, you must use the ABN in preference to the TFN.

Enter the ABN (plus ICA number) or TFN into the appropriate field. Select Search.

This will result in the Deferral/Suspension list displayed.


5	

Select File, then New. Alternatively, you can select the blank page icon, under File.

If there is more than one ICA role, select the relevant ICA role to ensure the suspension is added to the correct role.


6	

At:

Request Type, select Deferral or Suspension of Action

Status, select Approved.


7	

If the taxpayer has an ABN, you must use the ABN to input a deferral/suspension in preference to the TFN.

Enter the ABN (plus ICA number) or TFN into the appropriate field. Select Search.


8	

Complete the following actions:

Select the appropriate Rcvbl type from the drop down list.

Tick the Add both Payment and Lodgment box, if appropriate.

Enter the following details:

Period end date: Enter the end date of the lodgment for the reporting period

Original due date: Enter the original due date of the lodgment

Deferred due date: Enter the deferred due date granted (only applicable for a deferral)

SACT Review date: Enter the suspension due date granted (only applicable for a suspension)

Request reason: Select the appropriate reason from the drop down menu

Select OK.

9	

If the Client Not Found error message appears, this does not necessarily mean that the deferral or suspension has not been input successfully.

Conduct a search to confirm that the details have been accepted.

If you have entered the details using the ABN and you get this error message, it may mean:

that the ABN is still pending, or

there are other problems with registration. 

Do not attempt to input the deferral/suspension using the TFN, as the system will accept this, but the underlying problem with registration/non-generation will not be solved.

Cancel incorrect entries
Step	Action
1	

Access the Deferral/Suspension Input Module (DSIM).


2	

Select Cancel on the Search screen.




3

	

Highlight the incorrect entry that you want to cancel.




4

	

Select Edit then Cancel.

Opens in a new window

Eligibility

A deferral can be granted where it is fair and reasonable to do so. This would be after considering all relevant circumstances that lead to the inability to lodge and or pay by the due date.

The reason must be relevant to the deferral request. For example, the inability to pay by the due date is not a valid reason for failing to lodge on time.

It is not possible to anticipate every circumstance which may prevent on time lodgment and or payment. However, it is generally considered fair and reasonable:

to grant a lodgment and or payment deferral
where the reason is attributed to exceptional or unforeseen circumstances.

These may include: 

a legal or record access impediment
medical emergency/serious illness or death
natural or other disasters
Opens in a new window
 or events that may have, or have had, a significant impact
personal circumstances that resulted in the taxpayer experiencing vulnerability, such as
sudden homelessness
family violence.

When the reasons may not be exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are taken into account.

Specific principles and guidelines for considering deferral requests exist. For:

payment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles
 lodgment deferral requests, refer to PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals.

Low-risk criteria also exist in this procedure. These can help assess a lodgment deferral request.

If the taxpayer is found to be ineligible for a deferral, consider an alternative action, for example, a suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Information

Where the taxpayer has been affected by a natural disaster, you must confirm the appropriate deferral has been entered into the system. If not, you must take the required action to ensure the system correctly reflects the deferred due date. The exception to this is the running of a bulk process. An FAQ procedure will notify if this is the case.

Opens in a new window
 if required.

eligible AS

all future AS will be delivered to their
ATO online account
Online services for business, or

Deferral requests cannot be offered for the following:

where a self-managed superannuation fund (SMSF) has been contacted by the Superannuation Engagement and Assurance area to enter into an agreement to lodge overdue returns. If there is an active case in Siebel, all deferral requests should be forwarded to the Case Officer for determination

taxpayers are expected to lodge their returns by the due dates
they can either lodge their returns

how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)
Excess contributions tax and Division 293.
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted on an existing liability on the basis of a future credit.

For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Vulnerability

Where you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (i.e. threats of self-harm, family and domestic violence or financial coercion) as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability,

telephony

Statutory due dates

Deferrals can only be granted from the statutory due date. The statutory due date is the:

original due date, or 

concessional due date.

Information

Deferring a lodgment does not change the original concessional due date. If the taxpayer requests another deferral after one is already granted, this must be assessed against the original/concessional due date, not the deferred due date.

Refer to the following to check concessional due dates for:

self-preparers electronic lodgment
Opens in a new window

registered agents
Opens in a new window

individuals
Opens in a new window

non-individuals.

Scenarios
Recipients of Family Tax Benefit or Child Care Subsidy

Confirm with the taxpayer if they are a recipient of Family Tax Benefit (FTB) or Child Care Subsidy (CCS) from Services Australia.
Opens in a new window

If yes, advise them they and their partner must lodge their income tax return by 30 June the following year. Lodgment deferrals do not alter this requirement.

Information

Services Australia has granted additional time to access CCS for families who have not lodged their 2018-19 tax return.

If CSS recipients and their partners have not submitted their 2018-19 tax return, they can still access the CSS up until 31 March 2021.

While additional time has been granted to access the subsidy, this does not mean there is an extension of the time to lodge their 2018-19 return and failure to lodge penalties and general interest charges may apply.

Taxpayers are still encouraged to lodge their 2018-19 tax return as soon as possible, noting the extension is for CCS purposes only, and delaying lodgment of the return could affect other benefits, such as Family Tax Benefit.

Services Australia
Opens in a new window
 needs income details to balance payments for FTB and CCS, so late lodgment of the tax return may impact their benefits. If they are not required to lodge a tax return, they need to confirm this with Services Australia and add a return not necessary.
Opens in a new window

For example: A taxpayer has requested a lodgment deferral for their 2019/20 income year to lodge it after 30 June 2021. If the lodgment is not made by 30 June 2021, taxpayers receiving:

Family Tax Benefit may
miss out on additional payments
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year
Child Care Subsidy may
lose their ongoing entitlement
receive a debt and must repay the amount received in the 2019/20 tax year.

Services Australia can help taxpayers who have special circumstances preventing them or their partner from lodging before the deadline. Extensions are assessed on a case by case basis and taxpayers must contact Services Australia to discuss.

For more information, the taxpayer can contact Services Australia Families line on 13 61 50 and refer the taxpayer to Your family income estimate for family assistance payments.
Opens in a new window

Registered agent deferrals and suspensions

D-1, B-1 - Information about when tax and BAS agents request deferrals and suspensions.

Last published 30 March 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1, B-1

This page provides information for registered tax agents and registered BAS agents on requesting lodgment deferrals and suspensions.

Registered agents must use the lodgment deferral function in Online services for agents (OSfA) to apply for deferrals for:

income tax returns
Fringe benefits tax returns
eligible monthly and quarterly activity statements
annual GST returns
PAYG payment summary reports
Taxable payments annual reports.

Spreadsheet application forms are no longer available and will not be processed.

Registered agents cannot apply for payment-only deferrals via the lodgment deferral function. Refer to Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents.
Opens in a new window

Recent updates

30/03/2026

	

Updated 'Background' and 'Scenarios' sections.

	SD-103022
Self Help
ATO website
Help the caller to go to the page while on the phone.

This is a chance to educate the caller about self-help options, which may lead to:

them using self-service in the future, and
a better experience for them.

Agents have self-service options available to them on the ATO website, including:

Lodgment program deferrals
Opens in a new window
 which includes instructional videos on how to add

a single lodgment deferral

multiple lodgment deferrals

Online services for agents user guide.
Opens in a new window

Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

PORO

Establish PORO.
Opens in a new window

Scenarios
Agent situations
If the agent is...
	Then...


requesting deferrals over the phone for not-for-profit self-review return (NFP SRR) only

	

advise the agent:

the NFP SRR can be lodged without needing to request a deferral via
OSfA
self-help phone service, on 13 72 26
penalties will not apply for the 2023–24 income year, as it is a transitional period
they can obtain more information on the ATO website at
How to lodge the NFP self-review return
Opens in a new window
What happens if you lodge the NFP self-review return late.
Opens in a new window

If the agent insists on a deferral, action the request following Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window




requesting a lodgment or payment deferral for Global and Domestic Minimum Tax (GDMT)
Opens in a new window

	

advise the agent:

deferrals can be requested via OSfA practice mail using the relevant deferral form
they can obtain more information on the ATO website at Lodging, paying and other obligations for Pillar Two.
Opens in a new window



requesting deferrals over the phone

	

advise the agent:

they can no longer request agent assessed deferrals over the phone

all lodgment deferral requests must be submitted via the lodgment deferral function in OSfA.

Agents with new or re-engaged taxpayers can submit via OSfA a:

lodgment deferral for the current year

suspension of compliance action for overdue prior year income tax returns.

Refer to New or re engaged taxpayer deferral requests
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website for more information.

If the agent is calling about a final demand letter, go to Deferral exceptions.




querying a lodgment due date for a taxpayer they represent

	

escalate to the Tax practitioner lodgment program as follows:

Capability: Compliance
Category: Intermediaries
Type: Lodgment program query
Notes: add a note
Opens in a new window
 to include
taxpayer's TFN or ABN
agent number
contact name
email address
details of the issue.



requiring assistance with the lodgment deferral function in OSfA

	

go to OSfA Lodgment deferral function below.




asking about requesting deferrals using a deferral application form and practice mail message

	

advise the agent:

the spreadsheet application forms are no longer available
will not be processed, and
they must apply using the lodgment deferral function in OSfA.



querying the progress of deferral request

	

go to Progress of deferral request below.



experiencing exceptional or unforeseen circumstances affecting the whole of the agent’s practice, or
on a supported lodgment program, or
on a managed lodgment program
	
escalate the deferral or suspension request by following Supported lodgment program.
Opens in a new window

Note:

Check the agent’s Tax Practitioner Consolidated View (TPCV) for an alert with a Type of Lodgment Program and a Category of:

Supported Lodgment Program, or
Managed Lodgment Program.
In Detail
OSfA lodgment deferral function

Registered agents need certain identity strength and permissions to submit a lodgment deferral request using the lodgment deferral function in OSfA:

Standard or Strong myID identity strength

permission for
Registration - Add/Update, and
View and action own mail.

Assist the agent using the relevant sections below:

Benefits of the function

Getting started

Entering information into the lodgment deferral function

Agent has applied for a lodgment deferral - what's next?

How to know if a deferral request has been approved.

Benefits of the function

The benefits of using the lodgment deferral function include:

a single-entry point to request all deferral types

the ability to

request up to 40 deferrals in one request

view the status of the deferral request in OSfA

where the request meets low risk guidelines, that is an agent assessed or new or re-engaged taxpayer deferral

processing via the OSfA deferral function occurs within 48 hours

deferred dates updated in ICP occur within 48 hours

visibility of the request and the outcome in OSfA practice mailbox.

Requests that do not meet low risk guidelines are escalated for manual assessment as an ATO assessed deferral.

Processing of escalated requests can take up to 28 calendar days.

Getting started

Agents can access the lodgment deferral function from the OSfA home page by selecting:

Reports and forms

Forms

Lodgment deferral.

Agents can only request lodgment deferrals for taxpayers linked to their registered agent number (RAN) and must be authorised to act for them.

Entering information into the lodgment deferral function

The lodgment deferral function in OSFA will:

prompt the agent for information

validate the information provided
provide an alert or error message if the information entered is invalid or requires review by the agent.

Agents can refer to the OSfA user guide,
Opens in a new window
 view on-screen help (help Aside), or watch videos
Opens in a new window
 for assistance to complete the request.

If the agent is...
	Then advise the agent...

not sure which deferral reason to pick	
select the most relevant reason
supply additional information in the supporting information text field.

receiving an error message	

they will receive an error message if:

the agent is not authorised to defer the lodgment type, e.g. BAS agent attempting to defer an income tax return

the requested deferral date is more than 12 months from the date of their request

an eligible obligation has not been found for the obligation and/or period entered
the requested deferral date has passed
an SMSF annual return has been demanded

the request is for one of the forms below that are not eligible for a lodgment deferral. Advise the agent they can vary instalments before the instalment due date. Refer to How to vary your PAYG instalments
Opens in a new window

R, quarterly PAYG instalment notice
S, quarterly GST instalment notice
T, quarterly GST and PAYG instalment notice
N, annual PAYG instalment notice.
Agent has applied for a lodgment deferral - what's next?
The lodgment deferral function in OSfA will check each deferral request.
The agent will receive an on-screen confirmation of their request with an ATO receipt ID.
The agent will also be able to view a message in the Sent items of their practice mailbox with the outcome of their deferral request when the lodgment deferral function has completed its process.
Warning

A processed request does not mean that all deferrals have been approved. Some deferrals may have been escalated for manual assessment. This can take up to 28 calendar days.

If the deferral request has been processed and...
	Then...



meets low-risk guidelines (such as agent assessed or new or re-engaged taxpayer deferral)

	
the request will be approved and finalised in OSfA
the taxpayer's due date will be updated in our systems within 48 hours.



does not meet low-risk guidelines (ATO assessed deferral)

	

the request will be escalated for manual assessment. This can take up to 28 days in peak lodgment periods

agents will receive another practice mail message notifying them of the outcome of requests escalated for manual assessment

our large business specialists review lodgment deferral requests for

significant global entities

large business, including

large withholders, or

excisetaxpayers

if an obligation is lodged before an escalated deferral request has been finalised, a remediation process will remove penalties and interest.

How to know if a deferral request has been approved

Agents will receive a message in the Sent items in their practice mailbox when the request has been processed. The message contains a spreadsheet with the outcome of each deferral in the request:

approved

escalated

failed.

Agents can also check the status of lodgment deferral requests submitted within the past 90 days in their lodgment deferral history.
Opens in a new window
 To do this, they should click on the ATO receipt ID of the relevant request. If they do not know the ATO receipt ID of the request, they can filter the request by:

date submitted

ABN or TFN

lodgment type, e.g. income tax return.

If the agent's lodgment deferral history shows that a request is still processing, OSfA has not yet completed its checks. Processing will be completed within 48 hours.

If the status of the processed deferral request is...
	Then...

Approved	

OSfA has completed checks and:

approved the deferrals that meet our low-risk guidelines
updated the lodgment due date and, if applicable, the payment due date.

Escalated	

the request does not meet our low risk guidelines and has been escalated for review by an ATO staff member as an ATO assessed deferral.
Opens in a new window
 Additional reasons could include:

deferral is for a significant global entity or large withholder

taxpayer is

undergoing prosecution

insolvent or bankrupt, or subject to legal action.

It could take up to 28 days for escalated deferrals to be processed. Escalated deferrals will not have their status updated in OSfA.

The agent will receive a message in their practice mailbox advising them of the outcome.


Failed	

a technical or system issue has prevented the deferral request being submitted or processed.

For:

GST annual (GSTA) returns


do not action or advise the agent to resubmit

the request will be manually assessed (we receive an exceptions report which allows us to identify and process certain failed requests)

other obligations

the agent can resubmit their request. If this also 'failed', advise:

it will be manually assessed ( we receive an exceptions report which allows us to identify and process certain failed requests)

processing may take up to 28 calendar days.

They will receive notification of either decision via practice mail.

Requests submitted via OSfA lodgment deferral function

Deferral requests received via the OSfA deferral function will result in one or more activities being created in the agent's TPCV. The Siebel activities will have the following classification:

Capability: Compliance
Category: Intermediaries
Type: as per the table below.
Type
	Details



OSfA Deferral

	

The activity is a summary of the deferral request.

The activity has been auto finalised and saved to the agents TPCV.

No action is required on this activity.




Agent Deferral

	

The activity contains deferrals that have been escalated for manual assessment.




Agent Deferral Complex

	

The activity contains deferrals for significant global entities (SGEs) or large withholders that have been escalated for manual assessment by the Frontline Compliance team processing high impact deferrals.

Check if the receipt ID the agent has provided displays an activity with the following classifications:

Category: Compliance
Capability: Intermediaries
Type: OSfA Deferral
Interaction: Tax Agent Portal
Status: Done.

If yes, this activity is the internal record of the OSfA request and not the escalated request for processing. Ask the agent for the receipt ID from the lodgment history screen in OSfA. To access the OSfA lodgment history screen, the agent should go to:

Report and forms
Forms
Lodgment deferral.

If the agent cannot locate the original receipt ID:

access the OSfA deferral activity
open the attached spreadsheet
expand width of column A to 55
copy the Unique transaction ID. This will be the receipt ID of the original request
use this number to search for the original activity
the escalated activity will have the following classifications
Category: Compliance
Capability: Intermediaries
Type: Agent Deferral
Interaction: Tax Agent Portal.
Progress of deferral request

Deferral requests may take up to 28 days to be finalised, including back-end processing, during peak lodgment periods. This includes requests submitted using the lodgment deferral function in OSfA and escalated for manual assessment.

Agents will receive notification of the decision via practice mail if the deferral request is:

allowed in full

allowed in part

refused.

A remediation process is available to remove penalties and interest if the obligation is lodged before the deferral request is finalised.

If an agent asks about the progress of their deferral request, refer to table below.

Step	Action
1	

Check if the agent has checked:

the taxpayer's records in OSfA or PLS to see if the requested deferral date is showing
their own and the Practice mail
Opens in a new window
 inbox for notification of the deferral decision.

When submitted via the lodgment deferral function in OSfA

a secure mail message is sent to the practice's Practice mail sent items, (not the requesting agent's practice mail inbox) within 48 hours of receipt and completed processing, or
where the request was escalated for manual assessment, a secure mail message is sent to the practice's Practice mail
Opens in a new window
 inbox after the ATO has made a decision on the request (not the requesting agent's practice mail inbox).

2	

Check the deferral activity to see if the request has been actioned. Refer to Requests submitted via OSfA lodgment deferral function and the notes on the activity.

If the request has been:

allowed, go to step 3
refused, go to step 4
not actioned, go to step 5.

3	

Where the request has been allowed, advise the agent a response will be sent and can take up to 14 days to appear on the taxpayer's account.

Do not manually process any deferral requests already actioned, regardless of the note or secure message response. The processing of these deferrals can take up to 14 days to finalise. While the activity may have a Status of Done, the processing may still be in progress.

This is the end of the procedure.




4

	

Where the request has been refused, check the activity notes for the reason and advise the registered agent.

This is the end of the procedure.


5	

Where the request has not been actioned and 28 days has:

not passed, advise the agent they will receive a practice mail message when the deferral request is actioned

passed, advise the agent

deferral requests are actioned in date order

there may be delays during peak periods.

Deferral exceptions

If the taxpayer received a Final Warning letter:

advise the agent they should not submit a deferral request via OSfA

if you are

lodgment trained, refer to Final Warning
Opens in a new window

not lodgment trained, transfer to D-1.

Agent disagrees with deferral decision

An agent can request a review of a lodgment deferral decision
Opens in a new window
 if they are not satisfied with the outcome of their request. The same review process applies regardless of the method used to submit the original deferral request. The request must be lodged within 21 days from the date on the communication varying or declining their original request.

The Review of deferral decision form:

may not function correctly if opened or saved with alternate PDF reading software such as Nitro or, PDF extender
is designed to be completed on a desktop or laptop computer with the latest version of Adobe Reader
Opens in a new window
 software installed.

The form will not load on a mobile device, tablet or within a browser. Double clicking the form will not open it.

To download and open the form:

right-click on Review of lodgment deferral decision

select Save target as or Save link as to save it to a computer

open the saved form with Adobe Acrobat Reader DC

enable JavaScript if prompted

once the agent has completed the form, they can save it using the Save form button.

The completed form must be submitted through Practice mail in OSfA. It's important to select the correct topic and subject to ensure the request is directed to the relevant area for processing.

To submit the form through Practice mail in OSfA:

select topic View more topics
select other topics General questions, problems and help
select subject Review of decision for deferrals from tax agents
complete the required fields and attach the form to the message
select the Declaration, then select Send.
Lodgment program support

Lodgment deferrals can help where an agent or the taxpayers they represent are experiencing exceptional or unforeseen circumstances affecting their ability to lodge by a due date.

Where exceptional or unforeseen circumstances have affected the whole of an agent's practice, we can provide tailored support to help the agent get their Lodgment program back on track. For more information, refer to Supported lodgment program.
Opens in a new window

In the case of large-scale natural disasters or system outages, we will communicate through the Tax professionals newsletters and alerts
Opens in a new window
 to advise agents of the support being provided.

Payment-only Deferrals for Registered agents
Payment-only Deferrals for Registered agents

D-1 - Procedure to action payment-only deferral requests received through Online Services for Agents (OSfA).

Last published 24 April 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

D-1

This page:

provides the procedure to advise Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist staff on how to action registered tax or BAS agent payment-only deferral requests received through Online Services for Agents (OSfA)
is only to be used by staff assigned to this work.

Agents may request a deferral where the taxpayer's circumstances affect their ability to pay by the due date.

Where the circumstances are not appropriate to grant a deferral, you must consider negotiating a payment plan.

Use the table below to decide the relevant procedure to follow.

For...
	Then...



requests for payment-only deferral excluding:

High Impact Deferrals
form type Combined Global and Domestic Minimum Tax Returns (CGDMTR).
	

continue with this procedure.




Frontline Compliance staff skilled in Agent Deferrals

	

go to Lodgment program deferrals - for sophisticated taxpayers.
Opens in a new window




OSfA requests for:

lodgment deferral
lodgment and payment deferral
suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

Excluding form type:

CGDMTR
OSfA request for CGDMTR or payment deferral
	

go to

Lodgment program deferral instruction - OSfA function
Opens in a new window
CGDMTR deferral or suspension request.
Opens in a new window

OSfA request for CGDMTR lodgment or payment deferral
	go to CGDMTR deferral or suspension request.
Opens in a new window




all other deferral requests received outside OSfA

	

go to Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Submitting a deferral request
Criterion
	Requirement



Circumstances supporting the request

	

Agents can lodge a payment-only deferral application if:

there are exceptional or unforeseen circumstances beyond their taxpayer's control, and

the exceptional or unforeseen circumstances are consistent with those outlined in law administration practice statement PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 General debt collection powers and principles.




Required information

	

For the request to be considered, the agent must:

provide

the requested payment deferral date

full details of their taxpayer's circumstances that have or will prevent them from paying by the due date

the steps the taxpayer took to mitigate the effects of those circumstances

details of any payment arrangement relating to the debt (if applicable)

demonstrate that the taxpayer

can make full payment once the circumstances have been resolved

will pay their continuing tax-related liabilities on time, once the circumstances are under control.

Go to Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents
Opens in a new window
 on the ATO website.




How to submit the request

	

Agent must submit their request via OSfA on the Payment deferral application form.
Opens in a new window




Eligible debts

	

The Payment-only Deferrals for Registered Agents instruction allows the agent to request a payment-only deferral for the following debts if the relevant document is lodged:

Income Tax

Liabilities arising following lodgment of monthly and quarterly activity statements

Fringe benefits tax (FBT).




Other debts

	

The Payment deferral application form does not provide the option to request a payment deferral for other debts including:

GIC/FTL/SIC relating to eligible documents

Super Guarantee Charge

Excise

Excess Contribution tax

Div293

Jobkeeper overpayments

Judgment interest or costs.

Do not use this procedure to process payment-only deferral requests for other debts. Refer to the relevant procedure to process those requests.

the taxpayer using self-service in the future, and
a better taxpayer experience.

Taxpayers can obtain more information about Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents
Opens in a new window
 from the ATO website, which provides detailed information on how to submit the request.

Information
alert:
Use our self-service any time - it's easy, free and fast. Refer to Self-help services.
Opens in a new window

Checks
When making a decision
Information

Payment deferrals should not be granted for an existing liability based on a future credit including lodging an amendment. For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit that will extinguish the debt and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

The Commissioner will generally allow a deferral where exceptional or unforeseen circumstances have been provided and it is considered fair and reasonable to do so having considered all the material facts.

Payment deferral:

requests are considered high risk and should only be allowed where the circumstances are consistent with those outlined in paragraphs 32-42 of PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
are generally a short-term measure to provide immediate assistance in extenuating circumstances.

If payment is unlikely to be made in full by the deferred due date, a tailored payment arrangement may be more appropriate.

Where you need additional information to make your decision, contact the agent.

Establish PORO
Warning

PORO must be completed for all taxpayers listed before proceeding.

Information

For deceased taxpayer deferral requests, if the agent RAN is listed for the relevant taxpayer and role, there are no additional requirements to pass PORO. This is regardless of any reference on the deferral request about the taxpayer being deceased or any indicators on ATO systems.

Do not use this process for any other work types.

1

taxpayers are expected to lodge and pay their returns by the due dates

they can either lodge and pay their returns

how they believe the law applies to their circumstances (as outlined in their class ruling or private ruling request)

an existing liability based on a future credit posting, including lodging an amendment. For example, a taxpayer expects a future lodgment to result in a credit to cover the existing debts and requests a payment deferral on a June BAS (debit) until the September BAS (credit) is lodged.

Referred to ECA

Before granting a payment deferral, check to see if the taxpayer has been referred to the ECA.

Go to the taxpayer's Consolidated Taxpayer View (CCV) in Siebel and select Query, then Go.

Search for the following activity:

Capability: Debt Case
Category: Collection negotiation
Type: Referred to Mercantile Agent 2
Interaction: Internal - case for routing
Description: Recoveries Corp
Status: UnAllocated

You can also use the following ECP gadgets to confirm whether the taxpayer has been referred to ECA.

Select the:

Taxpayer tab, go to the Taxpayer Indicator
Identify whether there is an active External Collection Agency indicator

If you have identified an active case or ECA indicator in ECP, this means the taxpayer is still with the ECA.

If...	And...	Then...
the case is active
	

the taxpayer is:

experiencing extenuating or unforeseen circumstances beyond their control that reasonably contributed to the delay in payment

able to demonstrate steps were taken to mitigate the effects of those circumstances

be able to demonstrate the ability to

make full payment once the circumstances have been resolved

pay their continuing tax-related liabilities on time

	

consider whether a deferral is appropriate and whether it aligns with this procedure.

If you determine that a deferral is appropriate you will also need to withdraw the ECA debt referral case.
Opens in a new window

Once you have actioned the ECA withdrawal, go to task 1.

 for transfer details)

the ECA may be able to provide the taxpayer with additional time where appropriate

if unable to transfer, provide the contact details to call the ECA

do not update the Siebel debt case. This will withdraw the taxpayer from the ECA

disallow the request, and

send a mail message via OSfA using template Deferral disallowed (with Option 2).

if you have determined it is appropriate to grant a deferral or have agreed to allow additional time

Case Summary tab

Case ID hyperlink to open the case

go to task 1.

Procedure Steps
Task 1 - Previous request

Check ICP - View deferral
Opens in a new window
 and notes for previous deferral requests. Use this information to guide your decision.

Consider whether special consideration for ADF personnel and taxpayers affected by war
Opens in a new window
 applies.

Note if agent contact is required.

Warning

Do not grant another deferral for the same period if the taxpayer's previous request was:

allowed, unless the taxpayer's circumstances warrant a further extension

disallowed, unless the taxpayer has provided further information that warrants a review of the original decision.

Task 2 - Review information

Review the deferral request and all attachments. Determine if additional information is required to make the decision for all listed taxpayers.

For the payment-only deferral request to be considered, the taxpayer must:

be, or have been, experiencing extenuating or unforeseen circumstances beyond their control that reasonably contributed to the delay in payment which affects or affected their ability to pay by the original due date. For example

death or serious illness of person responsible for paying where there is no other person that can, or could, make the payment
inability to access funds because of a legal impediment (e.g. probate not being granted or access to funds being denied by the order of a Court)
entity or person responsible for payment had funds embezzled by their tax agent, solicitor or other third party
natural disaster, such as flood, fire, drought, earthquake and the like
other disasters that have, or may have, had a significant impact on the person responsible for payment
where appropriate, be able to demonstrate steps were taken by the taxpayer to mitigate the effects of those circumstances
be able to demonstrate the ability to
make full payment once the circumstances have been resolved
pay their continuing tax-related liabilities on time.

Reference: PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 General debt collection powers and principles.
