Deferral Suspension Instruction
Deferral Suspension Instruction

How to action requests for deferral of the due date for lodgment or suspension of lodgment enforcement action.

Last published 10 December 2025

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page instructs staff actioning non-lodgment work on how to action requests for deferral of the due date for lodgment (deferral) or suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension).

This procedure is to be used when directed from:

Non-lodgment case process
Opens in a new window
Non-lodgment actions overview
Opens in a new window
Call from an external collection agency.
Opens in a new window

When actioning agent deferral requests received via Online services for agents (OSfA), refer to Lodgment program deferral instruction - OSfA function.
Opens in a new window

For the purpose of this instruction, the term:

agent refers to a registered tax agent or registered BAS agent

Activity Statements (AS) can be read as any combination of the following

quarterly and monthly AS

annual GST returns where taxpayer is eligible and chooses to pay GST annually (GSTA)

annual GST returns where taxpayer is eligible and chooses to pay GST by instalments (GSTR)

pay as you go (PAYG) withholding annual reports

miscellaneous obligations refers to any obligation that cannot have a deferral/suspension keyed in ICP or the Deferral/Suspension Input module (DSIM). For example

quarterly TFN reports

superannuation annual withholding report

PSAR refers to PAYG payment summary annual report

WM refers to Siebel Work Management.

Recent updates
10/12/2025	

Update to the 'suspension' option in the table under 'ICP deferral/suspension'.

	SD-99949
Procedure Steps
Introduction

Deferrals are actioned in ICP for all obligations except for:

PSAR, which are actioned in the DSIM

miscellaneous obligations, for which there is no system functionality to process a deferral.

With the exception of staff actioning Lodgment program deferral WM activities, do not action requests for the deferral of a payment due date.

Requests for payment deferrals should be redirected, if received:

in writing, refer to Referral instruction
Opens in a new window

by phone, by advising the taxpayer to contact 13 11 42.

For more information on deferrals and suspensions, refer to Deferral suspension guideline.
Opens in a new window

Ensure PORO is conducted for all requests and all actions are recorded in line with the relevant procedures.

Actioning a deferral or suspension request

To action a deferral or suspension request the actions in the following table must be completed where appropriate.

If you are actioning a deferral approval request refer to Deferral approval below.

reason for deferring lodgment due date selected is Tax Agent Initiated. This will prevent ICP from generating an auto-letter.

A maximum period of two years applies to suspensions input by Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist staff actioning non-lodgment work.

Deferral Suspension guideline
Deferral Suspension guideline

Information on due date deferral for lodgment and payments or suspension of enforcement action of an approved form.

Last published 3 March 2026

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page provides Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist staff with information about:

lodgment due date deferrals and associated payments (deferral), and
suspension of lodgment enforcement action (suspension) on an approved form or other prescribed form.
Warning

Do not use this page for payment only deferrals.

For the purpose of this guideline, the term:

agent refers to a registered tax agent and registered BAS agent

due date refers to either the end date assigned under the agent lodgment program or the due date for lodgment

Online services refers to

Online services for agents (OSfA)

Online services for business.

Recent updates

03/03/2026

	

Minor update to 'Circumstances beyond or not beyond taxpayer control'.

	

SD-102281

In Detail
What is a deferral or suspension

A deferral or suspension may be granted when a taxpayer needs additional time to:

lodge an approved form
Opens in a new window
 or other prescribed form, or
to pay the associated debts.

Generally, a deferral request should be made on or before the due date. A suspension is usually considered when the reasons given for additional time are not sufficient to allow a deferral.

Requests for additional time for lodgment and payment must be considered independently of each other. Staff actioning Lodgment program OSfA deferral instruction
Opens in a new window
 requests from agents may action payment deferrals when this forms part of the request.

Characteristics
Deferral	

The legislative basis for the Commissioner to defer the time for lodgment of approved forms is under subsection 388-55(1) of Schedule 1
Opens in a new window
 of the Taxation Administration Act 1953 (TAA).

The requirements of approved forms are set out under section 388-50 of Schedule 1
Opens in a new window
 to the TAA.

Commissioner's discretion to grant a deferral of time to lodge other prescribed forms is in the various provisions requiring the lodgment of prescribed forms.

The lodgment due date is moved to a future date (deferred due date).

Failure to lodge on time (FTL) penalties will not be applied if lodgment is met on or before the deferred due date. Where FTL penalty has been applied before a deferral is processed, the FTL penalty will need to be cancelled.

Lodgment deferral may not defer the associated payment due date. General interest charge (GIC) will still apply from the original due date unless a payment deferral is automatically or separately granted - see Payment obligations and the effect of lodgment deferrals.

For:

obligations in ICP, input to the taxpayer record by modifying the expected lodgment date

activity statements, a re-forecasted lodgment due date
Opens in a new window
 will not override a deferral that has already been input for a later due date.




Suspension

	

The ATO agrees not to take further compliance action on the overdue lodgment obligation until an agreed future date.

The lodgment due date of the obligation does not change.

FTL penalties and GIC continue to apply from the lodgment due date.

Generally considered when the reasons given are not sufficient to allow a deferral.

For obligations in ICP, input to the taxpayer record using assessed low risk (ALR) accounting treatments.

Lodgment program deferrals (agent deferrals)

Taxpayers that have a registered agent authorised to act on their behalf for their lodgment obligations may be included in their agent's Lodgment program. The Lodgment program is a concession for agents to have further time to lodge beyond the statutory due date. Refer to the current lodgment program
Opens in a new window
 to determine the applicable due date for the relevant form and entity type.

Agents can also request additional time beyond the Lodgment program concession due dates when circumstances for their taxpayer or themselves has impacted their ability to lodge or lodge and pay by the due date.

If an agent needs to request a deferral for a taxpayer, the taxpayer must appear on the agent's current taxpayer listing for the roles specified.

Deferral requests lodged in accordance with Lodgment program deferrals
Opens in a new window
 are generally considered low risk. The ATO has established a principle that Lodgment program deferral requests will be granted unless the request is considered high risk, based on a risk assessment.

Agents need to submit their deferral request using OSfA. Go to:

Lodgment program deferrals
Opens in a new window
Payment-only deferral requests for registered agents.
Opens in a new window
Considering a deferral or suspension request

Deferral/suspension requests should include the:

type of document and the year/period it relates to

taxpayer's details, including ABN or TFN

circumstances that prevent the taxpayer from lodging the approved form or other prescribed form by the due date

steps the taxpayer has taken to mitigate those circumstances

proposed date of 

lodgment 
payment.

Each request for a deferral or suspension should be considered on its own merits, taking into account:

whether there is other relevant ATO activity
Opens in a new window

the circumstances preventing the taxpayer from lodging and paying

the taxpayer's compliance history as a whole, that is, compliance with both lodgment and payment obligations

the length of additional time requested

the risk to revenue

the value of the information contained in the document

steps taken to mitigate the circumstances, e.g. to lessen the severity of the circumstances or enable future tax obligations to be met as they fall due

the size and structure of the entity, e.g. large businesses may be more likely to be able to overcome circumstances affecting their ability to lodge

the principles in relevant practice statements and other administrative guidelines that have been issued, for example practice notes

for suspensions only

the stage lodgment enforcement action has reached

the likelihood of the taxpayer lodging within the period

the taxpayer's contact with the ATO to date

any other relevant information.

None of these factors should be considered in isolation. The decision to approve a deferral or suspension request should be based on all these factors together, as a whole. Use all the information provided to make your decision.

Specific deferral exclusions have been identified by business. These are listed under Exclusion checks on Deferrals and Suspensions.
Opens in a new window
 These exclusions apply when there are no other reasons or factors to support the deferral request.

The final decision must be fair and reasonable.

Requests should generally not be considered when a final notice has issued. This is because potential prosecution action may be compromised. Go to Final notice issued or case referred for prosecution action for further information.

Your decision must be supported by the relevant practice statements and other administrative guidelines issued. Principles and guidelines to be factored into your decisions include the following:

PS LA 2011/15
Opens in a new window
 - Lodgment obligations, due dates and deferrals

PS LA 2011/14
Opens in a new window
 - General debt collection powers and principles

relevant Frontline Operations Practice Note.
Opens in a new window

Go to Deferral requests in specific circumstances for further consideration when the deferral request is for:

a trust beneficiary statement
inability to pay a liability that may arise from lodgment
defence force personnel and war
delay obtaining information from a third party
a taxpayer experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability.
Factors affecting agents

An important consideration when assessing the reasons for deferral is whether the agent circumstances were unforeseeable and unavoidable.

Each request should be considered on its merits having regard to some or all of the following factors:

past lodgment performance

agent management of lodgment obligations

reporting period

approved form or other prescribed form type

number of deferrals

size of the practice

deferred due date

value of the information required

risk to the revenue.

Circumstances beyond or not beyond taxpayer control

A lodgment deferral will generally be granted when a taxpayer can demonstrate the inability to lodge by the due date is reasonably attributed to circumstances beyond their control (unforeseeable and unavoidable).

You must also consider deferral requests for taxpayers with a poor compliance history:

when the inability to lodge was caused by circumstances beyond their control, or

if it would otherwise be fair and reasonable to grant the deferral.

When the reasons may not be considered exceptional or unforeseen, a deferral can still be considered when all factors, as a whole, are considered. For example:

in conjunction with the taxpayer's good compliance history and the length of time requested

we would grant a reasonable deferral when a taxpayer advises they cannot lodge by the due date because of a planned trip overseas and the

ATO has not been able to generate an activity statement before the taxpayer's departure from Australia

taxpayer has genuine grounds for not having other arrangements in place to fulfil the obligation.

Circumstances	Taxpayer examples	Agent examples


Unforeseeable and unavoidable.

	
Natural and other disasters.
Serious illness of the taxpayer or family member.
Advanced age or youth of the taxpayer.
A problem due to language difficulties
The taxpayer is experiencing vulnerability, such as:

homelessness
family violence
financial coercion.
	

Natural and other disasters.

The serious illness of a sole practitioner or agent in a small firm.

Prolonged but unexpected staff absences.

Prolonged and expected staff absences but where other factors have prevented replacement by suitably qualified staff.




Foreseeable but unavoidable
i.e. may not be able to overcome the effects in the short term.

	

Impeded access to records.

	

Records seized during a police search or retained as evidence in a court matter.

Delays due to external auditor availability.

Despite making an active effort to achieve lodgment for a period, a small number of forms will not be lodged by the due date.




Foreseeable and avoidable.

	

The taxpayer forgot.

Anticipated absences from home or place of business or being overseas.

Installation of a new computer system.

Holiday shutdown.

Renovations to premises.

	

Taking of annual leave.

Effect of public holidays.

Final notice issued or case referred for prosecution action

Granting a deferral or suspension will compromise potential prosecution action. Due to this risk, requests will generally not be considered where either:

a final notice has been issued, or

the case has been referred for prosecution action.

Requests for a deferral or suspension should be referred to the Prosecution case owner to action when:

the request is after a case has been allocated to a prosecutor, or

a summons/court attendance notice/complaint has issued. 

If you consider a...	Then...
deferral should be granted after a final notice has issued	

the final notice will be either invalidated or unable to be used for prosecution

the final notice must be re-issued after the deferred due date has passed before prosecution action can proceed

consult your support team or team leader to check the decision is made by an officer with the appropriate authorisation for the deferral.


suspension should be granted after a final notice has issued	

it would be practical to withdraw the notice
Opens in a new window

an option would be to reissue a final notice with an agreed date to comply rather than suspend lodgment enforcement action.

Deferral requests in specific circumstances
Deferral request is for...	Then...
a trust beneficiary statement	

some closely held trusts are required to report on beneficiaries that are 'presently entitled' to one or both of the following:

a share of the trust income

a 'tax-preferred amount' of the trust.

These trusts are required to complete a trust beneficiary statement as part of the trust income tax return.

Deferral or suspension submitted for a trust beneficiary statement should be interpreted as a request for deferral or suspension of the trust's income tax return.

In Frontline Operations, trust beneficiary statement deferral requests from:

an agent are actioned by the lodgment program deferral teams when the trust's income tax return has not been lodged

a taxpayer are actioned by teams actioning debt general activities.


inability to pay the liability that may arise from lodgment	

encourage the taxpayer to:

lodge on time so they will not incur FTL penalty

negotiate payment options - go to Payment plan or payment in full.
Opens in a new window

Consider all factors in determining if it would be fair and reasonable to grant a lodgment deferral.


defence force personnel and war	

refer to Defence Force Personnel and War
Opens in a new window
 for additional information and factors to consider for deferral requests.


a delay in obtaining information from a third party	

for:

Partnerships and Trusts returns,

deferral request are generally not granted

where information to complete the return is held by a third party (in particular, a third party agent), the tax agent or taxpayer must have taken reasonable steps to get the information before the due date. They must demonstrate this for a deferral to be considered

Individual and Trust taxpayers may be subject to special considerations. Speak to your support staff or team leader to determine the treatment for this particular time

Research & Development (R&D) grant applications,

a deferral should be granted for 6 weeks from the date the application was lodged with AUSIndustry or the ATO (whatever is applicable)

where an agent has only recently taken a new taxpayer with R&D claims, additional considerations should be given. This is because the amount of time required to review the business and identify any R&D claims. Established companies will be more complex than a single purpose start-up company. 

 




a taxpayer experiencing severe and immediate vulnerability

	

if you have identified a taxpayer is experiencing immediate and severe vulnerability factors that significantly impacts their capacity to engage, lodge or pay (for example, domestic and family violence, coercion, threats of self-harm) and as per Tailored support for taxpayers experiencing vulnerability.
Opens in a new window

For:

Telephony, warm transfer to Specialised Client Support on x92196

Correspondence,

escalate the correspondence to Debt hardship

update the activity

Capability: Debt and Lodgment

Category: Debt - Specific

Type: Debt Hardship

Interaction: do not update

Status: UnAllocated

Description: Hardship

Notes: as appropriate

Assigned To: ENTERPRISE_Q.

Releasing held activity statements

An activity statement that has been generated can be held and not automatically released if there are problems with the taxpayer's record or ATO systems, for example a returned unclaimed indicator on file.

A deferral may be granted for lodgment and payment when a held activity statement is released because of the delay in issuing the activity statement. This applies to lodgment and payment requirements for any activity statement, whether monthly, quarterly, or annual.

The deferral must be input before re-generating the activity statement to allow the deferred due date to be printed on the activity statement.

The following table outlines deferrals that can be granted to different taxpayer types. If a taxpayer requests additional time beyond the timeframe in the table, they need to demonstrate that it is fair and reasonable to be given additional time.

Scenario	Action


All taxpayers with a PAYG instalment role using the ATO instalment amount

	A deferral of 26 days for lodgment and payment is to be granted from the date of release of the activity statement.


Large/medium taxpayers with either:

annual total income in excess of $10 million

annual GST turnover in excess of $20 million

annual withholding payments in excess of $1 million, or

an entity in a group of companies where at least one member of that group has an annual total income in excess of $10 million

	

For:

payments, unless the taxpayer has a PAYG instalment role, and is using the ATO instalment amount, payment is expected to have been made on time

lodgments, a deferral of 9 days to lodge the activity statement should be granted from the date of release.




All other non-large taxpayers, who do not fit the definition of large

	

A deferral of 26 days to lodge and pay the activity statement should be granted from the date of release of the activity statement.


Deferred GST (DGST) taxpayers	

The activity statement should be referred to DGST for action, go to Referral instruction.
Opens in a new window

Deferrals and regenerating activity statements

Before terminating and regenerating an activity statement, check for a granted deferral.

You may need to re-input the deferral when the activity statement is regenerated.

Payment obligations and the effect of lodgment deferrals

The taxable payments annual report is not eligible for payment deferrals. 

Legislation allows the Commissioner to defer the time for lodgment of any approved form or other prescribed form. Separate legislation allows deferral of time for payment of any amounts due. The relevant provision is section 255-10
Opens in a new window
 of Schedule 1 to the TAA.

The Lodgment Program details the specific lodgment and payment deferrals the Commissioner has granted to certain classes of taxpayers where the return is lodged by a registered agent.

Granting a lodgment deferral will defer the payment due date for individual or trust income tax returns. Under a long standing administrative practice the payment is generally due 21 days after either the deferred lodgment date or the issue of the notice of assessment, whichever is the later.

For further information on lodgment and payment due dates, refer to Lodgment and payment due dates.
Opens in a new window

Authorisation for deferrals/suspensions
For...	Points to note...
Deferral	

Tax legislation gives the Commissioner the discretion to defer the time within which an approved form or other prescribed form is required to be given. The Commissioner has delegated this power to the Deputy Commissioners who in turn authorise certain officers to grant deferrals.

You need to have the appropriate authorisation in order to grant additional time to lodge an approved form or other prescribed forms.

The authorisation for a deferral of time to lodge is calculated from the day after the date that the document was originally due to be lodged. However, for activity statements and payment summary annual reports (PSAR), the authorisation is calculated from the later of the original due date or the deferred due date granted to a class or classes of taxpayers.

For further information on authorisation on deferral of time to lodge refer to Authorisations and delegations guideline.
Opens in a new window

Where you do not have the authority to grant the deferral, you are still able to make recommendations and seek approval from an officer who has the appropriate authorisation.

Any request for a deferral that is beyond the authority of your support staff or team leader must be referred to a more senior officer for approval. The request must be referred via your support staff or team leader and should only be escalated if supported.

No specific authorisation level is required to deny a deferral request. Business procedures will specify if an administrative approval is required.


Suspension	

There are no specific authorisation guidelines for approval of a suspension. However, generally a suspension should not be granted for a period greater than four weeks.

If it appears the taxpayer's circumstances support a suspension beyond four weeks, the case should be discussed with your support staff or team leader and appropriate narrations recorded.

Each case should be based on its merits and the factors listed above taken into consideration for a period of suspension to be granted.

The maximum period an ALR accounting treatment (suspension) can be applied for is 2 years. This is a business decision applying to Frontline Compliance and Debt Assist teams actioning non-lodgment work.

Communicating and recording your decision
When...	Then...


communicating your decision

	

the type of request and your decision determines how you communicate the decision to the taxpayer. Generally, you will advise the taxpayer of your decision where a deferral or suspension is:

allowed, by phone, secure message reply or letter

disallowed, by letter (letter can be attached to a secure message reply)

refer to your business procedures for the requirements to be fulfilled and details of what the taxpayer must be advised

a manual deferral letter may be required depending on the type and circumstance of the request. For example, the:

request is for multiple taxpayers

letter is to be sent to an address not on ATO systems

taxpayer requested written confirmation as a result of the phone contact

where the request was lodged via Online services, the deferral decision may be communicated by a secure message.
Opens in a new window
 The letter can be attached to the unstructured Siebel secure message

where a deferral or suspension request is disallowed or varied, you must document the factors considered in making the decision and communicate those reasons to the taxpayer

when communicating your decision the taxpayer must be advised of their review rights when a deferral or suspension is disallowed or varied.


recording your decision	

you must document factors considered when making your decision and the reasons. You must follow quality narrations and decisions
Opens in a new window
 when recording your note. This is because:

it is consistent with legislation, procedures and expectations of ATO officers, who are accountable for their actions and decisions

external organisations such as the Tax Ombudsman, the courts and the Administrative Review Tribunal may review ATO records

taxpayers may also obtain copies of ATO records via the Freedom of Information Act 1982

taxpayers may seek statements of the reasons for decisions as part of Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977 (ADJR) processes

the taxpayer record should be updated in line with, for

lodgment program deferrals, Lodgment program OSfA deferral instruction
Opens in a new window

other requests, Deferrals and suspensions.
Opens in a new window

Review rights
Decision type	Review rights
Deferral	

The ATO's preferred method for reviewing adverse decisions on lodgment deferral requests is for the taxpayer to:

discuss the decision with the original decision maker

discuss the decision with a more a senior officer, if still dissatisfied.

If the taxpayer is still dissatisfied with the decision, they should be provided with the information on contacting ATO complaints.
Opens in a new window

The informal approach is the preferred method for taxpayers seeking a review but cannot be enforced. You are expected to advise taxpayers of the relevant avenues for review. Taxpayers also have formal external review rights available to them. Preferably they would only take this action after seeking an internal review.

Where a right of review is provided for in legislation, it is considered a formal review right. Such rights provide for ATO decisions to be reviewed independently or externally to the ATO.

Taxpayers cannot object to a deferral decision. However, a deferral decision is reviewable by the Federal Court
Opens in a new window
 or Federal Circuit Court under the Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977.

The grounds for review of a decision include a breach of the rules of natural justice, an improper exercise of power or an error of law.

While it is not common for a taxpayer to seek a judicial review, the existence of this formal review right means that all adverse decisions must be capable of being defended in the Federal Court.

Taxpayers may also lodge a complaint with the Tax Ombudsman. Generally, it is expected taxpayers will seek to resolve the issues directly with the ATO before approaching the Tax Ombudsman.


Suspension	

Refusing a suspension is not a reviewable decision under the Administrative Decisions (Judicial Review) Act 1977 (ADJR Act).

Taxpayers are not able to object to a suspension decision.

During a phone conversation, if the taxpayer expresses dissatisfaction with the decision to deny a suspension of lodgment action, advise them:

of their alternative avenues of redress (as per the 'Keep you informed' section of the ATO Charter).
Opens in a new window
 These avenues of redress may include

discussing the decision with a more senior officer
contacting ATO Complaints
Opens in a new window
lodging a complaint with the Tax Ombudsman. Generally, it is expected taxpayers will seek to resolve the issues directly with the ATO before approaching the Tax Ombudsman
they can issue a letter containing the decision and their review rights.

ICP-Grant a deferral

How to grant a deferral in ICP.

Last published 11 October 2024

Favourite Notes Collapse all 
Background

This page contains the procedure for granting a deferral in ICP.

Excluding Payment-only deferrals for Large SGE taxpayers, all other tax practitioner deferral requests must be submitted via Online services for agents (OSfA).

Agent Deferral skilled staff (excluding FC Firmer and Legal Recovery) need to refer to Registered agent requests
Opens in a new window
 if the agent deferral request is for:

lodgment only
lodgment and payment, or
review of previous decision (excluding payment-only deferral).
Recent updates
11/10/2024	Update made to step 4. 	SD-86430 


24/09/2024

	Update to Background, step 2, step 7 and step 9.	

SD-85765

Procedure Steps
Grant deferral

Complete the below steps to key in the deferral for the taxpayer.

Step	Action

1	

Navigate to Client home in ICP and select the relevant role.


2	

Select Client calendar. 

If the lodgment you are deferring is in the Expected lodgments tab make a note of the period start date and end date for the period to be updated.

Activity statements:

if you are granting a deferral for the Activity Statement period 01/01/2024 - 31/03/2024, look for 01/03/2024 - 31/03/2024 

activity statements will not be in the Expected lodgments tab if the due date falls 3 or more months in the future. ICP will need to generate the lodgment before a deferral can be submitted 

Tax returns: 

identify if any of the following have due dates linked to the due date of the tax return

returns

statements, or notices

the due date of these must be changed to the deferred due date (for example, an annual GST return).


3	

Select:

the link in Form type expected for the required role or account

Defer lodgment and/or payment due date.


4	

Update the required details to grant a deferral:

Received date - the date the deferral request was received in the ATO

Input reason from the drop-down box (do not use the non-pursuit status reason).

If the taxpayer has a tax agent concessional due date and advises they now wish to self prepare and require a deferral, select Commissioner Discretion from the Input reason field.

Warning

Do not change any fields in the lodgment details tab, this may cause processing on the incorrect taxpayer role. If a suspense error occurs, you must select the role that matches the expected lodgment.


5	

Select:

Due date to be deferred tab

the appropriate type of due date to be deferred from the drop-down menu

Information alert:
Select Both if the deferral is for a period that was not in the original period list and was only added by following the steps for Add a period.

Next.


6	

Determine if you are entering a deferral for a payment due date only. If:

yes, go to step 9
no, 

lodgment due date only, or
both lodgment and payment due dates
go to step 7.
Information alert:
If the deferral is for a period that was not in the original period list and was only added by following the steps for Add a period, go to step 7.

7	

Select Defer lodgment due date and complete:

New lodgment due date: ensure it

does not fall on a weekend or public holiday, and
when this applies, is entered as the next working business day

Lodgment due date type

Concessional manual - select if deferral was granted due to a Commissioner's concession

Deferred - select if granting a deferral request

Reason for deferring lodgment due date: select Tax Agent Initiated. This will prevent ICP from generating an auto-letter

If the taxpayer has a tax agent concessional due date and advises they now wish to self prepare and require a deferral, select Demanded for the lodgment due date type.


8	

If:

you are granting a lodgment deferral only, or a lodgment and payment deferral and the taxpayer is not being granted more than the automatic 21 days to pay

select Form Confirmation

go to step 10

the payment due date needs to be deferred, go to step 9.


9	

Select Defer payment due date and complete the following:

New payment due date

Payment due date type

Concessional manual - select if deferral was granted due to a Commissioner's concession

Deferred - select if granting a deferral request

Reason for deferring payment due date: select Tax Agent Initiated. This will prevent ICP from generating an auto-letter

If the taxpayer has a tax agent concessional due date and advises they now wish to self prepare and require a deferral, select Demanded for the payment due date type.

Select Form Confirmation.


10	

Determine if an ICP suspense item has been created. If:

yes, go to ICP-Correct suspended form
Opens in a new window

If the form suspends with the exception 831, seek assistance from your coach if you are unsure of how to action the suspense reason.

Do not select the Form save & suspend button as this will create a new activity. This activity will then be routed to another operative who will not have appropriate information to correct the error.

no, select Submit.
